# Fuel gap registry

**Every deferred, incomplete, or declined path in Fuel lives here — one greppable list, not scattered across 40 files.** This exists because the JIT launcher's scalar-only limitation was acknowledged in a code comment ("documented loader follow-up") but never on any schedule, so it rotted for months and shipped an all-zero output. A gap acknowledged in prose but not tracked is a leak.

## Rules

1. **Every** `todo!()` / `unimplemented!()` / `panic!(` on a non-test path, and every prose hedge, carries a `// GAP(GAP-NNN)` reference to a row below. **Enforced prose-hedge patterns (GAP-141 inc 2)**, in comments on production paths: `for now`, `not yet`, `doesn't yet`, `shortcut`, `bails`, `would need`, `stopgap`, `workaround`, `naive`, `temporary` (case-insensitive) + `TODO`, `FIXME`, `XXX`, `HACK` (uppercase markers) — **14 patterns**. A new hit fails CI unless GAP-referenced, reworded, or deliberately added to `fuel-ir/tests/gap_hedge_allowlist.txt` (which **may only shrink** — stale entries also fail, so removing a hedge forces removing its allowlist line). `todo!`/`unimplemented!` are enforced by inc 1 (`gap_refs.rs`); **`panic!(` is measured, not yet enforced — see GAP-016.** Baseline at adoption: 648 files, 221 hedges, 216-key allowlist, 0 new, 0 stale.
2. Adding such a path **without** a `GAP(...)` ref is a defect. **Enforcement is incremental and the gap between rule and gate is stated deliberately, because an unenforced rule 1 would be exactly the defect this registry exists to prevent** — see GAP-141. **Increment 1 (ratified 2026-08-07):** `todo!()` / `unimplemented!()` only, 36 sites. **Increment 2 (SCHEDULED, not deferred):** prose hedges via a shrinking allowlist — this is the increment that addresses the founding case, see GAP-141. **`panic!(` is measured but unenforced** pending a gap-vs-invariant policy — see GAP-016.
3. Close a gap → strike its row (`~~GAP-NNN~~ CLOSED <commit>`) and remove the `GAP(...)` ref from the code in the same change.
4b. ⚠️ **THE TIER COLUMN IS HEADED `Tier` — AND THIS FILE HAS FOUR TABLE SCHEMAS, SO A RULE PHRASED OVER “THE THIRD COLUMN” IS FALSE FOR ONE OF THEM. CORRECTED THREE TIMES, EACH CORRECTION BETTER-EVIDENCED AND STILL SCOPED TOO WIDELY.** **MEASURED across all 13 table headers 2026-08-26: TWELVE carry the tier at column 3** (11 x `ID | File:Line | Tier | Gap`, 1 x `ID | File / area | Tier | Gap | Status`, 1 x `ID | File:Line | Tier | Gap | Status`), **and the `## Meta` table is `ID | Area | Gap | Status` — four columns, no `File:Line`, and its column 2 holds PROSE AREA DESCRIPTIONS in 86 of 90 rows** (*“Process — how anyone verifies a claim about what a commit CONTAINS”*), with 4 rows putting a tier token there instead. **So Meta's column 2 is an AREA field used inconsistently within itself, and renaming it `Tier` would have been the third wrong answer.** ⚠️ **THE ERROR CHAIN IS THE LESSON, BECAUSE EACH STEP WAS AN IMPROVEMENT THAT STILL OVER-CLAIMED: (i)** read the column as SEVERITY and produced a false count; **(ii)** corrected it to OWNER **from the HEADER, without measuring the rows** — wrong, and the reading it overturned was ~97% right about practice; **(iii)** corrected it to TIER **from measuring ONE table's rows and renaming ONE header**, stated file-wide — which left the file **INCONSISTENTLY wrong where it had been UNIFORMLY wrong.** **THAT IS STRICTLY WORSE AND IT IS THE PART TO REMEMBER: before, any header sampled gave the same wrong answer and the contradiction was systematic enough to notice; after, a reader hitting line 24 concludes the file is correct, a reader hitting any of the other twelve gets the old answer, AND NEITHER CAN TELL THEY SAMPLED. A PARTIAL RENAME OF A REPEATED HEADER CONVERTS A DETECTABLE INCONSISTENCY INTO AN UNDETECTABLE ONE.** **Caught by a lane who then found their OWN verification had the same defect** — they ran `sed -n '24p'`, saw `Tier`, and reported it verified: **one artifact checked, a property ranging over thirteen.** **PRACTICE: a claim about “the Nth column” must name WHICH SCHEMA; enumerate every header before renaming any; and when correcting a repeated element, the unit of work is the POPULATION, never the instance you were shown.** This bullet used to read *"THE THIRD COLUMN IS OWNER, NOT SEVERITY"*. **MEASURED across the live table: C 65 · A 58 · B 57 · — 31 · P 9 — i.e. 220 of ~227 rows hold a TIER token, and the only two holding a peer ID were the two I filed the night I wrote the rule.** The header said `Owner`; the file has never once behaved that way. ⚠️ **THE MECHANISM, WHICH IS THE PART TO KEEP: I DERIVED THE RULE FROM THE HEADER AND NEVER MEASURED THE ROWS.** A two-artifact invariant checked against one artifact — the same defect as eight method rules being present in one file and unreachable from the other, where **neither artifact is wrong and the error lives only in the relation between them.** ⚠️ **AND IT IS A CORRECTION THAT OVERSHOT: the reading I "fixed" was ~97% RIGHT about practice.** The original survey's real defect was its NUMBER — *"42 open severity-A rows"* against a true **17 live Tier-A** — produced by counting rows the tier token does not describe and not restricting to live ones. **I corrected the MEANING (wrongly) when the defect was in the COUNT.** *A correction is a claim and needs the same verification as the thing it corrects; this one inherited the credibility of having caught a real error.* ✅ **RESOLVED, not merely documented: the header now reads `Tier`, matching 220 rows, and OWNERSHIP LIVES IN THE STATUS CELL** (*"OPEN — unallocated"*, *"allocated to X"*, *"measured by X; the follow-on is NOT theirs by default"*), which is where every row that records an owner already put it. **The two rows that carried a peer ID in column 3 were normalised.** ✅ **AND THE REDUNDANCY IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN WASTEFUL: the tier is ALSO encoded by the section a row sits in, so the column is a CROSS-CHECK — a row reading `A` inside Tier C is a detectable inconsistency, where a single source of truth would simply be silently wrong.** **(ii) STILL TRUE AND UNCHANGED:** every row filed 2026-08-20 by appending after the last table row — **GAP-214 (undefined behaviour), GAP-222, GAP-223 (the GPU mutex), GAP-224** — landed in **Tier P**, i.e. filed as *performance work with no correctness stake*, **in a registry whose section order IS the completion order.** Moved to Tier A on discovery. **PRACTICE: file a new row INTO its tier section by name, never by appending; put the tier token in column 3 and the OWNER in the status cell; and read a table column by measuring what its rows actually hold, not by trusting its header OR its position — both have now been wrong here, in opposite directions, within a week.**
4. Owners: **A** = launcher / KISS-alignment / FKC-dispatch; **B** = cuda-backend / concurrency / gate; **C** = decode / models / fuel-core; **—** = pooled / unassigned. (Owners are roles, not sessions; claim by editing the row.)

## Tiers (completion order)

- **A — Correctness:** panics on production paths, silently-wrong results, missing gradients, races. Do these first; several violate the "never panic on production paths" hard rule.
- **B — Roadmap commitments / frontier:** architectural work other things depend on.
- **C — Feature completeness:** missing capability that currently **declines cleanly** (typed error / CPU fallback) — honest, lower-stakes.
- **P — Performance:** optimization only; no correctness stake.

| GAP-214 | `fuel-dispatch/src/jit_cuda_load.rs:148` slot table (`claim_slot` + `guard[idx] = Some(Slot{..})`) | A | **A JIT SLOT'S `CudaFunc` OUTLIVES ITS `CudaDevice`, PERMANENTLY — UNDEFINED BEHAVIOUR WITH A CORRECT-LOOKING ANSWER.** Confirmed by exhaustion, not impression: the only write to the slot table is `guard[idx] = Some(Slot { func, entry_point })`; there is **no `= None`, no `.take()`, and no reset/clear/release function anywhere in the file**. `claim_slot` finds the first `None`, so a slot is claimed once and never returned. **Consequence: a `CudaFunc` bound to a dropped `CudaDevice` stays selectable in a process-global table for the lifetime of the process.** This is the UB link in GAP-001's chain and the one that is NOT a test artifact — GAP-001's ~50% intermittency is exactly "launching from a dropped context is sometimes fine and sometimes silently writes nothing". ⚠️ **THIS IS THE MULTI-SESSION SERVING HAZARD, and it is the normal case there rather than an accident:** sessions adopt kernels for the same recipe (which legitimately share one `FusedOpId` — recipe-identity dedup is BY DESIGN, see `fuel-graph/src/runtime_fused.rs:159`), selection returns the first-registered (GAP-213), and once that session's device is gone the launch is undefined — **correct-looking whenever the two kernels compute the same function.** ✅ **OWNERSHIP RULED 2026-08-20 (architect). A JIT SLOT IS A **DEVICE** RESOURCE, NOT A PROCESS RESOURCE — and modelling it as a process resource is the category error that produces the UB.** A `CudaFunc` is meaningful only inside the CUDA context that loaded it; a process-global `OnceLock<RwLock<Vec<Option<Slot>>>>` (`:88-90`) with **no `= None`, no `.take()`, and no release function anywhere in the file** says the opposite. **The ruling does NOT prescribe moving the table**, because the design's `DISPATCH_TABLE` (`:312`, 64 monomorphized `KernelRef`s indexed by a const `N`) depends on a global index, and restructuring that is a far larger change than the defect warrants. **Implementable form, THREE increments, and the order is load-bearing:** **(1) `Slot` RECORDS THE IDENTITY OF THE DEVICE THAT LOADED IT.** Without this nothing downstream is expressible. **(2) THE DISPATCHER REFUSES INSTEAD OF LAUNCHING when the recorded device is no longer the live device for that ordinal — a typed `Err`, never a panic and never a launch.** `KernelRef` already returns `Result`, so this needs no signature change. **THIS INCREMENT IS THE WHOLE FIX FOR THE UB; everything else is capacity.** **(3) DEVICE TEARDOWN RELEASES ITS SLOTS** (`guard[i] = None` for matching slots), which restores reclamation and makes the 64-slot bank an actual bank rather than a one-way allocation. ⚠️ **(2) MUST LAND BEFORE (3), and the reason is the standing keep-vs-revert rule: (2) removes an undefined behaviour, (3) removes an exhaustion limit. Shipping (3) first would reclaim slots while launches are still unchecked — turning a stale-but-quiescent slot into a REUSED one, which is strictly worse: the same UB with a fresh occupant, and a correct-looking answer becomes a wrong one.** **REJECTED, and recorded so it is not re-proposed: making `Slot` hold an `Arc<CudaDevice>` so the func cannot outlive its device.** It is the smallest change and it does make the invariant true by construction — **and it converts undefined behaviour into a DEVICE leak, which for the multi-session serving goal is the wrong trade**: 64 slots pinning session devices means GPU memory that is never freed, and a leak that only manifests under the workload we are building toward is worse than one that manifests today. **ALLOCATION NOTE (cost, not preference): this is `--features cuda` code, so gating it costs a baracuda forge — ~30-56 min COLD, and worktrees share NO forge cache. Give it to whichever lane already has a warm forge in its worktree**, rather than paying the cold cost again. Needs an ownership decision on slot lifetime; it cannot be fixed locally in `adopt_*`. | OPEN — architect-owned scoping; blocks nothing today but is the production form of GAP-001  ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-21 — ALL THREE INCREMENTS ON MAIN. inc 1+2 `285b1279` (the UB fix: per-operand `DeviceId` refusal), inc 3 `68a3d26f` (the capacity half: lazy slot reclamation on device teardown).** **Verified by content, not by report:** liveness registry live in `fuel-cuda-backend/src/device.rs`, reclamation wired into `claim_slot`. **Both increments red-then-green LIVE through `gpu-run`**, inc 3's pair re-run on the rebased tree because its inputs moved. ✅ **THE SABOTAGE DISCRIMINATED AT THE NEGATIVE CONTROL, NOT THE OBVIOUS ARM:** with liveness ignored, the dead-slot arm still reclaimed correctly — **only arm (b), *a LIVE device's slot must NOT be reclaimed*, failed (`freed=[0]`).** And because the assertion names WHICH slot rather than how many, **a bare count of 1 would have read as arm (a) succeeding** — the correct-total-hides-a-wrong-distribution rule catching a real ambiguity in a different lane's test, hours after it was written down. ✅ **TWO PLACES THIS COULD HAVE GONE SUBTLY WRONG, BOTH CLOSED BY READING RATHER THAN ASSUMING: (i) reclaim drops the slot's `CudaFunc`, which holds an `Arc<Stream>` — safe because baracuda's `StreamInner` holds its owning `context`, so the order is stream→context; (ii) the liveness signal is keyed on the monotonic `DeviceId`, NEVER the ordinal — an ordinal-keyed signal would free slots whose device is still alive, which is the same-ordinal-reuse trap inc 2's born-red exists to catch.** **SIX INVARIANTS are in the module doc as what must STAY true, and the sixth is the strongest: reclamation is IMPOSSIBLE while any storage from that device is live — storage holds a device clone and liveness is clone-keyed — so the dead-operand hazard is structurally unreachable rather than merely refused.** **Clone-keying therefore does two jobs: it avoids the rejected `Arc<CudaDevice>` device-leak trade AND makes the dangerous case impossible; the second is the stronger.** **The struct-field-add gate was discharged by PRIVACY rather than enumeration — see CLAUDE.md.** |

| GAP-224 | `scripts/gpu-run.ps1:222` exports `GPU_RUN_HELD=1`; **zero Rust code observes it** (`git grep 'GPU_RUN_HELD' -- '*.rs'` = 0) | A | **THE GPU MUTEX'S ABSENCE IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ITS SUCCESS — the run completes, the tests pass, and the only difference is a lock nobody observes.** Framing and evidence from **Vulkane (peer `bywamw5l`), who reported it unprompted against themselves**: on 2026-08-20 they ran `cargo test --workspace --features …,kiss-target` **with no wrapper at all** — a real GPU run, since `kiss_target_live.rs` creates an instance and enumerates physical devices — while chained behind a `cargo fmt`, **with "`cargo test --workspace` IS a GPU run" already written in their own durable memory.** Nothing went wrong; **the only reason anyone knows is that they said so.** ⚠️ **THIS DEFEATS EVERY FIX PROPOSED FOR GAP-223, INCLUDING THE GOOD ONES: the bypass does not need a broken wrapper as its excuse.** "Use `pwsh`" and "remember the wrapper" are both instructions that decay, and **decay was just demonstrated in someone who had the rule recorded.** **DESIGN: make the guarded thing detect the guard's absence.** `gpu-run.ps1` already exports `GPU_RUN_HELD=1` + `GPU_RUN_HELD_PID` into the child environment (built for nested-invocation passthrough, `:222-223`), so the token exists and costs nothing — a live-GPU test helper that refuses unless it is set, **naming the wrapper command in the refusal**, converts a silent success into a loud named failure **at the point of use**. ⚠️⚠️ **THE COVERAGE QUESTION IS THE WHOLE PROBLEM, AND THE OBVIOUS HOME WOULD SHIP AN INERT GUARD.** `fuel-test-support` is the right *conceptual* home — its own doc says **"a missing prerequisite is a failure, not a skip"**, which is exactly what an unheld mutex is — **but it has 8 call sites in the whole repo** (5 `require`, 2 `required_ok`, 1 `required`) against **588 `#[ignore]` occurrences** and **60 direct `CudaDevice::new(0)` calls in `*_live.rs` alone**. **Adding the check there today would be correct, green, and cover almost nothing** — the GAP-077 inert-flip shape, in a guard whose entire value is coverage. **CANDIDATE MECHANISM with real coverage (UNVERIFIED, needs a spike): a default-off cargo feature `gpu-run-guard` on the backend crates, self-enabled via the crate's OWN `[dev-dependencies]` entry** (`fuel-cuda-backend = { path = ".", features = ["gpu-run-guard"] }`), so the check compiles into that crate's test builds and **never into a downstream production build** — `#[cfg(test)]` cannot do this, because integration tests in `tests/` do not set `cfg(test)` for the library under test. **CROSS-PROJECT: the moment Vulkane or Baracuda adopt it, `GPU_RUN_HELD` becomes a shared contract — propose-first, not imposed from Fuel.** Open design questions: the escape hatch (necessary for legitimate cases, but an easy one recreates the problem), and whether the refusal belongs at device construction or at a test-gate helper.  **✅ DESIGN SETTLED 2026-08-20 with Vulkane, THREE CONSTRAINTS THAT ALL COME FROM HAVING BUILT THIS SHAPE BEFORE:** **(1) THE ESCAPE HATCH MUST NOT BE THE SAME VARIABLE.** If bypass means setting `GPU_RUN_HELD=1` yourself, **a hand-set value is indistinguishable from the wrapper's — the defect is rebuilt inside the fix.** Use a separate variable carrying a REASON — `GPU_RUN_UNGUARDED=<why>`, required non-empty — and **print `PROCEEDING UNGUARDED: <why>` to stderr on every run.** That makes bypass possible, costly to normalise (you must write a sentence), and **greppable**, so a bypass that quietly became habitual is discoverable. **An escape hatch that produces no output recreates the exact problem, because the whole defect is that absence looks like success.** **(2) A BARE FLAG IS DEFEATED BY A SHELL THAT OUTLIVES ONE RUN** — `GPU_RUN_HELD=1` exported once into a long-lived debugging shell satisfies the check forever after, **and that shell is exactly where someone would set it.** Vulkane proposed requiring `GPU_RUN_HELD_PID` to name a LIVE process; **a cheaper and STRICTER check exists using infrastructure already present**: `gpu-run.ps1` writes `$env:TEMP\gpu-run.lock` as compact JSON (`pid`/`project`/`cmd`/`start`, `:210-218`) and **DELETES it in its `finally` (`:237`)** — so the helper asserts *the lockfile exists AND its `pid` equals `GPU_RUN_HELD_PID`*. **Zero dependencies, no platform code, and it answers the RIGHT question** (is the lock held right now by the process that exported this) rather than a proxy that PID reuse can spoof. **(3) IT IS NOT A SKIP CLASS — IT MUST BE A HARD FAILURE.** Skipping would make *"you forgot the wrapper"* indistinguishable from *"this machine has no GPU"* — **a misconfiguration of the RUNNER conflated with a property of the MACHINE**, which is precisely the conflation `fuel-test-support` exists to prevent (*"a missing prerequisite is a failure, not a skip"*). **Vulkane is adopting it in `tests/common/mod.rs`** — which every live-device test there already routes through, so their coverage problem is solved where ours is not — **and will report the Windows cost as a second-platform measurement.** **✅ THE MECHANISM BLOCKER IS DISSOLVED — A SOURCE-SCANNING GUARD, AND VULKANE FOUND IT BY REFUTING THEMSELVES (2026-08-20).** They had told Fuel their `tests/common/mod.rs` was a funnel every live-device test routed through. **Then they measured: 20 direct `Instance::new` call sites across 6 test files, only 2 files routing through the helper, 12 sites in `safe_wrapper_test.rs` alone.** They installed the guard, ran a live test **without** the wrapper, and **it passed** — the file builds its own instance via a local helper. **A guard whose absence is indistinguishable from its success, inside the guard written for that defect** — found only because they checked whether it FIRED instead of whether the suite was GREEN. **THE FIX THAT FOLLOWS APPLIES DIRECTLY TO FUEL AND NEEDS NONE OF THE MECHANISM ABOVE: a second, cheap guard that READS THE TEST SOURCES and fails when a `*_live`-style test acquires a device outside the sanctioned helpers.** No `cfg(test)` reach, no feature flag, no dev-dependency self-enable — **an ordinary test that reads files.** Their reframing is the load-bearing part: **you cannot make 588 `#[ignore]` sites call a helper, but you can make a test FAIL WHEN A NEW ONE APPEARS THAT DOESN'T — which converts an unbounded migration into a ratchet that stops the bleeding today.** Fuel already has this idiom natively (`fuel-ir/tests/gap_hedges.rs` scans for untracked GAP hedges; `exhaustive_by_design_marker.rs` enforces a marker invariant), so it lands as an existing shape rather than a new one. **TWO CONSTRAINTS ON IT, both from defects found here the same day: (a) STATE THE POPULATION AT THE SITE — a source scan's population is NAMING AND SYNTAX, so it is a LOWER BOUND, and a device acquired by a spelling the scanner does not know is invisible to it** (the precision lane's `*Inplace` sweep hit this exactly, and wrote the weakness into the file rather than letting green imply proof); **(b) MAKE THE BASELINE A SHRINKING RATCHET, NOT AN ALLOWLIST — assert a COUNT THAT MAY ONLY GO DOWN**, or the existing 60 `CudaDevice::new(0)` sites become an approved list and the ratchet holds a line nobody ever moves.| **BOTH HALVES SHIPPED `ccf677d9`** — `fuel_test_support::require_gpu_run_lock()` (env + lockfile-pid witness, `GPU_RUN_UNGUARDED=<why>` escape printing on every run, 13 unit tests incl. a negative control and a discrimination arm) **plus** `fuel-test-support/tests/gpu_lock_ratchet.rs` (per-SITE attribution via enclosing `fn`, foundation check, two-directional budget, sabotage-validated both ways with the messages read). **First real caller wired** (`fuel-core/tests/recip_abs_realize_live.rs::dev_or_skip`), moving the budget **60 → 59** — a guard with no caller is the inert-flip shape, so the mechanism is demonstrated rather than asserted. **My own delimiter bug was caught by the test written for it:** a plain `contains` on the pid matched `424` against a lockfile holding `4242`, so a DIFFERENT run's lock would have been accepted as ours. **The budget is 59, not the 61 a `git grep` predicted — the scanner disagreed with my pre-measurement and was right** (one hit was a `//!` doc comment naming `new_device()` in prose: grep counts a STRING, the scanner counts a SITE). ⚠️ **RESIDUAL, stated not glossed: the first caller is NOT compile-verified** — that file is `#![cfg(feature = "cuda")]` and a cold forge is 30-56 min with two CUDA builds in flight. Positive control: four other `fuel-core` integration tests already use `fuel_test_support`, a `[dev-dependencies]` entry at `Cargo.toml:80`. ✅ **CONFIRMED 2026-08-20 by the warm-forge lane: `require_gpu_run_lock()` COMPILES.** Marker `GAP214_BASELINE_DONE_EXITCODE=0` with no `The process cannot access the file` anywhere (so the 0 is real, not a masked sharing violation); **BOTH target crates reached and confirmed independently** — `Checking fuel-dispatch v0.10.3` AND `Checking fuel-core v0.10.3` — `Finished dev profile in 62m 37s`, 0 error lines; `fuel-core --tests --features cuda` reached its `Checking` line and finished clean, so `recip_abs_realize_live.rs`'s `#![cfg(feature = "cuda")]` body type-checked. **No revert; the ratchet stays at 59.** **REMAINING: 54 sites of debt** (was 59; →58 when the GAP-214 born-red's own acquisitions were guarded, →54 at `3cf40196`). ✅ **THE FIRST REDUCTION BATCH, AND IT WAS REPORTED THE RIGHT WAY: 4 GUARDS, 0 CORRECTIONS.** Because `BUDGET` counts MATCHES rather than acquisitions, a batch can lower it by rewording a string that was never a site — legitimate, and zero real debt removed. **So a batch must SPLIT its number**, and this one is 4 genuine acquisition sites (`fuel-cuda-backend/tests/{pinned,byte_storage,backend_runtime,baracuda_contiguize}_live.rs`, each a `dev_or_skip() -> Option<CudaDevice>` funnelling one `CudaDevice::new(0)`), **per-site verified: none of the four remains in the unguarded list afterwards, so the delta is 4 RIGHT sites and not a count that moved by luck.** `required_ok` folded in — these were silent skips, so a device-less `--ignored` run now FAILS loudly rather than reporting `ok` having asserted nothing. ⚠️ **HANDOVER NOTE FOR THE NEXT BATCH, and it is why the remaining 54 are not four-a-file: `fuel-dispatch`'s `*_live.rs` MIX a helper with DIRECT `CudaDevice::new(0).expect(…)` in test bodies. Attribution is per enclosing `fn`, so a helper guard covers only what is inside the helper — each direct in-body acquisition needs its own coverage (or folding into the guarded helper). More edits per file than this helper-only crate was.** **Batch selection also had a cost axis: `fuel-cuda-backend --tests` compiled in 8.21s on a warm forge where a `fuel-core` batch would have paid a cold CUDA-stack build.** — the ratchet stops the count rising, it does not reduce it; each reduction lowers `BUDGET` in the same change. |

(Same bound applies to every `n/n` in this row: 20/20 bounds a miss rate under ~14%, not determinism; under 5% needs ~60 runs, under 1% ~300.) **TIMING-IN-READBACK IS FALSIFIED AS A FAMILY:** an explicit `device.synchronize()` between launch and readback predicted 0/20 and measured **10/20 at `.77` against an 11/20 baseline** (test-only diagnostic, marked NOT-A-FIX, deliberately not committed). **Forcing completion cannot complete something that never ran — the data was never in flight**, which kills async copy completion, D2H ordering, and legacy-vs-per-thread default stream in one measurement. **SHARPENED SIGNATURE: the launch returns `Ok`, the synchronize returns `Ok`, and the buffer is untouched — every layer reports success and nothing was written**; across 26 failures it is always all 7 elements or none, never partial, never a wrong value. Reading it as *"the loader is fine, so it's the kernel"* is exactly the inference this exercise exists to prevent. Baracuda's gate-5/6 exoneration is scoped by its own authors to **the bisect, not the intermittent** (their repeats were too few to rule out a 25% event). **NOT CLAIMED: that `.77` is worse than `.78`** — Fisher exact p = 0.105 at n=20/arm, and the `.78` arm ran under peer compile load while `.77` ran on a quiet box, which is a live non-version explanation for the gap if the mechanism is a race. ⚠️ **MECHANISM FOUND 2026-08-19, AND IT IS A PRODUCTION DEFECT, NOT A TEST ARTIFACT: TWO STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT JIT KERNELS COLLIDE ON ONE `FusedOpId` IN THE PROCESS-GLOBAL RUNTIME REGISTRY.** Measured, not inferred — both adopt sites instrumented, printing **`FusedOpId(32768)` at line 281 AND at line 539** in the same run: a hand-written PTX mock and a live Baracuda-synthesized kernel, loaded from different artifacts onto different `CudaDevice` instances (`dev_or_skip()` is `CudaDevice::new(0)`, so each test creates and drops its own device — **the mock's kernel is bound to a device already dropped when the live test runs**). **Isolated combinatorially:** live scalar ALONE 0/20; +`paged_attn` 0/20; `jit_scalar_param`+scalar 0/20; **`jit_adopt`+scalar 11/20**. Only `jit_adopt_loads_and_launches_a_synthesized_cuda_kernel` triggers it, and what distinguishes it is the RECIPE — it synthesizes **`relu_add_region()`, the same region as the live test**, where `jit_scalar_param` uses `mul_scalar_slot_region()`. **Synthesis is byte-identical between passing and failing runs** (same symbol `baracuda_gen_jit_relu_592022893ee04d9f_f32_scalar`, same `structure_key`, same `revision_hash`, same collided id) — **nothing observable differs before launch; the divergence is purely at execution.** **THIS EXPLAINS THE ORIGINAL SYMPTOM COMPLETELY** — all-zero output, no error, intermittency, and survival across `.76`/`.77`/`.78` all follow from a registry collision that has nothing to do with which generator produced the PTX. ⚠️⚠️ **LAST HOP NOW MEASURED — AND THE TEST IS VACUOUS, WHICH IS WORSE THAN THE FLAKE.** A launch-side probe printing the slot's own identity shows that in **both failing AND clean runs** the live test launches **`slot=0 entry_point=fuel_test_jit_relu_add_f32_scalar` — THE MOCK'S ENTRY POINT.** The live test's own symbol (`baracuda_gen_jit_relu_592022893ee04d9f_f32_scalar`), which it synthesizes, adopts and prints, is **NEVER LAUNCHED**: it goes into its own slot and is orphaned, because lookup on the collided `FusedOpId(32768)` returns the **FIRST** registration. **COMPLETE CHAIN, ALL MEASURED:** (1) mock and live share `(region, dtypes, backend)` -> same id; (2) lookup returns the mock's slot-0 dispatcher; (3) that `CudaFunc` is bound to a per-test `CudaDevice` that is **already dropped**; (4) launching from a dropped context is undefined — **sometimes it works, sometimes it silently writes nothing**, which IS the ~50%. **⚠️ THE RESIDUAL INVERTED INTO THE HEADLINE: both kernels compute `relu(add)` on f32, so when the stale kernel happens to work it produces the CORRECT ANSWER and the assertion passes.** Therefore `live_baracuda_synthesizer_full_loop_scalar` **has never exercised Baracuda's synthesized kernel in the full-suite configuration** — libtest orders alphabetically, `jit_` sorts before `live_`, so `jit_adopt` always runs first. **A VACUOUS TEST WITH A 50% FLAKE ATTACHED, AND THE FLAKE IS THE ONLY REASON ANYONE LOOKED** — had the stale context been reliably valid this would be a permanently green test asserting nothing, and GAP-001 would never have been filed. **So the eleven-day 'alpha.78 all-zero regression' was a test running a DIFFERENT KERNEL THAN IT CLAIMS on a destroyed context; no version of `baracuda-kernelgen` was ever involved in either the symptom or the oracle.** ~~RESIDUAL: necessary but not sufficient~~ **RESOLVED by the probe — the downstream decider is dropped-context validity, and the 'sufficient' half was the oracle accepting a correct answer from the wrong kernel.** **SEQUENCING RULING (vindicated): probe BEFORE fixing — the fix destroys the instrument.** ⚠️ **FIX (a) AS I FIRST SPECIFIED IT WAS WRONG — RETRACTED BY THE ARCHITECT 2026-08-19 after the lane pushed back with code and the constitution.** I wrote *"adoption must not return an existing `FusedOpId` for a DIFFERENT kernel"*. **The shared id is BY DESIGN:** `fuel-graph/src/runtime_fused.rs:159` dedups on `region_base_map_hash` and its doc says so explicitly — *"two calls with the same shape but different `name`s return the same id"* — and `docs/architecture/04-optimization.md` §72 ratifies per-decision-point **alternatives**, landed at v0.6. **Making `FusedOpId` kernel-unique would mint one id per artifact and fight the constitution.** The collision is the one link in this chain working as intended; the defects are **selection** (GAP-213) and **slot lifetime** (GAP-214), both now filed so a green suite cannot close them by implication. **REPLACEMENT FIX: (a1)** `adopt_from_response` returns the `KernelRef` it ALREADY loaded via `load_kernel(&art)` and currently throws away — so an adopter uses its own kernel instead of re-looking-up an id that legitimately maps to many. Cheap, no design change. **(b)** **the test must assert that the symbol it adopted is the symbol that launched** — its oracle cannot currently distinguish its own kernel from any other computing `relu(add)`, and (a) removes the flake that was the only thing making the vacuity visible. ⚠️ **SEVERITY RE-SCOPED: this is the MULTI-SESSION SERVING scenario** — many sessions adopting JIT kernels for the same recipe against a process-global registry and a 64-slot bank that is never released is the same shape with more participants. **The harness reproduces by accident what production would reproduce on purpose.** Baracuda's sweep corroborates independently: **20/20 clean at n=7 under a correct-sync launch**, clearing the kernel body AT THEIR GEOMETRY (0/20 bounds below ~14%, no overlap with our ~40%, so it discriminates) — agreeing with a mechanism that never involved the body. Also closed against itself by the lane that raised it: at n=7 with `OperandDesc::new(1,&[7],&[1],F32,4)` **layout and allocation AGREE**, so `elem_count()`-sized grids cannot produce these failures — still a real defect, explicitly NOT the explanation. ~~**OPEN QUESTION, well-posed:** what makes the live-synthesis path lose its write ~40% of the time?~~ ~~launch/readback stream ordering~~ **FALSIFIED by the synchronize arm above.** Next, and cheap because it is FALSIFYING in shape rather than comparative: **does the output pointer the kernel receives equal the pointer we read back?** One failing run showing distinct pointers measures the mechanism instead of reaching it by elimination. Then, only if pointers match: a static out-of-bounds write predicts the rate is INSENSITIVE to machine load, which one clear rate change under deliberate load falsifies. Cite Baracuda's `docs/gap-001-emitter-exoneration.md` (their PR #17) rather than the pin comment; their accepted high-repeat on-device sweep implicates the emitted side decisively on failure but clears it only UNDER THEIR LAUNCH GEOMETRY, since a correct grid for n=7 cannot by construction reproduce a consumer-side grid defect. | **CLOSED** `471cf376` — dispatch through the kernel you adopted; `adopt_from_response` returns the `KernelRef` it already loaded. **20/20 clean, p~4e-5 against the ~40% base rate**, re-verified AFTER the rebase (a clean rebase attests to mergeability, not to whether a measurement still stands). Identity assertion landed FIRST and measured born-red — RED on ~2 of 5 full-suite runs, never RED alone — and is now trivially true, failing the moment anyone reintroduces a lookup-by-id. ⚠️ **THE ROOT DEFECTS REMAIN OPEN AS GAP-213 (nondeterministic selection, ignores its own dtype key, can return a different operand arity) AND GAP-214 (slot lifetime). This fix ROUTES AROUND both; the suite is green with both open, and that green is not evidence about either.** Version attribution DEAD: pinned `=alpha.77` failed 11/20, accused `=alpha.78` failed 5/20 — do not read the difference as real (Fisher p = 0.105; arms ran under different machine load). Cite Baracuda PR #17 |

| GAP-016 | production `panic!(` surface — **118 sites**, not 612 | A | **MEASURED 2026-08-07, and the headline number I filed was WRONG BY 5x.** This row originally read *"~612 `panic!(` sites"* and framed that as *"a hard constitutional rule with a six-hundred-site unexamined surface"*. **The production surface is 118.** After excluding `tests/` dirs, `fuel-correctness-fixtures`, and `#[cfg(test)]` modules, **~80% of the raw count was test assertion helpers** — spot-verified rather than assumed: ALL of fuel-dispatch's ~238 panics sit inside `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (pipelined.rs test mod opens :7219, first panic :7560; dispatch.rs cfg(test) :7165, first panic :7213). **How the error happened, because it is the instructive part:** a raw grep count was reported by one party and filed as a *population* by another, and **neither checked what it contained**. Same instrument lesson as the `XXX`/`HACK` miscount an hour earlier — "I measured it" is not "I measured the right set". **The reframing is itself the first finding.** **Per-crate production panics (118):** fuel-core 37, fuel-cpu-backend 30, fuel-graph 29, fuel-ir 20, fuel-quantized 1, fuel-vulkan-backend 1. **Method:** deterministic stratified stride, **n=33 classified**, reproducible + reported + spot-verified (per the sample-don't-sweep steer). **DISTRIBUTION (n=33 -> 118):** bucket 1 (reachable = gap) 82% -> ~96; bucket 2 (structurally-enforced invariant) 9% -> ~11; bucket 3 (convention-only = gap) 9% -> ~11, floor ~4, range 3-9%. Gaps (1+3) ~91% -> ~107. **THE ARCHITECT'S PREDICTION WAS WRONG AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH:** I said bucket 3 *"is where the findings will be"*. It is **~4-11 sites**, and only **one** is unambiguous — `fuel-vulkan-backend/src/lib.rs:146` `buffer()` panics on host-evicted storage, the doc says "use `buffer_opt`", nothing enforces it, and **eviction is a real runtime state**: a textbook delay-fuse. Two others are op-id<->params pairing guards (`qmatmul.rs:59`, `lib.rs:9588` `_ => panic!`) held as bucket 3 on the conservative reading — the pairing is *registry-construction convention*, not type-enforced, so a new `FusedOpParams` variant or caller could mis-pair with nothing type-level stopping it. **THE REAL STORY IS BUCKET-1 CONCENTRATION, AND IT IS MOSTLY ALREADY TRACKED:** the mkl/accelerate length-guard idiom is **60 of 118 (51%)**, byte-identically duplicated across fuel-core + fuel-cpu-backend (the panic **is** the validation; no upstream `Result`) — already **GAP-008**; dummy-dtype stubs ~19 are reachable because the unit structs are `pub`-constructible — relates to **GAP-002/045/010**. So **~67% of the surface is TWO known idioms**, census-confirmed reachable, not a mystery. **RECOMMENDATION ADOPTED — a full sweep is NOT warranted.** GAP-016 dissolves rather than expands: **(a)** the 60 mkl/accelerate sites become ONE shared `Result`-returning length-checked wrapper, folded into GAP-008; **(b)** the ~19 dummy-dtype sites fold into the GAP-002/045 dummy-dtype family; **(c)** file the ~4-11 bucket-3 rows individually, headline `fuel-vulkan-backend` `buffer()`. Net: *"612 latent panics"* becomes **~11 genuinely-latent plus a mostly-already-tracked reachable backlog** — the registry gets more precise, not bigger. **FOLLOW-THROUGH STATUS (2026-08-07):** **(a) the mkl/accelerate wrapper is VERIFICATION-BLOCKED here** — `fuel-cpu-backend/src/lib.rs:8-11` gates `pub mod mkl` / `accelerate` behind cargo features whose DLLs are absent on this box, so the MKL FFI symbols do not resolve at link time and the `Result` conversion **cannot be test-driven**. Parked for a session with MKL. **A type-only `cargo check --features mkl` pass was considered and DECLINED** — staging an unverified never-panic fix is precisely what the standing rule forbids, and a green `check` on code that has never run would read as verification it is not. **(c) the bucket-3 rows are DONE** (GAP-018, GAP-047). **(b) dummy-dtype is NOT a mechanical fold — the architect mis-sequenced it as one; it is a DESIGN PASS and is UNSTARTED.** The ~19 sites are reachable `WithDType` **trait impls** that panic (`from_f64` / `to_f64` / `to_scalar` / arithmetic) on the **constructible** dummy unit structs. Three options, none local: **(i)** the `WithDType` method signatures return `Result` — a **trait-wide ripple across every dtype**, not a localized change; **(ii)** the dummy types are made structurally impossible to construct or use, so the panics become unreachable **by type** rather than by convention; **(iii)** the panics are documented into a genuine bucket-2 invariant with the enforcement named. **PRICED 2026-08-08, AND THE OPTION SPACE AS WRITTEN WAS WRONG IN TWO PLACES — RULED: DELETE the `dummy_dtype` modules.** **The ~19 panics are NOT one kind.** Per dummy type: **3 `WithDType` methods** (`from_f64`/`to_f64`/`to_scalar`) **plus ~14 `num_traits`/`std::ops` methods** (Add/Sub/Mul/Div/Rem + Assign, Zero, One, Num), the latter **forced by `WithDType`'s `num_traits::NumAssign` supertrait bound.** That split decides everything. **(i) `Result` signatures — DOMINATED, and NOT for the reason this row predicted.** The row priced it as *expensive* (a trait-wide ripple; ~15 impls, ~70/~58 raw call sites, many in hot per-element loops — an order of magnitude past GAP-002's small `Scalar` surface). **It is worse than expensive: it is structurally INCOMPLETE.** `Result` can only reach the **3** `WithDType` methods; the other **~14** are `std::ops`/`num_traits` impls with **FIXED signatures that cannot return `Result`**. **So (i) leaves 14 of ~17 panics standing AND carries the half-migrated-trait hazard.** Removing those would need a trait split (vocabulary vs numeric) — a far larger redesign. **An option that cannot finish is not expensive, it is wrong**, and the row's framing invited it to be compared on cost. **(iii) Document into an invariant — WEAK.** The invariant would be *"the dummies are never instantiated in generic code"*, which is **unenforced**: they are `pub`-constructible and `from_f64`/`zero`/`one` are **associated fns callable without an instance.** Documenting a convention does not convert a never-panic violation into a structural invariant. **(ii) WINS — but NOT in the form this row described.** The literal reading, *make them unconstructible* (private field), **does not work, for the same reason (iii) fails: the panics live in ASSOCIATED FUNCTIONS that need no instance.** **The only realization that makes them unreachable-by-type is that the types do not exist.** A positive-controlled Explore sweep confirms deleting the 4 unit structs, their impls and the 2 re-exports **compiles** — every other occurrence of those names is a `DType`/`HostBuffer`/`HostBufferRef` variant, a comment, or a string. **Deletion doubles as the compiler-adjudicated proof of unusedness** (GAP-075 site-3's lesson). **THE ANTI-REFLEX CHECK WAS RUN, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** The architect warned that four option-2 wins in one day is exactly the streak that stops the price-check running, citing GAP-095 where structural argued the **other** way (64 literals, zero live instances -> seam + parked cure). **Here the asymmetry inverts: structural is the CHEAPEST option, the ONLY COMPLETE one, and ATOMIC so it cannot half-migrate.** *When the cheap option is also the complete one it dominates — and the mover could say precisely why, rather than by reflex.* **ALIGNMENT, not merely cost:** the dummies are a **superseded pattern**. GAP-097's live approach for a new dtype (`F8E5M2`) adds `DType` + `Scalar` variants that **decline via `Result`** — no panicking `WithDType` dummy; `with_dtype!` is never invoked for them, and `dispatch.rs:5582` records them as deliberately *excluded*. **If `F4`/`F6*`/`F8E8M0` ever gain real numeric impls that is a fresh, correct `WithDType` — the placeholder contributes nothing toward it.** **REQUIRED WITH THE CHANGE:** a **decisions-log entry** (it removes `pub` types — a core-claim change) whose durable sentence is **what to do instead**; and the **panic-census before/after in the commit** — the production surface was measured at **118** and this removes **~17 textual sites (≈68 runtime across 4 types)**. That matters because **GAP-141's `panic!` ratchet is measured but NOT enforced, pending exactly this row's policy — and a ratchet is easier to arm the closer the count is to something defensible.** **DONE @ `3fce62d2` — and the pricing pass's own verification claim was FALSE, caught by the compile it had skipped.** **The mover reported "compiler-verified feasible"; it was an Explore-agent grep INFERENCE reported in the vocabulary of a compile.** The sweep **missed an entire second impl set** — `fuel-ir/src/cpu_storage.rs`'s `host_dtype_dummy!(crate::F6E2M3, …)`, the `HostDType` placeholders (2 more panics + 2 `Err` arms per type) — because its regexes did not reach a **macro-ARGUMENT type position**. A compile surfaced it on the first run: `cannot find type F6E2M3 in the crate root`, 4 errors. **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT'S CREDIT FOR THIS IS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** The catch came from `cargo check -p fuel-ir --features dlpack,ug,serde`, and the architect had pushed the feature list on the grounds that *features hide code*. **Checked: `fuel-ir/src/lib.rs:31` is a bare `mod cpu_storage;` — NOT feature-gated — so a DEFAULT `cargo check -p fuel-ir` would have caught it identically.** The features were **incidental**; **what did the work was running the compiler on `fuel-ir` at all instead of accepting a sweep as verification.** The rule stands on the `telemetry` incident; **it is not what fired here**, and attributing the win to it would be the fact-vs-consequence error in a case whose outcome flatters the rule. **THE LESSON, in the mover's sharper form: an agent's "it would compile" is not a compile, and DELETION-AS-COMPILE-CHECK is the oracle.** That is **their own GAP-075 site-3 principle**, under-relied on by the person who established it — **knowing a rule is not the same as reaching for it.** **Third grep-wrong-by-construction of the day**, after `Gelu` excluding `GeluErf` and `eprintln!("SKIP` missing 85% of its population. **Common factor: a pattern that cannot see a syntactic form the language allows.** **SCOPE GREW, DECISION HELD:** both impl sets are the same superseded placeholder, so the fix stayed deletion — of two files. **The row's original "~19" reconciles as 17 + 2**, so the ROW was right and the interim 17 was the incomplete count. **Census corrected 118 -> 99** (not the 101 that would have shipped), **and the decisions-log was fixed BEFORE the push.** **VERIFICATION, satisfying the provenance rule and the target-line amendment without being asked:** `-p fuel-ir --features dlpack,ug,serde` (`Checking fuel-ir`, 3.43s); `-p fuel-dispatch --features telemetry` (`Checking fuel-dispatch`, 37.87s, `structure_key_derive.rs` compiled); `-p fuel-core` default (`Checking fuel-core`, 1m14s, 0 errors, whole CPU dep tree). **WHAT THIS DOES NOT COVER (per GAP-159's standing check): GPU backends and feature-off code are TEXTUALLY-SWEPT, not compiler-adjudicated** — re-done with qualified-path forms, glob and grouped imports, and a positive control disambiguating the independent GPU enum variants. **SUPERSEDED GUIDANCE BELOW, kept because the row was wrong in a way worth seeing:** **~~START WITH OPTION (ii)~~ (2026-08-07):** check the feasibility of making the dummies **structurally unconstructible** FIRST — if it is feasible it likely dominates the other two and is much the cleanest, since the panics die *by type* rather than by convention. Only if it is not should (i) be priced against (iii). **Do not start this tired:** their standing note is that the failure mode here is not "unfinished" but **"a half-migrated trait the next session cannot distinguish from deliberate choices"** — and a trait-wide `Result` ripple begun and abandoned mid-context is exactly that shape. **This is the same SHAPE as GAP-002's `Scalar` question, and that precedent is useful but not decisive:** GAP-002 chose `Result` constructors plus an honest `Err` for the cases that cannot be represented — but `Scalar`'s constructor surface is small, whereas `WithDType` is implemented by *every* dtype, so option (i)'s blast radius is an order of magnitude larger. Whoever takes it should price that before choosing. **COUNT AUDIT (no change to 118):** the 118 is already the **corrected** figure — the original triage applied the scanner-leak corrections by hand before reporting. The pre-correction "leaky" count was **123**; the 5-site delta is auditable as 3 in `dlpack/header_check.rs` (bare `#[test]`), 1 `dlpack_view/tests.rs`, 1 `metal-kernels/tests.rs`, and the scanner-faithful re-count after the fix (`1b98c3bf`) confirms **118**. So the census and the now-fixed scanner agree. **Bucket-3 sites: only 3 are CONCRETE** — `fuel-vulkan-backend/src/lib.rs:146` (→ **GAP-018**) and the two pairing guards (→ **GAP-047**). The remaining ~1-8 of the ~4-11 estimate are **extrapolated from the sample, not identified sites**, and must not be cited as if they were. A **targeted mini-sweep of just the two classes found** (op<->params guards; host-eviction/tier guards) would surface the rest without a full sweep. **Also surfaced:** two test-leaks the initial file-scan missed (parent-mod `cfg(test)`; a bare `#[test]` in `header_check.rs`), and `test_utils.rs:36` — an assert helper in a **non-`cfg(test)` `pub mod`**, i.e. test support that leaked into the production namespace; worth relocating. | GAP-017 | `scripts/gpu-run.ps1` — DISCOVERABILITY, not the lock itself | A | **A sibling project has been hand-serializing GPU access for a problem that was solved 2026-08-01, and running UNGUARDED `cargo test` in the gaps.** Vulkane asked Fuel's coordinator to poll four Fuel sessions for an all-clear before its device-touching suites (2026-08-07), stating *"the lock protocol Fuel and Baracuda were going to land hasn't shipped"*. **It has.** `scripts/gpu-run.ps1` exists: `Global\gpu-run` machine-wide named mutex (line 76, commented "MUST be Global\ ... DO NOT VERSION"), `AbandonedMutexException` recovery that survives hard-kill/bugcheck (line 20), and **its own documented usage example at line 59 is `-Project vulkane -- cargo test --workspace`** — their exact case, written in as first-class. **Why this is Tier A:** unguarded concurrent GPU runs are what produced the 2026-07-31 host-aperture kernel bugcheck ([postmortem](../postmortems/2026-07-31-gpu-host-aperture-crash.md)); a peer who does not know the lock exists is an active recurrence risk, and a *second* project believing it must hand-coordinate means the belief may be general. **The defect is COMMUNICATION, not mechanism** — the lock works and was validated under a real contended multi-process kill. **Also note the anti-pattern in the request itself:** a human-mediated "is anyone using the GPU?" all-clear is **strictly worse than the mutex**, because it is a snapshot that can go stale between the answer and the run. Any peer asking for one should be handed the invocation instead. **SHARPER DIAGNOSIS (Vulkane, self-reported 2026-08-07) — this is NOT only a missed announcement.** Line 13 of `gpu-run.ps1` records it was **co-designed 2026-08-01 across fuel + baracuda + vulkane** — vulkane was *in the room*, then hand-serialized for six more days. Their account: a standing note said *"hold live GPU runs until Fuel+Baracuda's lock protocol lands, ping claude-peers to serialize by hand."* That note was **correct when written on 2026-07-31 and became wrong on 2026-08-01**, and it was acted on for six days without anyone re-evaluating its predicate. **The generalizable rule: a durable note that says "wait for X" needs a check for X, or it silently converts into permanent policy.** Corollary, in their words: *the recipients most likely to miss an announcement are the ones who already wrote down the old state, and they will look compliant the whole time.* An announcement alone therefore cannot fix this class — the fix is that "wait-for" notes carry an expiry check. **CAUTION, load-bearing given the Tier A framing (Lightbulb, 2026-08-07): the GPU lock is NOT a general flakiness fix.** GAP-019 is the worked counter-example from the same box the same day — a concurrent-*build* race the lock cannot touch. Anyone reading "Tier A GPU lock" and reaching for it against a superficially-similar flake will lose an hour. **Action:** confirm each GPU-touching sibling (baracuda, vulkane, lightbulb, kiss-ref) knows the lock shipped and how to invoke it cross-project; consider a line in each project's own CLAUDE.md rather than only Fuel's, since that is where the knowledge failed to reach. | OPEN |
| GAP-019 | build environment (shared box) — **not a Fuel source path**; mitigation belongs in `CLAUDE.md` | — | **RESOLVED SAME DAY: a concurrent-BUILD race, not a code defect. Fuel builds in the same environment and is exposed.** Lightbulb saw ~30 test binaries linking concurrently with rustc naming *different* dependency crates as "required to be available in rlib format" on each run, plus intermittent ICEs, while every target built and passed individually. **`cargo test --tests -j 4` passes completely** (614 lib tests + 25 integration targets, 0 failed, exit 0) on the identical tree that failed three times at default parallelism — this box gives cargo **~22 jobs**. **Five hypotheses falsified before the answer:** sccache, toolchain, stale artifacts, disk, and *Lightbulb-specific*. The fifth fell because **Fuel hit the same signature the same day**: Lightbulb's three dumps (`rustc-ice-2026-08-07T18_00_1{1,2}-*`, three within **two seconds**) lead with `delayed bug: no resolution for an import`, cascading through `path with Res::Err but no error emitted` and a run of `no type-dependent def for method call`; Fuel's lane-A worker independently reported the same leading string from two dumps in the `fuel-crash-vmm` worktree at 02:04/02:12. Surviving explanation: **concurrent rustc invocations against a shared build environment** — load-dependent, nondeterministic in *which* crates it names, and it **vanishes entirely when serialized**. **MITIGATION (adopt in Fuel): cap build parallelism.** `-j 4` is *known-good*, **not** a bisected boundary — the real threshold is un-measured, so do not treat 4 as "the limit". Note Fuel's existing rule is *"ONE cargo invocation at a time"*, which says nothing about `-j` **within** one invocation — that is the actual gap here, and a single `cargo test --workspace` at ~22 jobs trips it unaided. Pairs with GAP-017: different hazards, both fixed by not letting the box run flat out. **⚠️ EVIDENCE RETENTION — 5 occurrences today, 3 survive.** Fuel deleted two as untracked-file hygiene; Lightbulb deleted a fourth (`rustc-ice-2026-08-07T17_28_17-15204.txt`) about an hour before understanding what it was. Both checked the file was untracked and binned it anyway, for the same reason: **a stray file at a repo root reads as debris, and "untracked crash dump" is a category people tidy without thinking.** **Rule (Lightbulb's framing, adopted): it is not "never delete ICE dumps" — it is that a crash artifact is EVIDENCE until something explains it, so hygiene comes AFTER the explanation, not before.** Lightbulb's three are preserved outside the repo (verified on the bytes, not relayed) so a `git clean` cannot take them. **Owner: Lightbulb held the investigation and resolved it.** Fuel's remaining action is the `-j` cap. | **RESOLVED** (mitigation pending in Fuel) |
| GAP-018 | `fuel-vulkan-backend/src/lib.rs:146` (`buffer()`) | — | **The one unambiguous convention-only delay fuse found by the GAP-016 census (bucket 3).** `buffer()` **panics** when the storage has been **evicted to host**. The doc comment says to use `buffer_opt` instead — and **nothing enforces that**: no type distinction between an evicted and a resident handle, no `Result`, just prose. **Eviction to host is a real runtime state, not a hypothetical** — the Vulkan backend evicts under memory pressure — so this is reachable today by a caller who followed the type signature and not the comment. Tier A because it is a live never-panic violation on a production path, distinguishing it from GAP-047 (which needs a future code change to become reachable). **Fix, in preference order:** type-enforce the tier so an evicted handle cannot offer `buffer()` at all; else return `Option`/`Result`; else fault the storage back in. The first is the only one that makes the comment unnecessary. | OPEN |

If no, quantized prefill above batch 1 is *broken* on CUDA — a hard error, not a fallback. That distinction is exactly the "existence ≠ enforcement / correctly-named validator never invoked" pattern that has bitten Fuel repeatedly (see the `fkc-contract-verification-gap` memory), so **do not assume the fallback is wired because a comment says it should be**. **⚠️⚠️ SUPERSEDED — READ THE END OF THIS ROW BEFORE QUOTING THE NEXT SENTENCE. The conclusion below was RE-TIERED 2026-08-08 by measurement: the guard is NOT reached by a real prefill. Relaying the text that follows as current has now mis-informed the same external consumer TWICE. ⚠️⚠️** **ANSWERED 2026-08-07 (SUPERSEDED) — it is BROKEN, not unoptimised.** Advertisement does not exclude `m>1` and **cannot**: (1) `BackendCapabilities` has **no shape/extent dimension at all** — the full struct is `backend_id`, `device_location`, `op_dtype_support: HashSet<(OpKind, DType)>`, `required_alignment`, `access_granularity_bits`, `transfer_paths`, `storage_substrate`, throughput rates; support is keyed on `(op, OUTPUT dtype)` only, so "M must be 1" is **structurally inexpressible** — not a forgotten registration, a type that cannot carry the constraint. (2) No m-based guard exists anywhere in dispatch/placement; positive control: the same query **did** return Vulkan's `Q4_0 → qmatvec_q4_0 (M=1) or matmul_q4_0_tiled (M>1)` line, so it finds what it should — the CUDA side has nothing. (3) The bail text is *"only M=1 supported on CUDA today; got total_rows={total_rows}. Route prefill to Vulkan"* — an instruction to a caller with no mechanism to comply, and it computes `total_rows = batch * m`, so **batch>1 trips it even at m=1** — **CODE-GROUNDED, NOT MEASURED (labelled 2026-08-07 at the verifier's own insistence): the line was READ and its arithmetic is unambiguous, but no batched quantized decode was executed to observe the bail fire.** Cheap to confirm for whoever next holds a CUDA build: one realize of a batch-2 Q4_0 matvec. Kept distinct because this claim is already shaping a downstream consumer spec, and a read is a fact about the code while "the bail fires" is a fact about a run. (4) `pipelined.rs:798` is fail-fast by contract ("no runtime CPU fallback"), so the `Err` is terminal, not a retry. **Verdict:** an `Err` not a panic (no never-panic violation), but a **hard realize-time failure on a reachable path**, not a decline the optimizer routes around. The doc comment *was* the entire enforcement. **Structural root cause carved out to GAP-031.** **⚠️ THE SUGGESTED PROBE ABOVE TESTS THE WRONG AXIS — corrected 2026-08-08 by Lightbulb, and the correction is theirs about their own prior claim.** This row suggested confirming with *"one realize of a batch-2 Q4_0 matvec"*, i.e. probing the **batch** axis. **The consumer's actual failure is on the `m` axis at batch 1.** Lightbulb had recorded *"Lightbulb's runner builds `batch = vec![ctx]` (one request per batch), so GGUF at batch 1 is safe on CUDA — the wall is hit at the batching step, not the GGUF step."* **That conflates `batch = 1` with `m = 1`.** They verified their own side: `engine_model.rs:222` calls `model.prefill(&ids, &mut st)` with the **full encoded prompt in a single call**, so on every request `m = prompt_tokens` (6 for their test prompts, far more in practice) and **batch size never enters it**. **Consequence: a one-request-at-a-time server trips this on the FIRST forward pass of EVERY request. The wall is at the GGUF step, not the batching step** — so GAP-007 gates their GGUF work **outright**, rather than gating batching later as they had sequenced it. **`batch = 1` and `m = 1` coincide in the decode steady state — which is where most reasoning about a serving loop lives — and diverge exactly at PREFILL, which is the one place the arithmetic matters.** **MEASUREMENT COMMISSIONED as step 0 of GAP-029 (2026-08-08): one realize of a Q4_0 matmul at `m = 6`, `batch = 1`, on CUDA through `gpu-run`, with a positive build artifact required before either result is believed.** Requested by Lightbulb, who explicitly declined to treat the conjunction as established — **the `bail!` is a code read of `storage.rs`, not an observed failure**, and this row already carries that caveat because an earlier `batch > 1` claim was relayed to them over-firmly on the same basis. **Second time that discipline has caught something between these two projects.** **RE-TIERING HELD PENDING THE RESULT, deliberately not in anticipation.** Lightbulb verified their target path is CUDA (`src/model_fuel/device.rs:22` — CPU is the *degraded* fallback, not the target), so **if the bail fires this is a hard blocker on a named external consumer's target hardware**, not an internal optimisation gap — a different tier. If it does not fire, the caveat now attached to GAP-029 is wrong and comes off. **RE-TIERED 2026-08-08: STILL A REAL DEFECT, BUT NOT ON THE PREFILL PATH — AND NO LONGER LIGHTBULB'S BLOCKER.** The measurement commissioned as GAP-029 Step 0 (`origin/gap-029-quantized-decode@e8d63a2a`) shows a Q4_0 `m=6, batch=1` matmul realized through the **normal lazy path** never reaches this guard. **The guard is real and reachable by a DIRECT call; it is not reached by a real prefill.** So the `batch*m` arithmetic above is right about the **mechanism** and the earlier conclusion was wrong about the **path**. **Lightbulb had re-sequenced GGUF behind this on the architect's flag and has been corrected directly.** Tier drops: an internal correctness gap on a directly-callable surface, not a hard blocker on a named consumer's target hardware. | OPEN — **REAL, but off the prefill path** |

| GAP-015 | `fuel-dispatch/src/dispatch.rs:6554` (`global_bindings`) ×; `GLOBAL_REGISTRY` | B | **COLD-START LOCK DEADLOCK on a PRODUCTION path — reclassified 2026-08-07, was filed as a test-harness hang.** Reproduced + classified by the owner: a true user-space lock deadlock, not contention (CPU **flat** 25.38s→25.39s over 135s; 45 threads all `ThreadState=5`, **44 on `WaitReason=37` `WrAlertByThreadId`** = the SRWLOCK/condvar/`OnceLock` primitive; 45th is the main thread joining). **Deterministic with a concurrency threshold:** passes `-j2/4/8/16`, hangs `-j24/32`, and at both hanging values **exactly the same 343 of 760 tests complete — byte-identical sets** (a race would jitter). **Two-party:** removing either `dispatch::` or `optimize::` clears it (each against a still-hanging control); keep-based bisect on `dispatch::` converged to 7 `global_bindings_registers_*_family_from_contract` tests, and the culprit *spans* the final split ⇒ several concurrent callers, not one test. **MECHANISM — CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT 2026-08-07. It is NOT a two-lock ABBA; it is a SINGLE-lock recursive-read deadlock on `GLOBAL_BINDINGS`.** The earlier suspect (the `OnceLock` initializer calling `register_derived_gpu_caps` and taking a **write** lock on the separate `GLOBAL_REGISTRY`, an ABBA candidate) was the hypothesis held by *both* the owner and lane A, and an env-gated lock trace (`FUEL_LOCK_TRACE=1`) run on the deterministic reproducer killed it. At the block: **3 threads blocked wanting `BINDINGS.WRITE`, all in `extend_global_bindings` (`dispatch.rs:6618`)**; **~30 threads blocked wanting `BINDINGS.read`**; **zero threads inside the initializer**, with 20+ already holding `got BINDINGS.read` ⇒ **init had COMPLETED**. Since init is done, readers can only be blocked by a queued writer, and writers only by a reader that never releases — **both parties on the same `RwLock`**. `GLOBAL_REGISTRY` is not in the loop, which is *why* lane A's Party-2 search came back negative: **that negative is a real result, not a dead end.** Supported mechanism: std `RwLock` blocks new readers once a writer is queued (anti-writer-starvation), so a thread holding a read guard that requests a **second** read waits on a lock its own guard blocks. **Rule any static search for this needs:** `RwLockReadGuard` has a `Drop` impl, so NLL does **not** end it early — it lives to end of scope. **Explicitly NOT proven, do not inherit as fact:** (1) the trace does not log guard *drops*, so the three `got BINDINGS.read … later want BINDINGS.*` threads are equally consistent with sequential non-overlapping acquisitions — suggestive, not proof; (2) **the offending code site is not yet found** — the obvious candidate is refuted, since `pipelined_bridge.rs:795` and `:877` sit in *different* functions (`build_optimized_graph`@705, `dispatch_with_plan_retry`@807) so the first guard is dead by the second call. **Also refuted earlier and still refuted:** `SystemTopology::build` does not hold a registry guard across the bindings call (`topology.rs:419-427` scopes and drops before `:433`). **Why this is Tier A and not a gate nuisance (architect + owner, code-grounded 2026-08-07):** `global_bindings()` is a **production** entry point, not test-only. Verified **by enclosing `fn`** (see the method warning below): `fuel-core/src/pipelined_bridge.rs:795,877` in `build_optimized_graph` (the optimizer path); `judge/mod.rs:1233,1255,1274`; `cast_fusion.rs:51`; `fuel-dispatch/src/pipelined.rs:2051` in `order_for`; and — the strongest of them — **`pipelined.rs:2215`, the FIRST statement of `fn compiler_thread_body` (declared :2209), i.e. a background compiler thread whose opening act is to touch the `OnceLock`.** That is a concurrent cold-start caller present in production **by construction, not by workload shape**; `driver.rs:112`'s comment corroborates ("re-acquiring `global_bindings()`, so a background…"). Its own doc comment names "production callers picking up the global table". **The serializer that would have defused this does not exist:** the one eager `drop(global_bindings())` warm-up (`pipelined.rs:8494`, commented as forcing init "before we read it") sits inside `fn realize_with_optimized_route_empty_equals_arm0` (:8433) — **test-only**. No production eager-init was found. So the test suite at `-j24/32` is not the defect — it is the **first workload that fans out enough concurrent cold-start callers to expose it**, and concurrent cold start is precisely the multi-session serving profile (stated near-term priority). Treat as a latent production deadlock. **The corrected mechanism STRENGTHENS this:** the writer party is `extend_global_bindings`, which per-backend init paths call in production — so both sides of the deadlock exist outside tests. **METHOD WARNING — do not repeat this (architect's error, caught by the owner):** the first `#[cfg(test)]` in a file is **not** the test boundary. `pipelined.rs` has five (`58, 7219, 8722, 8725, 8739`) and its main test module is at **7219**; line 58 guards a single `use`. An earlier version of this row cited per-file "boundary" line numbers derived that way — they happened to be right for three files and the method is still unsound. **Resolve production-vs-test by enclosing `fn`, never by the first `cfg(test)`.** Still UNVERIFIED by that standard, and explicitly not to be assumed test-only: `jit_ingest.rs:2737,2979,3697`; `optimize.rs:1011,2080`; `plan.rs:2000`. **Symptom text corrected:** the old "32 tests stall (18 `optimize::*` + 14 `pipelined::*`)" is measured-wrong and understates it — 343/760 complete and 401 never report, but most of those **never started** (all workers parked; only ~44 can block at once). `ranker::` alone contributes 170 never-started, and those are pure-CPU. The suite stops dead. Interim gate: `--test-threads=1`. **DIAGNOSED 2026-08-07 — cause found, two independent instruments agree; not yet fixed.** `fuel-dispatch/tests/runtime_fusion_optimize_e2e.rs:84-90` already documents the hazard in prose: *"`adopt_runtime_fused` and `clear_runtime_fused_for_tests` WRITE the global binding table (`extend_global_bindings`), so a `global_bindings()` read guard must NOT be held across them — same-thread read-then-write deadlocks. Each optimize call below therefore scopes its own read guard and drops it before the next adopt/reset."* The lock trace matches it exactly. **The defect is that the discipline is HAND-MAINTAINED PER TEST — sound in isolation, unsound across CONCURRENT tests.** Thread A scopes its guard correctly and holds it briefly; thread B concurrently calls `adopt_runtime_fused` → a writer queues; A's next read blocks behind that writer (std anti-starvation); A never releases; B never gets the write. **Every individual test can be correct and the suite still deadlocks** — which is precisely why per-module runs and `-j≤16` pass while `-j24/32` does not. **Second incident on the same lock (pattern, not coincidence):** `runtime_fused_kernels.rs:415-424` documents a prior bug where `finalize()`'s `.expect()` inside `extend_global_bindings` **panicked while holding the write lock**, poisoning it for every later test in the binary. This is the "existence ≠ enforcement — seams beat vigilance" class: a correctly-stated invariant with nothing enforcing it. **Production reachability CONFIRMED by the comment itself: adopt runs on the G7 background thread in production**, so the writer genuinely exists outside tests. **FIX RULING (architect, 2026-08-07) — copy-on-write read path.** Three shapes were proposed; the ruling is the third. *(a) Enforce guard scope structurally* — better than a comment but still a rule whose violation reproduces this deadlock. *(b) Take `adopt_runtime_fused` off the global write lock* — **REJECTED, it does not fix the bug**: the trace's blocked writers are in `extend_global_bindings`, the per-backend **registration** path, so the deadlock stays reachable via backend init while appearing fixed. *(c) **CHOSEN — make the read path immune (copy-on-write), so readers never block behind writers.*** Rationale: it is Fuel's own idiom (immutable content-addressed structures with CoW/structural sharing — the same idiom the optimizer-architecture thread names for plan specialization), the profile is textbook read-mostly (reads on every optimize/dispatch/Judge/cast-fusion/compiler-thread; writes only on adoption + backend init), and it retires the **lock-poisoning** incident class too, since there is no long-lived write lock to poison. **Implementation: dep-free** — `RwLock<Arc<KernelBindingTable>>` with an accessor that clones the `Arc` and drops the guard *inside* the accessor, so no guard can escape and the recursive-read deadlock becomes unrepresentable; writers clone-mutate-swap. `arc-swap` only if profiling later shows the momentary read lock matters. **Snapshot semantics — the argument is STRONGER than "nice for replay" (owner, accepted 2026-08-07):** a reader holding an `Arc` snapshot sees a point-in-time table, so an optimize pass becomes a **pure function of (graph, table-version)**. Without that, a pass observing the table mutate mid-pass can produce a plan **not reproducible from its own inputs**, which breaks the **recipe-identity property (`base_map_hash` equality) that Tier-2 convergence depends on**. So CoW is not merely deadlock-avoidance with a determinism bonus — it repairs a latent reproducibility hole. *Stated honestly as unverified:* no path is known to depend on seeing a mid-pass adoption (adopt exists to affect *subsequent* passes), but that has not been exhaustively checked. **Empirical confirmation that the writers are NOT adopt-specific, and a correction with it:** a causal test removing every `runtime_fused`-named test still hangs (control 343 ok / HUNG; skip-runtime_fused 342 ok / HUNG). The earlier attribution of the writers to `adopt_runtime_fused`/`clear_runtime_fused_for_tests` was **over-attribution from the doc comment** — the doc names those two because they are the ones *that test* had to discipline, not because they are the only writers. This is why fix option (b) was rejected: it would have passed a reproducer built around adopt and left the production registration path deadlockable. **Instrumentation landed on main as its own commit `c9b9e991`,** blind spots documented with it (no guard-drop logging; "want" is ambiguous between initializer-block and read-block; dispatch globals only). **Weigh before building:** flag any path that depends on seeing a mid-pass adoption. **Precondition CONFIRMED by a bounded sweep (lane A, 2026-08-07):** `fuel-core/pipelined_bridge.rs:795` holds `bindings_guard = global_bindings()` **across the whole optimize call** (:804), so the recursive-read model requires no hole — a guard genuinely is held across a re-entrant region. **REFUTED in the same sweep, correcting lane A's own earlier lead:** `cast_fusion_predicate` is **NOT production-wired** (test-only); production optimize uses LoweringRule + FusionRule (`opt.rs:187-228`). **STILL OPEN:** the identity of the *second* `global_bindings()` read taken under the :795 guard — an optimize callee that ignores the passed-by-ref guard; candidates are `order_for` (`pipelined.rs:2051`), `compiler_thread_body` (:2215), Lowering/Fusion rule internals, and the residency/layout passes. **Note this does NOT block the fix:** option 3 makes a recursive read unrepresentable at *any* site, so the build can proceed while the specific site is still unnamed — but a sweep that comes back empty would mean the model has a hole, so record the result either way. **GATE MUST MATCH THE CHANGE KIND:** returning `Arc<…>` instead of `RwLockReadGuard<'static, …>` is a **type/signature** change touching every caller — `--lib` is a blind instrument. Gate `--all-targets` on every consuming crate, and under `--features cuda` and `--features vulkan` separately (feature-gated callers make a green default build prove nothing).  **BUILT 2026-08-07 @ `c95d140a`.** `global_bindings() -> Arc<KernelBindingTable>`; guard acquired+released inside the accessor, writers clone-mutate-swap. **GATE = the reproducer, not a proxy:** `-j32` **HUNG at 343/760 -> PASS 4.3s (744 passed / 0 failed)**; `-j16` passed before and after. Swept past the failure region (`-j1/24/48/64` all pass, `runtime_fusion_optimize_e2e` at `-j32/-j64` too). **Throughput control:** `-j1` 17.4s vs `-j64` 2.8s (6.2x) — a fix that accidentally serialized everything would also go green, so a pass alone was not sufficient evidence. **Regression test is BORN-RED-verified by construction, not asserted:** the new test was grafted onto *reverted* sources and observed to FAIL at 30.00s (watchdog on a real deadlock) vs 0.58s green. It reduces the deadlock to **one thread** — holding a snapshot across `extend_global_bindings()` was a *self*-deadlock under the old type — so it no longer depends on `-j24+` to reproduce. **WRINKLE THE HANDOFF NOTES MISSED, and it makes naive CoW WRONG:** `adopt_runtime_fused` requires its check-then-bind to be ATOMIC w.r.t. other writers (its own doc comment, `runtime_fused_kernels.rs:262-274`). A bare clone-mutate-swap breaks it — two threads racing the same region+kernel both see 'not bound', both register, and reproduce the duplicate-`KernelRef` panic; it would also lose updates outright. Fixed by serializing writers on a separate `BINDINGS_WRITER` mutex, so readers stay unblocked while the `RwLock` write guard covers only a pointer swap. That separation also **retires the second incident on this lock** (`runtime_fused_kernels.rs:415-424`): `finalize()`'s `.expect()` no longer panics under the write lock, so it cannot poison the read path, and the unwind discards the clone leaving the published table at its last good value. **Blast radius was far SMALLER than estimated** — exactly ONE signature named the guard type (`global_bindings` itself) and `&Arc<T>` deref-coerces everywhere `&RwLockReadGuard<T>` did, so all ~80 call sites compiled unchanged. Gated to the change kind anyway: `--all-targets` on fuel-dispatch AND fuel-core, under `--features cuda` and `--features vulkan` separately, all exit 0. **STILL OPEN (does not affect the fix):** the identity of the second `global_bindings()` read taken under `pipelined_bridge.rs:795`'s guard was never pinned — CoW makes a recursive read unrepresentable at *any* site, so it is now moot for correctness, but it was never found and that is recorded rather than quietly dropped. Snapshot semantics claimed as a reproducibility gain are argued, NOT exhaustively proven: no path is known to depend on seeing a mid-pass adoption. **ARCHITECT'S NOTE — the wrinkle is a defect in the RULING, and recording it only as an implementation detail would lose the lesson.** The builder framed it generously ("neither the ruling nor the handoff carried the invariant"). The sharper statement: **I ruled a SHAPE — copy-on-write — and a shape does not carry the properties the thing it replaces was providing by accident.** `adopt_runtime_fused`'s check-then-bind atomicity was written down as a **comment on the caller**, never as a property of the lock, so nothing in the ruling's field of view mentioned it. A session working faithfully from my notes would have written the clean two-line version, run the gate **I specified**, gotten green, and shipped a race — because the race needs two adopters of the *same* region and the suite does not reliably produce that. **The gate was correct and would not have caught it.** Generalizable, and it has no natural prompt: **when replacing a synchronization primitive, enumerate what callers were getting from it INCIDENTALLY** — the guarantees nobody wrote down as guarantees because the old primitive made them free. Same family as *existence != enforcement*: the invariant existed, as prose, and prose is not a mechanism. **THE CONTROL THAT MATTERED — framing CORRECTED BY ITS OWN AUTHOR, because the first write-up implied foresight they disclaim:** `-j1` 17.4s vs `-j64` 2.8s — **6.2x**. A fix that accidentally serialized everything would ALSO have gone green on the reproducer, so "it passes" was never sufficient evidence on its own. **But it was NOT designed as a false-pass guard.** `bzfevnl6` ran the wide thread sweep for *margin* — confidence `-j32` was not a fluke — and the 6.2x only registered as meaningful when they **read the numbers back**. They asked it not be recorded as "design a throughput control", because the next person would think they need foresight they do not need. **The weaker but far more usable lesson: when a fix works by changing CONCURRENCY BEHAVIOR, the pass/fail axis and the performance axis are DIFFERENT AXES, and a gate on one is blind to the other.** Checkable after the fact by anyone who looks at both numbers — which is the point. Pair that with the born-red-by-construction method used here — the regression test was grafted onto **reverted sources** and observed to fail at the 30s watchdog vs 0.58s green — and both directions are pinned by evidence rather than by assertion. | **FIXED** — built, gated on the reproducer, born-red verified. |
