Fuel co-authors; the architect writes Fuel's half (RFC work is the architect's lane, so it does NOT consume a worker or compete with the sk4 regen).** **⚠️ FUEL'S ORIGINAL CITATION WAS AN OVERREACH AND IS WITHDRAWN.** The architect said KISS violated its own §6.1-0004. **It does not reach `classify.md` §6.1 or `ops.md` §6.16 — it binds the TRACEABILITY MATRIX only.** The KISS architect's framing is narrower and worse for KISS in the way that matters: **the failure mode was identified, a normative rule was written against it for the ONE artifact where it had already bitten, and every other duplicated normative fact was left hand-maintained. The principle was adopted locally and never generalized.** That argues for a **clause**; a violation claim would only have argued for a script. **AND FUEL'S OWN SUGGESTION WAS OVER-PROMISED, corrected by the same reply: GENERATION WOULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT TODAY'S DEFECT.** The sk4 drift was entirely in hand-written prose *around* the tables (`6.1-0002`, `6.1-0003`, `6.1-0012`, the complex family across five `ops.md` sites). **It prevents the NEXT class, not THIS class** — and a hardening item that over-promises is the one that gets trusted past its scope. **THE RFC'S CORE CONTRIBUTION FROM FUEL: THESE ARE TWO PATTERNS, NOT ONE, AND ONE CLAUSE WOULD ADDRESS ONE WHILE CLAIMING BOTH.** **PATTERN A — a normative fact duplicated across N artifacts, one of which is checked.** Fuel: GAP-141 (gate enforced 14 patterns, rule named 5), GAP-058 (794 `audited: true`, no ledger row), GAP-076 (fused destructiveness hardcoded away from its registry), GAP-152 (doc comment asserting unestablished wiring). KISS: three dtype tables with one linted; `kiss_trace` printing orphaned tests informationally while §6.1-0003 calls an orphan a build-fail. **Fix: DERIVE the duplicates from the SSOT — drift becomes unrepresentable.** **PATTERN B — an instrument whose failure mode is indistinguishable from success.** KISS: `discover_tests` keying by bare test name tree-wide, so same-named tests silently overwrite and **the instrument under-reports itself**. Fuel (GAP-149's whole lineage): a coverage scanner that goes blind reports FULL coverage; three `--workspace` aborts each reporting `E0004: 0` with jobs queued; a fixture `Ok(())` mapping to **0.0 rel_err — "perfect"**; a metric whose maximum grows with sample count reporting "8% wrong". **Fix is NOT derivation — it is asserting the instrument DISCRIMINATES:** derived set non-trivial, predicate selects neither all nor none, and **where the corpus cannot exhibit the phenomenon, discrimination established on CONSTRUCTED inputs.** **Why the distinction is load-bearing: both end in "the check passed and shouldn't have", but A is about WHERE A FACT LIVES and B is about WHETHER A MEASUREMENT CAN SEE.** A clause saying *derive duplicates from the SSOT* **does nothing for a lossy key collision** — that is not a duplicated fact. **Under one heading, implementers derive their tables, believe they are done, and keep shipping instruments that cannot fail.** Proposed shape: **one RFC, shared motivation and six worked instances, TWO normative clauses with separate conformance tests.** **OPEN QUESTION PUT TO THE KISS ARCHITECT RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** does Pattern B belong in KISS-Conform at all, or is it a **testing-discipline** rule outside a wire-format standard's remit? Fuel enforces it internally with no standard requiring it. **If B is out of scope, A alone still carries the RFC.** | OPEN — drafting Fuel's half |
| GAP-157 | Fuel's test suite — tests that return early at run time and still report `ok` | B | **33 runtime-skip sites across 22 of Fuel's 78 test files (28%), plus 56 more in in-module `#[test]` code. A test that returns early PASSES, so every one of these is a green that may have asserted nothing — and no coverage mechanism can see it.** Method, stated so it can be re-run or refuted: `git grep -cE 'eprintln!\("[^"]*(SKIP\|skipping)\|println!\("[^"]*(SKIP\|skipping)'` over `*/tests/*.rs` at `origin/main`, positive control 78 test files total. **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT'S FIRST SWEEP FOUND 5 AND WAS WRONG BY 6.6x, FOR TODAY'S RECURRING REASON: THE PATTERN WAS NARROWER THAN THE PHENOMENON.** It searched `eprintln!("SKIP` (uppercase, colon form) while the common Fuel idiom is lowercase `"…; skipping: {e:?}"`. **Same defect as `Gelu\b` excluding `GeluErf`, and as scoping a `DType::` grep to six crates — a search that returns hits and is still wrong by construction.** Reported to KISS with the correction attached, since the number was going into a cross-project audit. **THE SHARPEST INSTANCE IS NOT THE BARE ONES — it is `fuel-parallel/tests/tri_backend_device_group_live.rs`, where the author UNDERSTOOD THE FAILURE COMPLETELY AND WROTE IT DOWN:** *"an early return from a `#[test]` is a **pass** … no line begins `SKIP:`"*. **They named the exact discriminator in the module doc, and it is enforced by a human being remembering to grep stderr.** The tests are also `#[ignore]`-gated with reasons, so the *harness* gate is declared — **the runtime skip inside it is not.** **That instance forecloses the comfortable reading of every other one: "they did not think of it." They did.** **DOCUMENTATION OF A HAZARD IS NOT A CONTROL FOR IT** — with a witness. **And the local prose solution was not laziness: there was nowhere to put a declaration.** KISS's proposed primitive (#140) — **a test that can decline to run MUST declare that it can, discoverably** — is precisely the mechanism this author wished for and could not express. **⚠️ A THIRD AXIS — *SILENT PARTIAL DEGRADATION* — AND TWO OF FUEL'S FIVE SITES ARE THE CLAUSE-COVERAGE GATES THEMSELVES.** Found by KISS in their own tree and swept here: `let Ok(rd) = read_dir(dir) else { return }` on a **discovery/enumeration** step. **Invisible to BOTH earlier methods** — it prints nothing (no skip-string to grep) and is not inside a `#[test]` fn (test-level enumeration misses it) — and the failure is not a skip at all but an **empty or truncated sweep result**. **Fuel sites (positive control: 14 `read_dir` call sites total):** `fuel-dispatch/tests/fkc_clause_coverage.rs:150` (`else { return }`), `fuel-dispatch/tests/fkc_prose_clause_coverage.rs:122` (**`else { continue }`** — worse: a failed subdirectory is skipped and the sweep CARRIES ON), `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/validate.rs:2161`, and two in `fuel-mkl-cpu-backend/src/dll_path.rs`. **The first two are the artifacts built THIS MORNING to prove every FKC clause is cited by a test** — so, exactly as in KISS's twin: **a function written to mechanize a hand-maintained claim, which silently truncates its own corpus and becomes the unchecked claim it exists to prevent.** **⚠️ AND IT EXPOSES A HOLE IN THE RULE THE ARCHITECT HAS BEEN MANDATING ALL DAY.** GAP-036 and GAP-077 were both required to **assert the derived set is non-trivial and that the predicate discriminates.** Those gates do. **It catches TOTAL failure and is SILENT on PARTIAL failure:** empty sweep -> minimum-count trips; sweep that reads 9 of 10 directories -> count still above minimum -> **green, with 10% of the corpus invisible.** **So the rule needs a THIRD clause: non-trivial, discriminating, and COMPLETE — the enumeration must be able to detect that it enumerated less than everything.** For a directory sweep the fix is small and total: **propagate the error instead of `return`/`continue`. A discovery step that can fail should FAIL, not SHRINK.** The general case is harder and belongs in the RFC as a stated limit rather than a clause. **Fuel's total-failure catch is DESIGNED where KISS's is INCIDENTAL** (a staleness assert that happens to require non-empty) — **the only difference between the two, and the smaller half. Neither catches partial.** **BOUNDARY OF PATTERN B, established by kiss-ref's audited ZERO and worth its own line in the RFC: the pattern requires an ENVIRONMENT DEPENDENCY to exist. A component whose tests take no environment input is IMMUNE, not lucky.** Their zero is a *positive result* — 364 tests run, 4 `#[ignore]`d and accounted for — not an unfalsified absence. **A document that cannot say where a pattern STOPS is overclaiming.** **SCOPED 2026-08-08 — the row's "blocked on KISS ratifying a convention" was TOO COARSE. Most of it is blocked; a real subset is not, and the split is the first deliverable.** **(a) `#[ignore]`-gated AND runtime-skipping — FIXABLE NOW, no convention needed.** The `#[ignore]` **is** the declared gate: a human had to pass `--ignored` to run it at all. **So the runtime skip is redundant, and worse — you asked for this test explicitly and it decided not to run.** Those should **fail hard**, not skip. Local reasoning. **Start with `fuel-parallel/tests/tri_backend_device_group_live.rs`**, whose module doc already names the discriminator (*"no line begins `SKIP:`"*) and enforces it by **a human remembering to grep stderr** — mechanizing that one converts the best-documented instance in the RFC from prose into a control. **(b) NOT `#[ignore]`d and runtime-skipping — genuinely blocked.** These pass silently in an ordinary `cargo test`, and that is the case needing the declarable-gate convention KISS is landing (#141 + proposed §6.1-0009). **Inventory and classify; do NOT invent a private idiom** — a per-project skip convention is the same defect one level up. **METHOD REQUIREMENT, from the architect's own failure on this row: ENUMERATE THE POPULATION, do not grep for the strings people happened to use.** The first sweep was wrong by **6.6x** because the pattern was narrower than the phenomenon. The population is *every `#[test]` that can reach a `return` before an assert*. **If that cannot be enumerated, saying so IS a valid deliverable** under GAP-159's standing check — *"I could not enumerate it; here is what I swept for"* beats a number whose scope is unstated. **ENUMERATED 2026-08-08 BY CONTROL FLOW — THE POPULATION IS ~435, NOT 33. The string sweep was wrong by ~13x, and the reason is the finding.** The skips are **silent `else { return }` after a shared device-guard helper** (`dev_or_skip()` / `cuda_or_skip()` / `backend_or_skip()` = `Device::new().ok()`) **with no printed message** — so a string sweep sees **none** of them. `cuda_dispatch_live` alone has **61**; `vulkan_dispatch_live` has **196**. **The strings were never the population; they were an artifact of which authors happened to LOG.** That retires the "grep was too narrow" framing used earlier in this row: **the pattern was not narrow, it was the wrong KIND of thing** — the same defect as the macro-argument miss (GAP-016(b)) and `Gelu\b`, at its largest scale. **THE SPLIT: (a) `#[ignore]`d AND skipping = 412** (375 `fuel-dispatch` + 37 across `fuel-core`/cuda/vulkan/parallel), uniform idiom, asserts-after-guard. **(b) NOT `#[ignore]`d AND skipping = 23** (all `fuel-core`: `phase6b_cuda`, `phase_c_rotating_kv`, `write_slice_doff`, `cuda_composed_bisect`, plus the `phase7b_mkl`/`aocl` anchors). **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT'S CLAIM ABOUT THE mkl/aocl ANCHORS WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN.** It read: *they run in every ordinary `cargo test`, take the skip branch every time, and have almost certainly never executed an assertion on this machine.* **They are `#![cfg(feature = …)]`-gated and do not compile at all without the feature.** The architect inferred *not `#[ignore]`d* => *runs by default* from a **file-level `#[ignore]` count** — **a measurement of one gate, concluded about a different one.** **THE CORRECT AND SHARPER STATEMENT: NO skipper runs in a bare `cargo test`. Every one is already behind a DECLARED gate** — `#[ignore]` for (a), `#![cfg(feature=…)]` for (b). **The defect is that the feature-cfg is a COMPILE gate while the runtime DEVICE requirement is undeclared: under `--features cuda` you have explicitly asked for the CUDA path to be tested, and a missing device silently converts that request into nothing.** The test has **one declared gate and two real requirements** — exactly what KISS #140/#141's declarable gates address, which is why (b) is genuinely blocked and (a) is not. **RULED: (a) exemplar FIRST (`tri_backend_device_group_live.rs`), then a CONSUMER ENUMERATION, then decide the rollout.** A centralized `dev_or_skip -> hard-fail` is right in principle — *you asked for the test, it could not run, that is a failure* — **but it is a default-polarity flip across 412 tests**, the shape twice ruled to need consumers enumerated first (GAP-058's flip; `masked_fill`'s exhaustive match beating an allowlist). **The specific question: does any CI job, script or habit run `--ignored` on a box that may lack a device?** If yes, the flip converts 412 silent passes into 412 hard failures for someone who did not ask; if no consumer exists, centralize and it is clean. **One helper, one flip, 412 tests is a great fix or a great outage depending on a fact nobody has yet.** **THIRD AXIS confirmed and assigned:** `fkc_clause_coverage.rs:150`, `fkc_prose_clause_coverage.rs:122` (`else { continue }` — sweep carries on), `validate.rs:2161`. **The 3 `fuel-ir` marker/hedge/ref gates use the same `Err(_) => continue` shape but carry a REACH assertion catching TOTAL truncation — partial still slips**, which is this registry's own known limit. **`dll_path.rs`'s two `read_dir` are EXCLUDED as legitimate multi-directory probing (try-next)** — the discrimination that makes the sweep trustworthy, since a sweep flagging them would be converting everything *shaped* like the defect. **ADJACENT — VACUITY IN FUEL, found independently:** `phase7b_mkl_anchor.rs:15` and `phase7b_aocl_anchor.rs:19`'s `*_loadable_check` are `if/else eprintln` with **no assert** — **they report `ok` having verified nothing, and are not early returns at all.** That is Baracuda's *vacuity* mechanism appearing in Fuel independently, which matters because the architect had just told KISS the four-mechanism taxonomy generalizes. **SCOPE STATEMENT (GAP-159, unprompted):** integration tests **fully enumerated by control flow, bodies read**. In-module `#[cfg(test)]` skips **NOT** enumerated — string lower bound **70** (fuel-dispatch 26, fuel-core 21, …); the silent ones need per-test control-flow reading across ~15 crates and were **bounded out of this pass. A stated limit, not a covered claim.** **AND THE STALE CHECKOUT BIT A THIRD TIME:** the enumeration agents were dispatched against the **filesystem** at `C:\Projects\fuel` (stuck at `efa71c39`) while the mover's own greps used `origin/main`. **Reach and currency, independent, again.** Recovery was the right one — **bound the delta (7 test files changed since), hand-classify those against `origin/main` (none are (a)/(b) skippers), and delta-correct** rather than re-running everything. **Cross-project: the class is confirmed in 2 of 5 chain projects (Fuel, KISS); Baracuda, Vulkane, unpopped and kiss-ref are being audited.** Feeds GAP-156's Pattern B and is the RFC's lead instance. **Fuel's fix should follow whatever KISS ratifies rather than inventing a private convention** — a per-project skip idiom would be the same defect one level up. **2026-08-08 — CONSUMER FACT ESTABLISHED, ROLLOUT SIZE MEASURED, AND THE ARCHITECT'S SCOPING WAS WRONG.** **(1) THE FLIP IS SAFE, enumerated not assumed:** `ci_cuda.yaml` runs `cargo test --features cuda` **with NO `--ignored`** on an AWS g5 GPU runner, so the (a) tests are skipped by `#[ignore]` and never reached; `rust-ci.yml` runs `cargo test --workspace` with no cuda/vulkan/`--ignored` on CPU runners, so they are `cfg`'d out entirely. **Every `--ignored` invocation in the repo is a documented MANUAL GPU-box run with a device present.** So the flip changes behaviour for **exactly one caller: a device-less `--ignored` run — the case we WANT to fail.** Scope honesty required in the commit: this is **repo-scoped** and cannot see a developer's habit or a future CI job — the claim is *"no consumer in-repo; out-of-repo callers get the intended failure"*, **not "safe"**. We are not claiming zero blast radius; we are claiming **the blast is the point.** **(2) THE ARCHITECT SCOPED THIS ROW "CPU-only, no box needed" AND THAT WAS A CLAIM ABOUT THE ENUMERATION APPLIED TO THE FIX.** All 412 are `#![cfg(all(feature="cuda",feature="vulkan"))]`: **reading them needs no box; Guards flipped to `cuda_or_fail`/`vulkan_igpu_or_fail`, panic naming the requirement (*"no usable CUDA device (<err>) — required by this explicitly-run (`#[ignore]`) test; a missing device is a failure, not a skip"*), and the human-grep discriminator mechanized. **Hazard deliberately disarmed: one file hard-failing among 39 skipping neighbours READS AS A MISTAKE in a copy-paste convention** — the helpers carry a comment marking it the deliberate GAP-157 exemplar whose polarity is the intended end-state, so the next reader does not "fix" it back. **THIRD AXIS CLOSED @ `9db80430`:** all three `read_dir` sites now `unwrap_or_else(panic)`. **The mover's framing is sharper than the tip it subsumes: a floor bounds the DERIVED SET; propagate-error proves the DISCOVERY was complete.** `MIN_EXPECTED_CLAUSES` cannot tell a corpus that shrank from one that was always that size — **failing the discovery step can.** Fourth mechanism eliminated **by construction rather than by threshold**, which is the standard wanted wherever this appears. | OPEN — (a) rollout scheduled |
| GAP-158 | `fuel-cuda-backend` `storage.rs` (tuple matches) + `cast.rs` — `DType` wildcards | B | **GAP-075's fabricating-wildcard class in the ONE crate that sweep never reached — filed BEFORE the CUDA verification leg returns, deliberately, so a green leg cannot imply these sites were examined.** **The asymmetry is the row's point: a compile check answers "what BREAKS"; it cannot answer "what QUIETLY ACCEPTS."** `device.rs` and `pinned.rs` carry **exhaustive** `match dtype` blocks with no wildcard, so adding `DType::F8E5M2` produces `E0004` there and the compiler enumerates them. **`storage.rs`'s tuple matches and `cast.rs` carry WILDCARDS over `DType`, so E5M2 falls into them and is SILENTLY MISHANDLED rather than rejected — invisible to the compiler by construction.** Those two questions need different instruments and only the first is being run. **Identified textually by the GAP-097 lane, who explicitly declined to treat the scan as the answer** (*"a textual scan is exactly the instrument that has been wrong all day"*) — so treat the site list as a starting point, not a survey. **Fix follows GAP-075's ruling**: decline loudly or make the match exhaustive; **do not fabricate**. Note GAP-075 closed as complete for the sites it swept, which did not include this crate — **the row was accurate about its own scope and this is outside it**, not a reopening. **RESOLVED 2026-08-08 — THE HYPOTHESIS IS FALSE. NOT-A-DEFECT, credited to `d27pmruh` via GAP-075.** Every `DType`-scrutinee wildcard in `fuel-cuda-backend` is a **typed decline**: `cast.rs`'s `baracuda_dtype_tag` — the site both the architect and the mover named — is `_ => return None`; `storage.rs`'s where-kernel tuple table is `_ => None`. **E5M2 is not silently mishandled there; it declines.** **THE METHOD IS WORTH MORE THAN THE RESULT, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT IS THE REASON.** The mover's initial grep — *"DType wildcards that return a value"* — came back **EMPTY**, and **its positive control proved the pattern COULD NOT have matched a value-returning arm even if one existed.** Reporting that as *"no fabrication found"* would have been **the right conclusion reached by an instrument incapable of finding the counterexample** — a true statement with **no evidential content**, which is the failure mode nothing downstream can detect. **The replacement is now the standard shape for this class of sweep: classify EVERY wildcard arm by RHS, then check each arm's SCRUTINEE.** 59 arms: 18 `_ => None`, 15 `_ => Err/bail!`, 3 `_ => return Err(UnsupportedDtype)` (36 declines), **12 `_ => unreachable!` (panics)**, 1 `_ => false`, 1 `_ => "unknown status code"`, 2 blocks. **The scrutinee step is the one most people skip and it is what decides the row:** all 12 `unreachable!` arms match on `&cos.slice`/`&sin.slice` (`CudaStorageSlice`), **and E5M2 has no `CudaStorageSlice` variant, so it cannot construct one to reach them**; `_ => false` matches `dims()`; the blocks match `inputs.len()` and a graph-update result. **None are dtype-reachable.** Coverage control: `find` reports 46 `.rs` files, the glob covered **46/46**, and the complement contains no wildcard arms. **THE GENERAL LESSON, AND IT RETIRES AN INSTRUMENT THIS REGISTRY USED TWICE: the presence of a wildcard says NOTHING until you read what it does.** GAP-075 and GAP-158 were **both scoped off wildcard counts**; GAP-075's count was mostly real, GAP-158's was **entirely artifact**, and **nothing about the count could distinguish them.** **AND THE SYMMETRIC ERROR, NAMED:** the mover had earlier reported *"`cast.rs` silently accepts E5M2 — GAP-158 confirmed rather than predicted,"* inferred from the **compiler not firing there** — when the actual reason it did not fire is that the arm **declines**. **Not-firing is consistent with both fabricate-silently and decline-correctly, and the alarming reading was picked without reading the arm.** Same defect as that morning's empty-grep, in the **opposite direction**. ***An uninformative signal does not become informative by being read pessimistically.*** **The positive-control rule as everyone carries it is one-sided — *an empty grep is not evidence of absence* — which quietly implies the safe move is to assume the defect is there. It is not.** Absence-as-proof-of-absence and absence-as-proof-of-defect are the **same error**, and in a registry the pessimistic one is **more expensive**: a wrong OPEN row costs a lane a day *and looks responsible while doing it*. **`fuel-metal-backend` is untouched by this analysis and is now GAP-160.** | RESOLVED — NOT-A-DEFECT |

| GAP-161 | Typed declines have NO EXPIRY — correct-today becomes wrong-tomorrow, silently | A | **FOUND AS A RESIDUAL OF GAP-158's CLEAN NEGATIVE, AND IT IS THE MORE IMPORTANT HALF.** GAP-158 established that `fuel-cuda-backend` **fabricates nothing** — 36 wildcard arms decline honestly. **It did NOT establish that the declines are SEMANTICALLY RIGHT**, and the reason they are currently right is the problem: **E5M2 has no CUDA storage at all, so declining is the only correct behaviour AVAILABLE.** **That makes them correct FOR A REASON THAT EXPIRES.** The moment `F8E5M2` gains CUDA storage, **every one of those arms keeps declining** and nothing marks the transition: **the compiler will not fire** (the wildcards still typecheck), **the tests will not fire** (they decline or skip), and **the capability will simply be absent while every instrument reports health.** **Same shape as GAP-157's runtime skips — a REQUIREMENT THAT IS REAL BUT UNDECLARED — one level up.** GAP-157 is about a test whose device requirement is undeclared; this is about a **decline whose JUSTIFICATION is undeclared.** In both cases the code is locally correct and the system-level claim it supports is false. **Generalizes well beyond E5M2:** the same argument applies to every `_ => None` added because a backend lacked a capability at the time of writing. **PROPOSED MECHANISM (not yet designed — bring a design before building):** a decline should be able to name the capability whose absence justifies it, e.g. `decline_pending!(F8E5M2, reason = "no CudaStorageSlice variant")`, so that **the arrival of the capability can be made to FAIL the build or a gate** rather than silently satisfying nothing. **The KISS-side analogue is #140/#141's declarable gates**, and this should be checked against them before Fuel invents a private form — **a per-project decline idiom would be the same defect one level up**, which is the exact note already standing on GAP-157. **DO NOT convert this into a sweep-and-annotate task on sight:** the population is every decline arm in the workspace, and **GAP-158 just retired wildcard-counting as an instrument.** Scope it by *capability*, not by *arm*. **ESCALATED by the GAP-158 mover, and they are right that it is worse than filed: A STALE DECLINE DOES NOT MERELY FAIL SILENTLY — IT ACTIVELY MISDIRECTS, AND IT GETS MORE CONVINCING THE LONGER IT STANDS.** When E5M2 gains CUDA storage those arms do not become *loud* wrongness; they keep declining a dtype that now works. **The symptom is *"E5M2 is unsupported on CUDA"* — which is INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM THE TRUE STATE TODAY** — so the natural diagnosis is *"storage isn't wired yet,"* **pointing the investigator at the one thing that was just fixed.** **AND THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS A REAL DEFECT CLASS RATHER THAN HOUSEKEEPING: this is NOT a stale TODO. A TODO is inert text. These are LOAD-BEARING CONTROL FLOW whose correctness is TIME-DEPENDENT and whose expiry is invisible to the compiler, to the tests, AND to the person reading the error message.** That third one is what a TODO can never do — **a stale comment misleads a reader who happens to read it; a stale decline manufactures evidence for a false diagnosis.** **DESIGN 2026-08-08 (architect). The row proposed a `decline_pending!` macro; that was the wrong shape and is withdrawn.** **THE KEY OBSERVATION: an expiring decline is a CROSS-TYPE COUPLING, which is exactly why no single exhaustive match catches it.** The arm matches on `DType`; the justification is a fact about `CudaStorageSlice`. **Making the `DType` match exhaustive — this project's own first-choice remedy — does NOT help, because `DType` is not the enum that changes when the capability arrives.** The event we need to trap is *"`CudaStorageSlice` grew a variant"*, and it happens in a different file from the decline it invalidates. **That is the whole difficulty, and it is why the obvious fixes miss.** **REJECTED — `decline_pending!(F8E5M2, reason = …)`:** a macro can record the reason but **cannot observe the capability arriving**, so it produces better documentation of a hazard, which GAP-157 already establishes is not a control for it. **Strictly worse than the current state, because it would LOOK like a mechanism.** **PROPOSED — A CANARY TEST OVER A DECLARED PAIR SET, which is the cheapest thing that actually FIRES:** one list of `(backend, dtype)` pairs that decline **because unimplemented** (distinct from declining because *impossible* — that distinction is the design's real content), and a test asserting each pair still has no storage/kernel. **When someone lands the capability, the assertion INVERTS and the test goes RED, naming the pair and pointing at the decline arms to revisit.** No macro machinery, no new vocabulary, and it fails in the direction that costs a build rather than a wrong answer. **THE TWO REASONS A DECLINE EXISTS MUST BE SEPARATED, AND THIS IS THE PART TO GET RIGHT:** *impossible* (F4 has no meaningful CUDA GEMM — will never be implemented; a permanent decline, correctly a plain wildcard) versus *unimplemented* (F8E5M2 has no storage YET — a temporary decline that expires). **Today both are spelled `_ => None` and NOTHING distinguishes them, which is why the expiry is invisible.** Even without the canary, **spelling them differently is most of the value** — a reader can then tell a settled decline from a queue item. **STILL OPEN, and it is a real design question rather than a detail: does the pair set get DERIVED or DECLARED?** Declared is cheap and rots (someone adds a decline and forgets the list — the registry's own recurring defect, one level up). Derived needs a single authority answering *"does backend B support dtype D"*, which Fuel does not have as one thing today. **Recommend DECLARED for increment 1 with the canary, then derive it if a capability table ever lands** — a declared list that FIRES beats a derived one that does not exist. **Check against KISS #140/#141 declarable gates before building: a per-project decline idiom would be the same defect one level up**, which is the note already standing on GAP-157 and it applies here unchanged. **NOT ALLOCATED — bring a design review before any code.** **⚠️ RE-RULED 2026-08-12 — THE REVIEW CAME BACK AND REFUTED MY DESIGN. THE CANARY-OVER-A-DECLARED-PAIR-LIST IS WITHDRAWN; THE LANE'S SINGLE-DECISION-POINT FORM IS ADOPTED.** Recorded as a refutation rather than an evolution, because the review found the design fails the standard *I cited to reject the alternative*. **(i) MY CANARY IS THE PRE-#141 STATE.** I rejected a `decline_pending!` macro for being "better documentation of a hazard, which GAP-157 establishes is not a control" — then proposed a design that puts the decline (the arm in `storage.rs`) and its declaration (a hand list in a test) in **two separate acts**, which is exactly what KISS #141 abolished: *"the skip mechanism and the declaration are now the same act — a test cannot decline invisibly."* **I proposed the thing I would have rejected in review.** (Also: my instruction to "check against KISS #140/#141's declarable gates" MIS-CITED them — they are the KISS side of GAP-157, and no pre-existing declarable gate for expiring *capability* declines exists to borrow. The lane extracted the principle anyway and used it against the design, which is the better move than noting the mis-citation.) **(ii) THE MEASURED KILL SHOT: THE CANARY'S PREDICATE CANNOT OBSERVE THE EVENT.** `F4`/`F8E8M0`/`F6E2M3`/`F6E3M2` already exist as **raw-byte `CudaSlice<u8>`** variants (`storage.rs:134-137`), so if F8E5M2 storage arrives by that same dummy-byte route, a canary asserting "typed variant absent" **never moves.** A control that reports coverage while being structurally unable to see the event is worse than none. **(iii) MY KEY WAS ILL-TYPED.** `F4` is **present** at `transfer_to_device` (~`:2947`) and **declines** at the compute op (~`:3365`) — same dtype, opposite answers — so storage and kernel are two populations and `(backend, dtype)` cannot express it; it needs `(backend, dtype, OP)`, multiplying the hand-maintained list. **(iv) CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CLAIM: "making the `DType` match exhaustive does NOT help" IS AN OVERCLAIM.** True that a `DType`-exhaustive match does not re-fire when an *existing* dtype gains storage later — but it **does** force every dtype to be classified and **does** fire on dtype-add. **It is HALF the mechanism, complementary to the expiry trap, not a failed alternative**, and as written this row would send the next reader past the half that works. **(v) THE REFRAMING THAT MAKES THIS A SMALLER ROW THAN I FILED: the distinction I called the design's real content IS ALREADY IN THE CODE — as prose the type erases.** `storage.rs:3023-3025` writes *"declines because no variant exists — NOT because the dtype lacks a representation… Distinct from F8E6M2 below, whose encoding is unauthored (GAP-045)"* and then `:3026-3039` returns the **identical** `UnsupportedDtype` for all of them. Three reasons spelled out, collapsed to one value. **GAP-161 is therefore "stop erasing a distinction the author already made," not "invent a mechanism" — and my declared list would have been the SIXTH restatement of a decision already made five times, coupled to none of them.** **ADOPTED INCREMENT 1:** `StorageStatus` + `cuda_storage_status(DType)` as the ONE storage-existence authority, wildcard-free, typed reasons (`Present` / `Impossible` / `UnimplementedYet` / `UndefinedEncoding`); gate **`transfer_to_device` only** — the one site whose present-set genuinely equals storage existence; at the other sites single-source **the REASON** via a shared helper. **⚠️ THE LANE CAUGHT A WIDENING-BY-REMEDY I HAD APPROVED: `zeros`/`alloc_uninit`/`rand_*` decline the dummy-byte types as an OP decline layered on top of storage existence (you can transfer existing dummy-byte storage but not zero-init it), so gating those whole sites on storage-status would have wrongly ALLOWED zeros for dummy types — and no current test asks for that, so a green suite would not have caught it.** **ARCHITECT AMENDMENTS: (1) the `StorageStatus` TYPE lives in a SHARED home from day one** (`fuel-ir` — a fact about a `DType`, zero new dep edges) **even though only CUDA has a status fn in increment 1**: per-backend *functions* should differ, the *type* and the reason-is-typed rule must not, or Vulkan and CPU each invent their own and it becomes three copies instead of one type-move ([[fix-the-generator-not-the-output]] applied to the remedy). **(2) ⚠️ TWO AXES, NOT ONE ENUM (KISS architect's caution, and it supersedes my own "match lane A's reason strings" nudge, which pushed toward the collapse): *WHY* a decline exists (`Impossible`/`UnimplementedYet`/`UndefinedEncoding` — only the middle one EXPIRES) is a different axis from *WHAT KIND* it is (`reserved`/`recognized-but-unsupported`/`unknown` — a fact about vocabulary membership). `f8e4m3fnuz` is *reserved* in kind AND *expiring* in why; `F4` on a CUDA GEMM is *recognized-but-unsupported* in kind and *Impossible* in why. They COMBINE; they do not collapse. Merging them yields a taxonomy that answers neither cleanly — wanting a `Reserved` variant inside `StorageStatus` IS the collapse happening.** **(3) STATED LIMIT, recorded in the doc comment on the function and not only in a message: without a macro co-generating the `CudaStorageSlice` variants and the `Present` arms from one list, someone can wire F8E5M2 storage via a non-gated site without flipping the central classification. Strong, not airtight; the macro form is increment-2+.** Gates: both sabotages with confirmed recompilation, and the consistency canary must be SHOWN CAPABLE OF FAILING ("near-tautological, one place" is its virtue and its risk). **THE PRECISE NAME FOR THIS DEFECT, from the KISS architect 2026-08-12, and it is better than the one this row has been using: THE REASON→VALUE MAPPING IS NOT INJECTIVE.** Three distinct reasons at `storage.rs:3023-3039` return the **identical** `UnsupportedDtype`. **"Reasons erased into prose" describes the symptom; "not injective" names the instrument that catches it.** **AND IT PLACES THIS ROW IN A CLASS WITH A KNOWN CONSEQUENCE: exhaustiveness (`E0004`) buys COMPLETENESS — a new variant falling through a catch-all is loud once you look — while TWO VARIANTS MAPPED TO ONE OUTPUT IS SILENT ALL THE WAY DOWN: green build, complete report, and a consumer that AGREES WITH A REFERENCE FOR THE WRONG REASON. Strictly worse than a disagreement, because a disagreement gets investigated and a false agreement gets FILED.** **⚠️ SCOPE THE DEMAND, because a universal injectivity rule would be wrong: INJECTIVITY IS MANDATORY WHERE THE OUTPUT IS AN IDENTITY, OPTIONAL WHERE IT IS A CLASSIFICATION.** `sk4_token` is an identity (a collision means two operand shapes share a `structure_key`); **`map_element_kind` is a classification — two Fuel dtypes legitimately COULD map to one backend kind if the backend does not distinguish them, and forbidding that would encode a false constraint.** So `StorageStatus`'s reasons must be distinguishable **because a consumer branches on them**, not because distinctness is a general virtue. **CROSS-PROJECT: the same gap exists in the KISS reference — `decline_wire()` is exhaustive over all 20 `KeyDecline` variants with no catch-all and there is NO test asserting the 20 output strings are distinct.** Their Lane A is adding an injectivity test with a pinned count and a seeded-duplicate proof. **Fuel's `sk4_token` injectivity turns out to be covered INCIDENTALLY** — a token `BTreeSet` of cardinality 14 asserted against a dtype population of 14 — **but nothing names it as an injectivity check, so a refactor can delete the coverage unknowingly. Name the property the assertion protects.** **⚠️ NAME CORRECTED AGAIN, AND THIS ONE IS RIGHT: NOT "non-injective" — A DISTINCTION STATED IN PROSE MUST BE CARRIED IN THE TYPE** (KISS architect, KISS #167, third case). **A decline reason is neither an identity nor a classification: many causes legitimately share one verdict.** What is illegitimate is that **the author knew the distinctions, WROTE THEM DOWN, and shipped an interface that cannot express them — the comment claims a resolution the type does not have.** **THE GREPPABLE INSTRUMENT, RUN ON FUEL 2026-08-12: comments matching `distinct from` / `NOT because` / `as opposed to` / `unlike` in production Rust = 137 candidates; narrowed to within 5 lines of a decline return = 6 hits / 5 distinct sites; POSITIVE CONTROL (`storage.rs:3027`) SURVIVES the narrowing; 96% exclusion (discriminating, not eating the population). ⚠️ THE 5-LINE WINDOW IS A LOWER BOUND — this row's own arms span ~15 lines, so a comment preceding a long match can have its collapse out of reach.** **⚠️⚠️ AND READING THE ONE HIT THAT LOOKED LIKE A SECOND INSTANCE PRODUCED A REFINEMENT THAT STOPS THE INSTRUMENT FLAGGING CORRECT CODE: `fuel-metal-backend/src/storage.rs:128` joins SIX dtypes into one decline arm and IS CORRECT.** Its comment anticipates the objection — *"the arm below is dtype-parameterised, so joining it names the dtype and asserts nothing false about the format"* — and `Err(UnsupportedDTypeForOp(self.dtype, op))` really is parameterised, so six joined arms yield six distinct values. **SO "A DISTINCTION IN PROSE + A SINGLE RETURN" IS NOT THE TEST. THE TEST IS: IS THE *DIMENSION THE COMMENT DISTINGUISHES* CARRIED AS A FIELD IN THE RETURNED VALUE?** Measured: CUDA's `UnsupportedDtype { dtype: DType, op: &'static str }` and Metal's `UnsupportedDTypeForOp(dtype, op)` **both carry `dtype` and `op`; NEITHER carries a reason** — and this row's comment distinguishes **why** (unimplemented-yet / undefined-encoding / impossible). **The defect is exactly: the distinguished dimension has no field, while an undistinguished one does.** **LOSSY-AND-HONEST IS FINE (Metal claims only that it asserts nothing false, the weaker and correct claim); LOSSY-WHILE-ASSERTING-OTHERWISE IS THE DEFECT.** **CROSS-PROJECT, unhedged by them: the KISS reference has the adjacent gap — `decline_wire()` exhaustive over 20 `KeyDecline` variants with NO test that the 20 output strings are distinct.** Fuel's `sk4_token` injectivity is covered but **INCIDENTALLY** — a token `BTreeSet` of cardinality 14 asserted against a dtype population of 14 — **an assertion simultaneously load-bearing and INVISIBLE, so naming it is the entire fix: there is no code to write, only a name to attach.** **INCREMENT 1 LANDED `625c6b4d`** — `StorageStatus` in `fuel-ir` (zero new dep edges, re-verified on current `origin/main` rather than the lane's base: all four backends already dep `fuel-ir`) + `cuda_storage_status` as the ONE storage-existence authority, wildcard-free, typed reasons; phase 1 `storage_status` unit tests **6 passed / 0 failed** with artifact `Compiling fuel-cuda-backend` and a `GAP161_ALL_GREEN` terminal marker; phase 2 `--all-targets --no-run` finished in 11.41s, **so the new `CudaError` variant broke no exhaustive match anywhere.** Re-verified on the MERGED tree after a `fuel-ir` doc change forced a `fuel-cuda-backend` recompile — *clean rebase is not compatibility*, applied unprompted. **BOTH SABOTAGES RED-CONFIRMED, AND IN THE DISCRIMINATING FORM — each reddened EXACTLY the intended tests with the others staying green** (a sabotage that reddens everything proves the suite runs, not that the guard works): **(1)** marking `F32` — which HAS a constructor — `UnimplementedYet` reddened exactly `present_set_is_exactly_the_storage_backed_dtypes` and `storage_unavailable_on_a_present_dtype_is_a_loud_error`, other 4 green; Both `exit 101` **with a DONE marker present**, distinguishing *finished-failing* from *killed* — the distinction that cost two builds tonight to learn. **⚠️ SCOPE ON SABOTAGE-2, RECORDED RATHER THAN CLOSED, BECAUSE "SABOTAGE-VALIDATED" WOULD OTHERWISE BE READ AS COVERING MORE THAN IT DOES: THE MECHANISM IS SABOTAGE-VALIDATED; THE FOUR SITES' LIVE RUNTIME PATHS ARE NOT.** They are device-gated, so no pure test exercises them. **What IS established about the sites: (a) phase-2 `--all-targets` COMPILED them calling `storage_unavailable(dt, "op")` and the old hardcoded errors are GONE — so they demonstrably route through the tested mechanism, proven by the COMPILER rather than an assertion; (b) sabotage-1 corrupts the exact classification `transfer_to_device`'s gate derives from, transitively covering that repoint.** **ARCHITECT RULED AGAINST closing the gap with a live GPU test, and the reason generalises: it would add a FRAGILE Display-through-`fuel_ir::Error` assertion, and a fragile assertion is a LIABILITY not coverage — it breaks on unrelated message edits, gets weakened by whoever hits it next, then reads as coverage while asserting almost nothing. | GAP-162 | (B) the shared device-guard helper — the refactor that removes GAP-157's reproduction mechanism | B | **SCHEDULED (fresh session — a 40-file refactor at the tail of a long day is the half-migrated-trait shape to avoid). Filed in the mover's words; the FACTS live on GAP-157, this row is the DESIGN, which is the non-mechanical part.** **GATING SHAPE — THE ONE PLACE (B) CAN GO WRONG:** the shared helper must be **ABSENT when its feature is off, never STUBBED to a no-op.** A test-support module (shared `tests/common/mod.rs` per live-test crate, or a small always-compiled `fuel-test-support` crate) exposing `cuda_dev_or_fail() -> CudaDevice` under `#[cfg(feature="cuda")]` and `vulkan_backend_or_fail() -> VulkanBackend` under `#[cfg(feature="vulkan")]`, **each PANICKING on a missing device — never returning `Option`, so there is no silent-`None` path to inherit.** **FORBIDDEN BY CONSTRUCTION: a helper behind `#[cfg(any(cuda,vulkan))]` that on a plain build resolves to an empty/no-op stub — that is the gap eating its own fix.** A shared decision point that silently compiles out is **GAP-157's undeclared requirement at the fixture layer**, and **GAP-161's decline-whose-justification-expires one level up** — the same class. **Rule: NO no-feature arm. A miswired caller (references `cuda_dev_or_fail` without `--features cuda`) must be a COMPILE ERROR (fn absent), not a silent no-op** — caller's `#![cfg]` and helper's `#[cfg]` then match by construction. **REPOINT ORDER:** one crate at a time (`fuel-dispatch` -> `fuel-core` -> `fuel-cuda-backend` tests + the 5 in-module `baracuda/` copies -> `fuel-vulkan-backend` -> `fuel-parallel`), each verified under its feature build, **reusing the warm baracuda cache from the tri_backend exemplar build (no cold `ptxas` re-pay).** **`tri_backend` stays FILE-LOCAL** — its Vulkan iGPU-by-name / refuse-NVIDIA selection is unique and unshareable; the shared helper covers only the ~45 identical `dev_or_skip`/`backend_or_skip` copies. **WHAT VERIFIES IT — THREE CHECKS, BECAUSE POLARITY AND GATING ARE SEPARATE RISKS: (1)** each converted file compiles under its feature (mechanical, cache-warm); **(2)** the shared helper is **ABSENT, not stubbed**, with no features — a `#[cfg(not(any(feature="cuda",feature="vulkan")))]` **`compile_fail`** check that referencing it without the feature does **not** compile; **(3)** polarity is intended — a device-less `--ignored` run **FAILS**. **Check (2) is the one most refactors would omit, and it is the only one that tests the gating rather than the mechanics.** **HALF-MIGRATION MARKER:** the `tri_backend` exemplar comment **stays until** the shared helper is the single source and all 40 files use it — so the intermediate state is self-describing rather than looking like an inconsistency. | SCHEDULED |
| GAP-163 | A STANDING aarch64 cross-check — the gate that would have caught GAP-160's `I8` gap in May | P | **ASSIGNED to `bzfevnl6`. This is the fix for the CLASS, not for Metal: `DType::I8` went uncovered for ~3 months for exactly one reason — NOTHING COMPILES THAT CODE.** A check that compiles it is what would have caught it on day one. **THE CAPABILITY, discovered 2026-08-08 while disproving GAP-160's premise:** `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin` on this x86 Windows box **compiles the entire aarch64 dependency chain** — `fuel-cpu-kernels` (17 warnings, `neon.rs` x21 sites), `fuel-quantized` (4, `neon.rs` x5), `fuel-cpu-backend` (2). **These are NEON code paths this machine has NEVER compiled, and they are now compiler-adjudicated for free: no GPU, no Mac, no CI, no device.** **SCOPE IT TO THE CRATES THAT ARE GREEN TODAY, AND EXPLICITLY EXCLUDE `fuel-metal-backend`** — it is 26 errors deep (GAP-160), and **a standing gate that is RED ON ARRIVAL gets disabled within a week**, which is worse than not having one. Excluding it is a declared limit, not an oversight. **DELIVERABLE: a runnable check plus its documented invocation — and, applying GAP-160's own rule to this gate, A STATEMENT OF WHICH PHASES IT REACHES.** If it only reaches type-check on those crates, the gate must say so; **a gate that does not declare its phase coverage is the same defect it exists to catch.** **CHASE THE ONE SUBSTANTIVE WARNING SPECIFICALLY: `falling back to 'f32' as the trait bound 'f32: From<f64>' is not satisfied`.** *"The numeric path silently degrades on aarch64"* would be a real defect **hiding inside a warning nobody has ever read** — and unlike the other 22, it is about behaviour rather than style. **DELIVERED 2026-08-08 @ `72638aa5` — `pwsh scripts/aarch64-cross-check.ps1`.** Scoped to the three green crates, `fuel-metal-backend` excluded as directed. **The gate states its own phase coverage in BOTH directions, in its PASS message rather than only its docs:** REACHES parse / macro expansion / name resolution / type-check / **match exhaustiveness (E0004)** / borrow-check; **DOES NOT reach** monomorphization of uninstantiated generics, codegen, **LINKING**, runtime. **So it cannot say the aarch64 build links, and it cannot say the NEON kernels are numerically correct — compiling a NEON path and that path computing the right answer are different claims, and only hardware settles the second.** **TWO BUGS IN THE GATE, BOTH FOUND BY TESTING IT RATHER THAN TRUSTING IT — and the second is the more valuable finding of the day.** **(1) FALSE RED ON A WARM CACHE:** the first draft grepped for `Checking <crate>` lines, and **cargo does not reprint those when every unit is fresh**, so the gate failed on a cached run. **That is the architect's own red-on-arrival warning arriving from an unanticipated direction — a gate nobody trusts, via a mechanism unrelated to the code under test.** Now parses `--message-format json` for `compiler-artifact`. **(2) RIGHT VERDICT, WRONG DIAGNOSIS:** a **failed** compile and a **never-attempted** compile both produce no artifact, so checking artifacts first reported a genuine `E0004` in `fuel-quantized` as *"the compiler did not reach these crates"* — **true about artifacts, and a wrong diagnosis pointing the reader at the harness instead of at their own code.** Cargo's exit is now checked first. ***THE RULE THIS ESTABLISHES, IN THE MOVER'S WORDS: the sabotage's job is usually to prove the gate goes RED. Here it went red for the RIGHT REASON and said the WRONG THING — which a pass/fail-only check could never surface. VALIDATING A GATE MEANS READING ITS MESSAGE, NOT JUST ITS EXIT CODE.*** Both directions validated: planted non-exhaustive `DType` match -> exit 101, `E0004 seen: 6`; reverted -> exit 0. **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT'S `f32: From<f64>` HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN.** It was called *"the one substantive item… behaviour rather than style"* **from the warning STRING, without checking whether it was aarch64-specific.** **It is not — it appears identically on x86**, in a log already on hand. The macro instantiates at `f32` and `f64`; for `f32` rustc falls back and produces the **correct value**. **It is a future-compat lint, not silent numeric degradation.** *"The numeric path silently degrades on aarch64"* would have been a real defect had it been true — **and it is exactly the sort of claim that becomes fact once it is in a registry.** The mover tested before chasing, which is the only reason it did not land here as one. Fixed anyway (`1.0_f32` satisfies both instantiations); **the change is to the lint, not the math.** **THE GATE JUSTIFIED ITSELF ON DAY ONE: 21 x `E0133` `unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn` warnings** (`rust_2024_compatibility`) across the NEON paths — **real future-compat debt in code this box had never compiled until today**, surfaced by the gate's first run. Out of the gate's scope to fix; carved out here as its own follow-up. **`fuel-metal-backend` JOINED THE GATE 2026-08-08 @ `cbb775d0`, after being repaired to green (GAP-160) — never before, per the red-on-arrival rule. The gate now covers FOUR crates, PASS exit 0.** And it carries the Metal limit in its own PASS output, not merely its docs: *"COMPILE-correct is not KERNEL-correct. No Metal hardware here and no Metal runtime test exists, so this says its types line up — NOT that any shader computes the right answer."* **Read a PASS as "the rot is gone", never as "Metal is supported."** | DELIVERED |
| GAP-164 | `.cargo/config.toml` — `[build] rustflags = ["-C", "target-cpu=native"]` leaks the HOST cpu into every CROSS-compile | P | **Found by the GAP-163 gate on its first run, and reported rather than fixed because it is a repo-wide codegen-flag decision affecting every lane's builds, not a lane's call.** `[build] rustflags` applies to **all** targets, so an aarch64 cross-check is handed the host's `znver4`, producing **~20 KB of "not a recognized processor/feature" chatter that would BURY any real diagnostic.** Ignored by LLVM and harmless to correctness — **but a host cpu-tuning flag is meaningless for a cross-target, and burying diagnostics is how a gate stops being read.** The gate overrides it for its own target; **the repo-wide question is open.** **THE OBVIOUS FIX HAS A SUBTLETY THAT MUST NOT BE SKIPPED — this is why it is not a one-line change.** Moving to `[target.x86_64-pc-windows-msvc] rustflags` host-scopes it, and cargo does **not merge** the two: when a `[target.<triple>]` entry matches, `[build] rustflags` is **ignored entirely**, so the host build sees the same flags and the cross build sees none. **BUT `[target.<triple>].rustflags` does NOT apply to build scripts or proc-macros** (which compile for the host), whereas `[build].rustflags` **does**. **So the move is behaviour-preserving for the crate graph and a REAL CHANGE for build scripts** — they would lose `target-cpu=native`. **That is almost certainly fine and possibly desirable, but it is a codegen change to every build script in the workspace and it must be stated, not discovered.** **~~RECOMMENDATION: host-scope it, and say in the commit that build scripts lose `target-cpu=native`.~~ SUPERSEDED — the real config made this both smaller and different, and the recommendation was written without reading it.** **FIXED 2026-08-08 by the architect. The file ALREADY uses per-target overrides TWICE** (`wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `x86_64-apple-darwin`) — **so `aarch64-apple-darwin` was not a design flaw needing `[build]` restructured, it was a MISSING ENTRY in a pattern the file already establishes.** Adding one touches no other target, no host build, and no build script — **strictly safer than the host-scoping move recommended above, which would have changed build-script codegen flags for a problem that didn't require it.** **⚠️ AND THE FIRST FIX WAS A NO-OP THAT LOOKED LIKE A FIX — caught ONLY by measuring it.** `rustflags = []` **does NOT displace `[build] rustflags`: cargo treats an empty list as UNSET and falls back**, so the host flag returns silently. Measured with the empty override in place and correctly on disk: **still 4816 `not a recognized processor` lines.** The entry must be **non-empty** to displace. **A config edit that lands correctly, reads correctly, and does nothing is exactly the class this registry keeps recording — and the only reason it did not ship is that the change was re-measured instead of reasoned about.** **SHIPPED: `[target.aarch64-apple-darwin] rustflags = ["-C", "target-cpu=generic"]`.** `generic` rather than a specific Apple core because **this target is type-checked and never executed, so any tuning choice is unverifiable** — while `native` is an outright lie, naming an x86 core to an ARM target. **NEON is baseline-mandatory in ARMv8, so `generic` still compiles the NEON paths the gate exists to reach.** **RESULT: 4816 -> 0 chatter lines; total log 5083 -> 292.** `[build]` now carries a comment stating that any NEW cross-target needs its own entry or it silently inherits the host flag. | FIXED |
| GAP-165 | T5 / Whisper — encoder-decoder decode is a DIFFERENT contract from `DecodeModel` | C | **A TRACKED SUB-SCOPE OF GAP-029, NOT A DECLINE. CireSnave scoped GAP-029 to all 11 families as one lane, so this must read the way GAP-078/098 read: carved out, numbered, owned, VISIBLE.** Ruled 2026-08-08 on the gap-029 lane's recommendation. **THE REASON, stated so the carve-out is defensible rather than convenient: cross-attention KV has DIFFERENT LIFETIME SEMANTICS.** Self-attention KV is **appended every decode step**; cross-attention KV is **computed ONCE from the encoder and then read-only for the entire generation.** **A trait method meaning *"extend the cache with this step's keys"* has NO MEANING for cross-attn — that is not a parameter difference, it is a different contract.** Folding it into `DecodeModel` would either **distort those methods for the 9 decoder-only families** or push encoder-decoder through a shape that lies about what it does. **Neither is better than a second row.** **SEQUENCED AFTER the 7-family trait lands** (GAP-029), because the decoder-only contract is the thing this one has to be designed *against* — designing both at once means designing the shared part twice with no evidence. | OPEN — sub-scope of GAP-029 |
| GAP-166 | `fuel-inference/src/multi_session.rs:77` — `DecodeModel::n_kv_heads()` / `head_dim()` vs `fuel-core/src/lazy_deepseek2.rs:563` | C | **`DecodeModel`'s geometry methods ASSERT a KV cache shape that MLA does not have — and the failure is SHIPPED, not prospective.** Found by the GAP-029 lane 2026-08-12 while designing the trait *against* the describe-do-not-assert constraint; they found the constraint already violated on `main` in the present tense. **Filed as its own row rather than a line on GAP-098 because GAP-098 is *LFM2 is not built*, and this is *a shipped trait invites a wrong allocation* — different defects.** **MEASURED (all verified by the architect against `origin/main`, not relayed):** the trait requires `n_kv_heads()` and `head_dim()`, both documented *"(cache geometry)"*. `DeepSeek2Model` is decode-capable **today** — `decode_shape_key` (`lazy_deepseek2.rs:499`), `rebind_and_realize_prebuilt_mla` (`:1373`) — and has neither, because MLA's decode state is a `LatentCache`, not a `KvCache`: per layer, slot 0 is the post-norm compressed latent trailing `[kv_lora_rank]`, slot 1 the post-RoPE `k_pe`. **TWO INDEPENDENT AXES OF MISMATCH, and the second is the one that gets missed because it is not about geometry at all: (1) STATE KIND** (`LatentCache` vs `KvCache`); **(2) OWNERSHIP SHAPE** — `forward_with_latent_cache(&self, tokens, cache: LatentCache) -> Result<(Tensor, LatentCache)>` threads the cache **BY VALUE AND RETURNS IT**, where the trait takes `&mut KvCache`. **⚠️ THE SHARP PART IS NOT THAT DEEPSEEK2 CANNOT IMPLEMENT THE TRAIT — IT IS THAT IT CAN, AND WRONGLY.** `DeepSeek2Config` has `num_attention_heads` (`:70`) and `v_head_dim` (`:90`), so **both methods are syntactically returnable.** *(FACT: the fields exist, verified. CONSEQUENCE, which is REASONING FROM THE TRAIT'S STATED CONTRACT AND WAS NOT RUN: a scheduler that trusts them allocates a standard `KvCache` of `[n_kv_heads, head_dim] x n_layers` for a model whose decode path never reads a `KvCache` at all — it type-checks, it runs, and it allocates the wrong state.* The lane marked that split itself; it is preserved rather than smoothed over.)* **This is the LFM2 failure mode GAP-098 flags as prospective, except already shipped.** **WHERE THE CONFLATION LIVES — ONE SENTENCE, and it is the generalisable part.** `n_layers`' doc says the geometry is *"the KV cache geometry the scheduler builds a session's private cache against (**all sessions share one model, so this is uniform**)"*. That parenthetical is a **TRUE justification for uniformity ACROSS SESSIONS** — and the methods then use it to assert uniformity **ACROSS LAYERS and ACROSS STATE KINDS**, which it does not establish. **A true reason attached to a wider claim than it supports: the citation is real, so the claim reads as sourced, and the scope gap is invisible unless you ask what the reason actually licenses.** See CLAUDE.md. **SCOPE: DeepSeek2 is OUTSIDE GAP-029's 11** (positive-controlled — the 11 `lazy_quantized_*.rs` files are gemma3, glm4, lfm2, llama, phi3, qwen2, qwen3, qwen3_moe, smollm3, t5, whisper; there is no `lazy_quantized_deepseek2.rs`). **NOT a port and NOT a widening of GAP-029.** **FALSIFICATION ATTEMPT RUN 2026-08-12 (GAP-029 lane, spec §3.3 @ `6c6e64aa`) — IT SPLIT, AND NEITHER HALF WAS THE EXPECTED ANSWER. The value of the exercise is that it did not come back "yes it fits" on both axes, which would have been the same artifact as not having tried.** **AXIS 1 (state kind) — EXPRESSIBLE, and the vocabulary DID NOT HAVE TO BE INVENTED: it is shipped TWICE.** `LatentCache::new(…, slot_trailing: Vec<Vec<usize>>, …)` (`lazy_latent_cache.rs:74`, readers `n_slots()` `:226` / `slot_trailing(slot)` `:228`) and `LatentKvCache::with_capacity(…, slot_trailing, …)` (`inference_context.rs:651`) — the device-resident twin, **in the same file as `KvCache`**. Concretely: KV layer = `vec![vec![n_kv_heads, head_dim]; 2]`, MLA layer = `vec![vec![kv_lora_rank], vec![qk_rope_head_dim]]`. **So `KvCache` is the 2-slot SPECIAL CASE, and `(n_kv_heads, head_dim)` is not a more primitive fact than `slot_trailing` — it is one inhabitant of it.** That upgrades the describing spelling from *defensible generality* to *the generalization the tree already made twice and `DecodeModel` never adopted.* **⚠️ AND THE CATCH, WHICH IS WHY ATTEMPTING BEATS CONCLUDING: `slot_trailing` is ONE list applied to EVERY layer** (`new()` loops `for _ in 0..n_layers`). **It describes state KIND generically while still ASSERTING uniformity across LAYERS — adopted as-is it buys one of the two dimensions and FEELS like both.** So the vocabulary must be `slot_trailing` **indexed by layer**. Note that lands on the independently-derived *"`layer_idx` is a real parameter, not a loop counter"* from the opposite direction. **AXIS 2 (ownership) — DOES NOT CARRY OVER, and the architect's stated REASON was wrong in both halves.** I predicted ownership would slip and guessed by-value was the stronger guarantee. Measured: **all six validation errors in `forward_with_latent_cache_impl` fire BEFORE any mutation, so MLA's all-or-nothing comes from EARLY VALIDATION — a property independent of the threading**, and by-value is carrying a persistent-lazy-rebuild idiom (cf. `advance_by(mut self) -> Self` `:200`), not an error-semantics choice. **THE REAL CONSEQUENCE, which is not obvious: under by-value, on `Err` the cache is CONSUMED AND DROPPED — so there is nothing left for `advance_batched` to route to the serial arm**, which is precisely what the batched arm's "before mutating any cache" contract relies on. `&mut Self::State` can serve MLA via `mem::replace`, **but it CHANGES MLA's error contract and must be written deliberately.** Ruled: `&mut Self::State`, **recorded as a choice with a named cost** — an inherited default and a deliberate choice are indistinguishable in the code and completely different in the next reader's hands. **BOTH FINDINGS WERE INVISIBLE TO THE EXISTING CORPUS: eight uniform-KV models satisfy either spelling, returning a clean 8-of-8 that certifies nothing** ([[control-design-corpus-traps]], [[justification-scope-mismatch]]). **UNIT A LANDED `2699fbad`. `DecodeModel::{n_kv_heads,head_dim}()` -> `layer_state_specs() -> Vec<LayerStateSpec>`; the three impls (all Llama variants) return uniform `KeyValue`. Ripple was EXACTLY ONE SIGNATURE — `SessionScheduler::new -> Result<Self>` — with all 16 callers in-file. `ModelDims`/`KvGeometry` scalar fields UNTOUCHED, so the vLLM commitment stands.** **⚠️ THE HEADLINE IS NOT THE REFACTOR: THIS FLIPS GAP-170's `collapse_uniform` FROM INERT TO LIVE.** It shipped with all four refusal cases and four refusal tests, `pub mod`-declared and **referenced nowhere outside its own file** — *a correctly-named validator guarding no path*, the pattern this registry keeps recording. **This change gives it its first real caller**, and `mla_shaped_model_is_declined_at_scheduler_construction` (watched FAILING under a temporary fabricating boundary, then passing) is the proof it is now load-bearing. Verified by the architect at head: `fuel-inference --lib` **193 passed / 0 failed**, that test included. **⚠️ ROW PHRASING CORRECTED BY THE LANE: this row said *"a shipped trait invites a wrong allocation"*. Measured — ZERO `impl DecodeModel for DeepSeek2*` (positive-controlled) — so the wrong allocation was reachable only by a FUTURE `impl`. "Shipped FOOTGUN" is exact; "shipped mis-allocation" is not.** **UNIT B (`type State`) DEFERRED — no consumer until a real MLA-into-scheduler port, a distinct feature. ⚠️ And a finding to VALIDATE when it is built rather than act on now: the row's `&mut Self::State` "named cost" was reasoned against the BY-VALUE lazy variant, but MLA already ships `forward_with_latent_kv_context_persistent(&self, …, &mut LatentKvCache, …) -> Result<…>` — already `&mut`, so on the PERSISTENT path the named cost appears NOT TO APPLY. Two true statements about different methods; not a refutation.** | **UNIT A CLOSED `2699fbad`** — geometry honest, `collapse_uniform` LIVE; **Unit B deferred behind an actual MLA port**; GAP-170's boundary is wired but not yet exercised by a shipped non-uniform model |
| GAP-167 | METHOD — a test at the WRONG SITE, not a weak test | C | **A CORRECT, SINGLE-SOURCED, TESTED, GREEN FIX CAN BE EVIDENCE ABOUT A DIFFERENT QUESTION THAN THE ONE IT IS LABELLED WITH.** Carved out of GAP-155 on the KISS architect's framing, kept as its own row because it generalises past dtypes and past KISS. **THE LINE, theirs verbatim: *"A test at the wrong site is not weaker evidence than a test at the right one; it is evidence about a different question wearing the right label."*** **THE INSTANCE:** GAP-155 was hours from landing a reserved-KISS-dtype recognition fix — single source of truth, matchable typed error, three tests — on `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs::from_str`, **a function that parses Fuel's INTERNAL token vocabulary** (`"f8e4m3"`), reachable only by Fuel's own graph-attr deserialization. The tokens it was written to recognise (`"f8e4m3fnuz"`) **cannot arrive there.** It would have gone green, and **its greenness would have been cited as KISS-Classify conformance evidence.** **HOW THIS DIFFERS FROM THE ROWS AROUND IT, which is why it is not a duplicate:** [[self-checking-has-a-ceiling]] and [[control-design-corpus-traps]] are about an instrument that **cannot observe** the property (degenerate corpus, non-discriminating predicate). **This is the adjacent failure — an instrument that works PERFECTLY and is aimed where the property does not live.** No control on the instrument's sensitivity catches it, because the instrument is fine. **THE CHECK THAT DOES CATCH IT, and it is one question: before writing the test, ask WHICH SURFACE THE OBLIGATION BINDS, and prove the trigger can REACH that surface.** For a conformance obligation: whose act is it (reader? emitter? provider?), and does the input the clause describes physically arrive at the code under test? **In this case the answer was a positive-controlled absence — Fuel has NO structure_key token reader at all (zero dtype-token parse arms vs 116 op-token arms in `fkc/lower.rs`), so the obligation had no site in Fuel to land on.** **Family: [[gap-077-flip-is-inert]] (a correct audited flip with 0 live triggers), [[enumerate-the-population-not-the-strings]] (counting the wrong construct). The shared root: the artifact was verified, and what it was verified ABOUT was assumed.** **Standing rule, now in CLAUDE.md.** | **CLOSED** — recorded as method; rule in CLAUDE.md |
| GAP-168 | `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs` — 8 of KISS sk4's 24 dtypes have NO Fuel `DType` at all | C | **CIRESNAVE'S RULING 2026-08-12 SETS THE BAR AND IT IS NOT ENUM COMPLETENESS: *comprehensive support means we can read, write, and TALK ABOUT these dtypes over the seams — AND have the code to actually USE them intelligently anywhere it makes sense. Not whether we have a `DType::<name>`, but whether we have the code to do things with it.* Plus: *declining a dtype we could easily support is acceptable ONLY as a short-term workaround until the code exists.*** So this row is a **sequenced build program**, not a compliance checklist, and a decline here is a scheduled debt rather than an answer. **MEASURED against sk4 (`origin/main:conformance/src/structure_key.rs`, `SCHEMA_VERSION = 4`, `DTYPES: [&str; 24]`): 14 of 24 have a Fuel `DType`; 2 are reserved and correctly have none (GAP-155); 8 HAVE NONE — `u16`, `u64`, `bool`, `i4`, `u4`, `b1`, `c64`, `c128`.** **⚠️ THEY ARE NOT 8 EQUIVALENT LINE ITEMS, AND REPORTING THEM AS ONE NUMBER IS THE MISTAKE TO AVOID — four different sizes of job, one of which is not a dtype addition at all:** **(a) `u16`, `u64` — MECHANICAL.** Pure additions; the measured cost is the `e5m2` ripple (~20 sites / 7 crates per dtype). No design questions. **(b) `i4`, `u4` — REUSES EXISTING MACHINERY.** KISS specifies packed-pair byte, low nibble = even index, sign- vs zero-extended on read — the layout Fuel already implements for `F4`. **(c) `bool` — A SEMANTICS DECISION, NOT PLUMBING, and it needs CireSnave's ruling because it changes an existing public surface.** Measured: **every Fuel comparison returns a `U8` MASK** (`fuel-graph/src/lib.rs:385-386`; `fuel-ir/src/dispatch.rs:222-233` *"output is a `U8` mask (`1` where equal, `0` otherwise)"*), and `grad.rs:146` records the mask as non-differentiable. Adopting `bool` decides whether `eq`/`lt`/`gt` START RETURNING `Bool`, rippling into `where`/`select`/`masked_fill` and the autodiff rule. **Adding the variant without answering that yields a dtype nothing produces.** **(d) `b1` — NEW PACKING GRANULARITY** (8/byte, LSB = lowest logical index); bounded, but nibble code does not generalise to it for free. **(e) `c64`/`c128` — NOT A DTYPE ADDITION. A PROGRAM.** Complex is not a storage width, it is a different ARITHMETIC: complex GEMM, conjugation, what a reduction means, comparisons undefined, different transcendentals — and KISS carries an `fft` op family that presumes it. **Adding the two variants would be cheap and would produce exactly what CireSnave's ruling forbids: a token that cannot compute.** Scope with the FFT work as its own program. **RECOMMENDED ORDER: `u16`/`u64` → `i4`/`u4` → `bool` (after the comparison-return ruling) → `b1` → complex as its own program.** Sequencing only — *"no consumer" is not a reason to skip a capability, but it IS a reason to sequence it behind things that have consumers.* **NOTHING STRUCTURAL BLOCKS ANY OF (a)-(d).** **⚠️ 2026-08-12 — THE EXTERNAL BACKEND VOCABULARY ALREADY NAMES SIX OF THE EIGHT, WHICH CHANGES THE SEQUENCING PICTURE. VERIFIED BY THE ARCHITECT** at `~/.cargo/registry/.../baracuda-kernel-vocab-0.0.1-alpha.78/src/element.rs:1014` — `ElementKind` carries **18** variants: `F16 Bf16 F32 F32Strict F64 S8 U8 I32 I64 Bool Fp8E4M3 Fp8E5M2 S4 U4 Bin Complex32 Complex64 U32`. Mapping onto this row's eight: **`Bool` → `bool`; `S4`/`U4` → `i4`/`u4`; `Bin` → `b1`; `Complex32`/`Complex64` → `c64`/`c128`. Only `u16` and `u64` have NO `ElementKind` at all.** So **the descriptor/seam boundary is not the wall for six of the eight.** **⚠️⚠️ AND THE CAVEAT IS THE WHOLE POINT, because it is the exact error this row exists to prevent, one remove out: THIS IS EVIDENCE ABOUT VOCABULARY, NOT CAPABILITY. | GAP-169 | `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs:52` `DType::F8E4M3` + its `"f8e4m3"` token vs sk4's `f8e4m3fn` | C | **SAME FORMAT, TWO NAMES — and the divergence is a defect rather than a translation layer, because FUEL'S OWN DEFINITION DOES NOT SAY WHICH E4M3 IT IS.** `F8E4M3`'s doc reads only *"8-bit floating point with 4-bit exponent and 3-bit mantissa"* — **no bias, no statement about infinities, no NaN encoding.** The sibling `F8E5M2` doc calls E4M3 *"the OCP-standard FP8 pair's other half"*, so the INTENT is OCP, **but the variant itself never says so.** Meanwhile `telemetry/structure_key_derive.rs:459` maps it to sk4's `f8e4m3fn`, which asserts specifically **OCP finite: bias 7, max ±448, no infinities, single NaN.** **SO THE SEAM MAKES A CLAIM THE DTYPE'S OWN DEFINITION DOES NOT BACK.** **That is precisely the ambiguity KISS DELIBERATELY ELIMINATED:** bare `e4m3` can mean OCP-finite, an inf-carrying variant, or AMD `fnuz` (different bias, no −0); sk3/sk4's rule is that **byte-incompatible hardware variants MUST NOT share a token** and *"the ambiguous bare `e4m3` is gone."* **FIX: rename to `F8E4M3FN` / `"f8e4m3fn"` and put the bias/max/inf/NaN facts in the doc.** Three reasons: **(1)** Fuel's `F8E5M2` **already** matches sk4's `f8e5m2` exactly, so the divergence exists **only** on e4m3 — an artifact, not a policy; **(2)** two vocabularies for one format is where mistakes hide — **it is WHY the GAP-155 lane nearly landed its fix in the wrong file**; **(3)** when fnuz activates (a scheduled additive schema event), the pair `F8E4M3` vs `F8E4M3FNUZ` reads as general-case-plus-variant when they are **siblings with different biases** — the `FN` has to be there. **⚠️ THE GATE THIS NEEDS, and it is the one a rename's compiler-enforcement does NOT cover: STRING LITERALS IN SERIALIZED DATA.** The compiler finds every code site; it does not find `"f8e4m3"` inside captured/replayed graph attributes on disk. **`runtime_fused.rs:339/:473` deserialize `cast_dtype` through `DType::from_str`, so an existing capture containing `"f8e4m3"` silently fails to deserialize after the rename.** Either accept the old spelling on parse (and say so) or migrate the captures — **decide before renaming, not after a replay breaks.** **RELATED, same class, settle before KISS PR-2 merges:** Fuel spells its 4-bit type bare **`f4`** (*"4-bit float (MX4 format)"*). If PR-2 lands a variant-explicit `f4e2m1` — the same logic that produced `f8e4m3fn` — Fuel respells. **⚠️ THE CASE FOR THE RENAME STRENGTHENED 2026-08-13: `F8E4M3 == OCP E4M3FN` IS NOW A SHIPPED, UNIT-TESTED IDENTITY AT **TWO** INDEPENDENT SITES — `telemetry/baracuda_provider.rs:384/389` and, after GAP-177 (ii), both `jit_adopt.rs` arms.** So the name is the only thing still under-specified; **the semantics are doubly committed in code, and a name that disagrees with two tested sites is the drift this registry exists to catch.** **⚠️ AND THE BLOCKER IS CONFIRMED REAL RATHER THAN SUSPECTED — MEASURED 2026-08-13: `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs:27` is `#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(Serialize, Deserialize))]` WITH NO `rename_all` AND NO PER-VARIANT `rename`. Serde therefore tags the variant BY ITS RUST NAME, so renaming `F8E4M3` -> `F8E4M3FN` SILENTLY CHANGES THE SERIALIZED FORM AND BREAKS EVERY STORED CAPTURE.** A pure-source rename is not pure. **THE FAILURE MODE IS THE BAD ONE: an old capture does not fail loudly at the rename, it fails later at REPLAY, far from the change.** **RULED APPROACH: keep the WIRE spelling stable with `#[serde(rename = "F8E4M3")]` while the Rust name becomes `F8E4M3FN`.** Note these are INDEPENDENT serializations and conflating them is the trap: **serde's `DType` tag and the sk4 structure-key token are different paths** — sk4 already emits `f8e4m3fn` via `token_kind.rs` and is unaffected by the Rust name. **GATE: a test that deserializes a capture written with the OLD spelling and passes, born-red (assert it FAILS without the attribute) — otherwise the attribute is untested decoration.** **FAVOURABLE INTERACTION: GAP-177 step (i)'s exhaustiveness means the rename becomes a COMPILE ERROR on both `jit_adopt` arms and the telemetry arm — it CANNOT silently orphan them.** That is the mechanism-fix paying out on a change nobody had planned when it landed. **⚠️⚠️ RULED 2026-08-13: DO NOT RENAME. The rename would buy the same protection a second time, at 110 sites.** **⚠️ FACT 2 — THIS ROW'S OWN STATED IDEAL WAS WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE COORDINATOR'S. It called for changing the TOKEN to `f8e4m3fn`. THAT MUST NOT HAPPEN: `fkc/lower.rs:390` parses `"F8E4M3"` from EXTERNAL, HAND-AUTHORED `.fkc.md` contracts, and TEN checked-in contract files name it — including `metal/cast.fkc.md`, `vulkan/cast.fkc.md`, and the verified-ledger JSON. NOT per-operation.** The architect prescribed per-op **from outside the code**; the lane tested it with the code in front of them and it fails on three grounds, **and the second is decisive on its own: A PRESCRIPTION THAT IS SYNTACTICALLY IMPOSSIBLE FOR A MEMBER OF THE POPULATION IS NOT A PRESCRIPTION.** **(1) HOMOGENEOUS OBLIGATION** — every unsafe op in a given fn rests on the same obligation (the `neon` target-feature contract, plus pointer validity for load/store), so per-op blocks **repeat one SAFETY note N times** and surface nothing. **(2) NOT EXPRESSIBLE:** `*y = Self::reduce_one(x[0])` (one of the 12, `neon.rs:72`) is an **assignment-LHS dereference** — `unsafe { *y } = …` is not valid Rust, so the statement MUST be one block. **(3) WORSE FOR MULTI-INTRINSIC EXPRESSIONS:** `reduce_one` sums four `vgetq_lane_f32` calls; four nested `unsafe{}` in one expression is noise. **WHY THE ARCHITECT'S RULE WAS WRONG, WHICH IS MORE USEFUL THAN THE RULE: IT USED GRANULARITY AS A PROXY FOR INFORMATION CONTENT, AND GRANULARITY IS A BAD PROXY.** The property actually wanted is **the obligation is stated where it applies**. Fuel shows the same field-growth history: `align_bytes` arrived after the original shape. **⚠️ `Contiguity` IS THE ONE NOT TO SKIP — a pub enum where a NEW VARIANT IS A DOWNSTREAM MATCH BREAK is this hazard in its most direct form.** **WHY RIDE THE `Bool` CUT SPECIFICALLY: GAP-168's ruling changes the return type of six public comparison methods, so that release is SOURCE-BREAKING BY CONSTRUCTION.** Reserving in the same cut is one migration; deferring buys an extra breaking release **plus** the same break at every schema revision after. **⚠️ AND THE ARGUMENT THAT PERSUADES SOMEONE WHO DOES NOT CARE ABOUT DOWNSTREAM MIGRATIONS — it is why this row is not merely hygiene: A RESERVATION DONE FOR SOURCE-STABILITY BUYS A WIRE-CONFORMANCE GUARANTEE FOR FREE.** With Unpopped's `AccMp` reserved, its constructor became the only way in from outside, and it **refuses a non-deviating pair — so KISS §6.7-0013's rule (d) stopped being a CHECK and became UNREPRESENTABLE.** A consumer cannot *hold* a redundant `(acc+mp)`, therefore cannot ask a codec to emit one. **That fell OUT of the reservation rather than being designed for.** **THE LADDER THIS ESTABLISHES, and it relabels one of Fuel's own open increments: PROSE → TYPED VALUE → UNCONSTRUCTIBLE, with the last strictly dominating.** GAP-161's canary makes an inconsistent `StorageStatus` **detectable**; its recorded increment 2 (a macro co-generating the `CudaStorageSlice` variants and the `Present` arms from ONE list) makes it **UNCONSTRUCTIBLE**. **Detectable needs the test to run AND the trigger to be on the side the test watches — exactly the "strong, not airtight" limit already in that function's doc comment. Unconstructible needs nothing to run.** Increment 2 is therefore **the top rung, not hardening** — which changes what it is worth, not just what it is called. **PLAN APPROVED 2026-08-13:** `#[non_exhaustive]` on all three · `StructureKeyToken` gains `::new` (a tuple struct needs an external constructor) · **`FdxOperandDesc` KEEPS `from_layout` as its constructor and deliberately does NOT get a field-by-field `new`** · `Contiguity` needs none (unit variants) and goes **first**, being the hazard in its most direct form. **⚠️ THE `from_layout` REASONING IS THE TRAP MOST PEOPLE WALK INTO WHILE BELIEVING THEY HAVE CLOSED IT, AND IT GENERALISES: A CONSTRUCTOR THAT TAKES THE SAME INPUTS AS THE FIELDS IS NOT A RESERVATION — it merely MOVES the breakage from a struct literal to a function signature. A CONSTRUCTOR THAT *DERIVES* THE FIELDS FROM A SMALLER INPUT SURVIVES FIELD GROWTH.** `from_layout(layout, dtype)` derives every field, so a future field changes nothing at any call site. **`#[non_exhaustive]` PLUS a field-by-field `new` is the version that achieves NOTHING while looking careful** — the architect would have accepted it had it been proposed. **⚠️ CHECK THE CHANGE CREATES, MIRRORING WHAT IT CLOSES: `#[non_exhaustive]` BLOCKS EXTERNAL CONSTRUCTION *AND* EXHAUSTIVE PATTERN-MATCHING — VERIFY DOWNSTREAM CAN STILL *READ* WHAT IT NEEDS.** External code can no longer write `StructureKeyToken(s)` in a pattern (field access `.0` should survive — **confirm, don't assume**;
