The instinct about the hole was right; the explanation was right for that case and wrong for floats.** **CORRECTLY REFUSED AND WORTH KEEPING: routing `X -> Bool` through `cast_*_u8` would TRUNCATE (`5.7 -> 5`) and fabricate a non-canonical Bool. Declining beats shipping a plausible arm.** **BLAST RADIUS TODAY IS ZERO** — nothing in Fuel produces a U8 mask any more (swept: zero numeric-mask uses) — **but "zero today" is a measurement, not a guarantee, and under CireSnave's standing instruction (*declining a dtype we can easily support is acceptable only as a short-term workaround*) the FLOAT half is exactly the kind of decline that instruction forbids leaving permanent.** **⚠️ CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE LANE RATHER THAN TAKEN ON RELAY, WHICH IS THE RIGHT RESPONSE TO A COORDINATOR CORRECTION: `cuda/compare.fkc.md` ships exactly FOUR `ne` kernels (`ne_{f32,f64,f16,bf16}_u8`) and ZERO integer compare kernels (`grep -cE '^kernel: (eq\|ne\|lt\|le\|gt\|ge)_(u8\|i8\|u32\|i16\|i32\|i64)_'` -> 0). The split holds in both directions.** **⚠️⚠️ AND THE LANE WENT PAST THE CORRECTION IT WAS GIVEN, CORRECTLY — I ASKED FOR THE COMMENT TO BE FIXED, WHICH WOULD HAVE LEFT THE DEFECT INTACT.** A corrected comment over one returned value is **still** GAP-161's shape, merely better worded. `src_dtype` is in scope, so **the two reasons are now carried in the VALUE**: float source -> *"not wired — the `!= 0` kernels exist but are unreachable from a storage-level cast; needs a graph-level `x.ne(0)`"*; integer source -> *"no CUDA compare kernel at all"*. **A caller can now distinguish a wiring gap from an absence, which is the whole point and is exactly what a prose fix cannot deliver.** **That also sharpens the remaining work: the float kernels are UNREACHABLE FROM A STORAGE-LEVEL CAST, so the fix is a GRAPH-LEVEL lowering of `Cast(float -> Bool)` to `Ne(x, 0)` — not a storage-layer wiring change.** Truncation routing stays refused. **SHIPPED AND MERGED `f1c40270` — `Cast(float -> Bool)` now lowers to `Ne(x, x*0)` in `optimize()`, beside the identity-Cast rule, which is where the codebase already puts Cast rewrites. Born-red observed (*"float -> Bool must lower to Op::Ne, got Cast(Bool)"*), then green. IEEE behaviour pinned by a value test on the three inputs that discriminate: `-0.0 -> FALSE`, `NaN -> TRUE`, `±inf -> TRUE`.** Integer half untouched, truncation refusal untouched, two-reason decline value ships as-is. **⚠️ THE TRADEOFF, RULED RATHER THAN BURIED: the rewrite is UNCONDITIONAL, so CPU's 4 float->Bool cast kernels are bypassed on the `to_dtype` path (the 7 int->Bool ones are not). The lane flagged it instead of hiding it and did NOT delete the kernels.** **RULED KEEP, on three findings the lane did not have:** **(1) THE ESCAPE ROUTE DOES NOT EXIST — coordinator-verified with a positive control: Fuel has NO comparison-against-scalar op; the `*Scalar` family is only the affine pair `AddScalar`/`MulScalar`. So `Ne(x, x*0)` is the available spelling and the second pass is genuine, not an artifact of how it was written.** **(2) THE COST IS FAR SMALLER THAN IT SOUNDS, AND THIS SIZES IT: after GAP-168(c), comparisons produce `Bool` DIRECTLY (`fuel-graph/src/shape.rs:65`), so THE FRAMEWORK NO LONGER GENERATES `Cast(float -> Bool)` ANYWHERE — it is reachable only via an explicit user `to_dtype(Bool)`. The bypassed kernels sit on a path the framework itself stopped using when comparisons migrated.** The lane had reported the cost as if it were on every framework-built mask and **self-corrected the scope error**. **(3) UNCONDITIONALITY IS WHAT THE RECIPE PRINCIPLE DEMANDS, NOT A CONCESSION TO LAYERING. Same rule as *a guard must not encode a false sentence*, pointed at a comment. **Also correct: `Op::ZeroFill` was checked and rejected as the zeros source because it is DESTRUCTIVE (writes through input 0).** **NOT VERIFIED, AS STATED BY THE LANE: no live-GPU run. | GAP-187 | CPU's single-pass float->Bool cast kernels are unreachable after GAP-183's lowering — the recovery is a RE-FUSE PATTERN, not a capability condition | C | **FILED 2026-08-13 as GAP-183's follow-up, and it exists to make sure the right fix is taken rather than the tempting one.** GAP-183 lowers `Cast(float -> Bool)` to `Ne(x, x*0)` unconditionally — correctly, because **a `decompose` must be TOTAL** — which leaves CPU's 4 registered, correct, contract-declared single-pass cast kernels bypassed on that path. **⚠️ THE WRONG FIX, NAMED SO NOBODY REACHES FOR IT: making the lowering capability-conditional. The lowering is the base map; CPU's `cast_f32_bool` is exactly what the COVER step should recover, via a re-fuse pattern over `Ne(x, MulScalar(x, 0))` on backends that register it.** That shape is **same-shape-elementwise**, which is what `PatternNode` v1 supports — unlike rope's rotate-half, the known non-expressible case. **So this should be expressible today; confirm that before designing around it.** **PRIORITY IS LOW AND THE REASON IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: after GAP-168(c) the framework never generates this cast (comparisons produce `Bool` directly, `shape.rs:65`), so the doubled pass lands ONLY on an explicit user `to_dtype(Bool)`.** Worth doing as the clean expression of the architecture; not worth rushing as a performance fix. **DO NOT DELETE THE CPU KERNELS in the meantime — their contract claim is TRUE, the contract declares the full matrix, and under this pattern they become reachable again.** The Bool lane declined to delete them unilaterally and that was the correct call: deletion is the irreversible half of a reversible decision. **⚠️ EXPRESSIBILITY ANSWERED 2026-08-13: YES — `PatternNode` v1 CAN express `Ne(x, MulScalar(x, 0))`. Four requirements verified at head: `OpTag::Ne` and `OpTag::MulScalar` both exist (of 80 variants) with live `Op -> OpTag` projections at `jit.rs:66`/`:62`; the SHARED `x` is sanctioned — `PatternNode::Bind { index }` documents that *"a repeated `index` is a node-identity guard on a shared input"*; the baked `0.0` is supported and ALREADY has a discrimination test (`baked_scalar_is_a_pattern_constant_not_a_slot`, `jit.rs:612`) proving `OpAttrs { scalars: vec![v] }` REFUSES a different value; and both ops are same-shape-elementwise.** **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT'S FLAGGED CONSTRAINT WAS NOT THE BINDING ONE. Same-shape-elementwise was never at risk; the real hazard was NON-LINEARITY — the pattern uses the same variable TWICE, which many pattern languages cannot express — and the lane identified and cleared it. That was the question that could have killed the row, and it is not the one that was asked.** **⚠️⚠️ AND THE ANSWER WAS NEARLY THE OPPOSITE, OFF A BROKEN EXTRACTION: a first measurement reported `OpTag` had 20 variants containing NEITHER `Ne` NOR `MulScalar` — a clean, confident *"not expressible"* that would have re-scoped this row. THE ENUM DECLARES MULTIPLE VARIANTS PER LINE and the regex captured only the first identifier on each. REAL COUNT: 80.** **WHAT CAUGHT IT WAS NOT THE OUTPUT LOOKING WRONG — IT LOOKED FINE — BUT A HARD CONTRADICTION WITH AN INDEPENDENT ARTIFACT: `jit.rs:33` maps `Op::Mul => OpTag::Mul`, and `Mul` was absent from the extracted list. Two sources that could not both be right.** The construct was invisible in the number again (identifiers-per-line, not variants), **and a "not expressible" null is precisely the direction that gets filed rather than investigated.** **NEXT, AUTHORIZED AND NOT YET BUILT: trace WHERE the re-fuse consumer lives, and treat the answer as possibly changing the DESIGN rather than only the crate. If it is `fuel-graph`'s runtime-fusion path this is a graph-level rewrite; IF IT IS `fuel-dispatch`'s BINDING TABLE then "re-fuse" is really *the binding table matching a subgraph* — a DIFFERENT MECHANISM, and the pattern may not be the right artifact at all. ⚠️ The constitutional constraint holds either way: the decision stays in the optimizer and the backend never decides strategy — if the trace shows the binding table would have to DECIDE, that is a finding and the work stops.** **⚠️⚠️ CLOSED WON'T-DO 2026-08-13 — THE CONSUMER TRACE FOUND THAT THE PRESCRIBED MECHANISM CANNOT DO THE PRESCRIBED JOB, AND THE ARCHITECT'S FRAMING WAS WRONG, NOT MERELY ITS SCOPING.** Consumer located in **`fuel-graph`** (`registry.rs` `FusedOpMeta.pattern` -> `opt.rs` `FusionRule::from_entry` -> `jit::match_region_extract`), so the layering worry did not materialise and the constitutional constraint is safe. **BUT `FusionRule::rewrite` PUSHES EXACTLY ONE THING — `Op::Fused(self.id, params)` — SO A PATTERN CANNOT REWRITE BACK TO A PRIMITIVE, and CPU's cast kernel is bound to the PRIMITIVE `Op::Cast`.** The row's *"re-fuse pattern that recovers `cast_f32_bool`"* **is not expressible as stated.** **THE LANE'S FORMULATION IS THE KEEPER: "THE PATTERN WAS THE RIGHT ARTIFACT FOR THE WRONG TARGET." Expressibility (confirmed the report before) was NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT — the pattern matches fine; there is nowhere for it to land. Having just delivered a YES on expressibility, treating it as the green light would have been the natural move; CHECKING THE LANDING SITE AFTER PROVING THE MATCH IS THE STEP THAT IS NOT OBVIOUS.** **THE ARCHITECTURALLY CLEAN ROUTE EXISTS AND WAS PRICED: make `Cast(float->Bool)` a registry FUSED op — `pattern` = the verified `Ne(Bind0, MulScalar(Bind0, 0.0))` tree, `decompose` = the same subgraph, bound to CPU's EXISTING `cast_*_to_bool` kernels (no new kernels, no deletions). Termination VERIFIED rather than assumed: the fused op's decompose is the `Ne` subgraph, not `Cast`, so the chain cannot oscillate. Totality is preserved — `lower_to_base_map` dissolves it, so CUDA still gets `Ne(x, x*0)` and its shipped `ne_*` kernels.** **AND IT IS STILL NOT WORTH IT — the lane RECOMMENDED AGAINST WORK IT HAD JUST MEASURED, which is the second such report today: a permanent registry entry, params encoding and backward rule, to create "the fused spelling of a cast" FOR A PRIMITIVE ALREADY IN THE BASE MAP'S OWN VOCABULARY, against a benefit landing ONLY on an explicit user `to_dtype(Bool)` — because after GAP-168(c) the framework never generates this cast. PERMANENT MAINTENANCE SURFACE FOR A PATH NOTHING INTERNAL TAKES.** **⚠️ THE GENERAL FINDING OUTLIVES THE ROW AND IS NOW IN THE CONSTITUTION: `docs/architecture/04-optimization.md` bumped to **v0.11** stating the bound it had asserted without saying — THE COVER STEP PRODUCES ONLY `Op::Fused` AND CAN NEVER RECOVER A PRIMITIVE'S KERNELS. Once a lowering dissolves a primitive, that primitive's registered kernels are unreachable on that path unless the operation is re-registered as a fused op. That makes this an internal inconsistency in v1's own definition for this case, not a feature request.** **FUEL IS THE MOTIVATING INSTANCE: `fuel-cuda-backend/src/baracuda/quant_w4a16.rs` ships THREE 4-bit weight-only GEMM families behind separate cargo features — Marlin (symmetric int4, GPTQ-derived), AWQ (asymmetric int4), NF4 (bitsandbytes). Someone reached the same gap FROM THE ENCODING DIRECTION before the op-family question existed — two independent routes to one finding, worth more than either alone.** **FUEL-SIDE OBLIGATION, WHICH DOES NOT DEPEND ON KISS ACTING: when a SECOND `AffineBlock` family is wired, kernel selection MUST NOT key on `structure_key` alone. If it was, #189 is a v2 pointer against a known cost; if it was not, the case list was incomplete. The decision record is unrecoverable and guessing would be the exact failure both projects have spent two days cataloguing.** **⚠️ WITNESS LANDED AND MERGED `3d2f907a`: `gap190_affine_block_cannot_distinguish_codebook_from_linear` (`fuel-ir/src/stype.rs`) asserts THE PREMISE — Marlin (linear `q*scale`) and NF4 (codebook) produce the SAME `Encoding::AffineBlock` and collide under `Hash` too, which is what a plan cache actually keys on. AWQ is asymmetric and therefore separable, which is why this row names Marlin/NF4 and not AWQ — stated in the test.** **EXPIRES AT COMPILE TIME, not only by assertion: the descriptor is built FIELD-BY-FIELD, so the day the variant gains a discriminator it STOPS COMPILING and the author must decide what each family sets; if the arms are instead made to differ, the assert fails. Non-vacuity asserted in-test.** Premise control DISCRIMINATES: `AffineBlock` has zero production constructors while sibling `GgmlBlock` IS found in production, so the query demonstrably finds wired variants when they exist. **⚠️⚠️ THE LANE STATED THE WITNESS'S BOUND RATHER THAN LETTING THE GREEN STAND FOR MORE: `fuel-ir` CANNOT SEE ITS OWN CONSUMERS, so this fires on the DESCRIPTOR'S SHAPE and NOT on the event the obligation is actually about — a second quant family getting WIRED in `fuel-cuda-backend`. Marlin wired as `AffineBlock { packed: F4, block_shape: [64], zero_point: None }` — THE LIKELY CASE — SLIPS PAST ENTIRELY.** The invariant writable at that layer is *"the type cannot distinguish them"*, **not** *"only one family exists"*. | GAP-193 | `main` was RED under `--features jit` from the Bool cut until found — and the SAME merge broke the reverse mapping SILENTLY | C | **FOUND 2026-08-13 BY ACCIDENT, while testing something unrelated. `cargo check -p fuel-dispatch --features jit` exited 101 with `error[E0004]: non-exhaustive patterns: DType::Bool not covered`, target crate reached. FIXED `93f194b3`.** **FOURTH main-red from the GAP-168(c) merge, same cause every time: an enum-variant addition needs a gate pointed at EVERY feature that parses a consumer, and `--lib` + `telemetry` + CUDA did not include `jit`.** **⚠️⚠️ THE INSTRUCTIVE PART IS THE ASYMMETRY — THE BOOL CUT BROKE BOTH DIRECTIONS OF THE JIT SEAM MAPPING AND ONLY ONE COMPLAINED. `dtype_to_element_kind` keys on `DType`, so adding a Fuel dtype is a HARD `E0004`. `element_kind_to_dtype` keys on `ElementKind`, so a new FUEL dtype applies NO EXHAUSTIVENESS PRESSURE TO IT AT ALL — it kept declining `Bool` under the comment *"no Fuel `DType` for these seam kinds"*, which the Bool cut had made FALSE. ONE DIRECTION BROKE LOUDLY; THE OTHER JUST BECAME QUIETLY WRONG.** That asymmetry is now written at the site. **FIXED BY MAPPING, NOT DECLINING, in both directions — `DType::Bool <-> ElementKind::Bool`: baracuda's `Bool` is 1-byte storage with 0/non-zero truthiness and Fuel's is canonically 0/1 in one byte, THE SAME REPRESENTATION, so a decline would have asserted something false.** Matches what already shipped on the telemetry path the same day. **GAP-177 (i)'s exhaustiveness is what turned a silent mis-mapping into a build failure — it worked exactly as designed; the gate was simply never pointed at it.** `bool_maps_to_the_seam_bool_and_did_not_stay_declined` pins both directions **so a future decline has to argue with a test rather than only with a comment.** | **CLOSED `93f194b3`** — both directions mapped and pinned; **the lesson is the asymmetry: a match keyed on the FOREIGN enum gets no pressure from OUR additions** |
| GAP-194 | The flash-decode OFFER SITE hardcodes `window_size_left/right: None` — a guard asserting a claim that is false for any windowed family (⚠️ REPLACES a filed premise that was WRONG) | C | **⚠️⚠️ ROW REWRITTEN 2026-08-14 — THE ORIGINAL PREMISE WAS FALSE, AND IT WAS FILED BY THE LANE THAT HAS NOW DISPROVED IT AND CARRIED BY THE ARCHITECT WITHOUT CHECKING. It read: *the CUDA flash-decode arm cannot express a sliding window; offering it on a windowed layer would silently drop the window.* Both halves are wrong.** Verified at head by the architect rather than accepted — *a correction is the claim LEAST likely to be re-checked, because it arrives wearing the credibility of having caught an error*: **(1) `DecodeFlashSpec` ALREADY CARRIES `window_size_left` / `window_size_right`** (`fuel-dispatch/src/decode_flash.rs:129-131`), documented *"disqualifies (no kernel support)"* — versus `causal`, documented *"accepted-and-ignored… so it does not disqualify"*. **(2) `flash_decode_admissible` (`:189`) RETURNS FALSE when either bound is set**, under the comment *"Config the capacity-K kernel does not implement"* — so **no arm node is ever created and the decomposed path runs. (3) THE DECLINE IS ALREADY TESTED** (`window_softcap_alibi_get_no_flash_arm`, `:498`), and `baracuda_dispatch.rs:2147` hard-errors defensively if a window ever reaches the kernel. **(4) The PREFILL flash path DOES support windows** (`fuel-cuda-backend/src/flash_attn.rs::translate_window`) — so this was never family-wide. **WHAT ACTUALLY CANNOT EXPRESS A WINDOW IS THE `flash_decoding` CAPACITY-K KERNEL — not the arm, not the spec, not the graph — and the machinery around it already declines safely and provably.** **THE REAL DEFECT IS ONE OF OUR OWN NAMED RULES, AT THE OFFER SITE: `offer_flash_decode_arm_for_region` (`fuel-core/src/lazy.rs:~10232`) hardcodes `window_size_left: None, window_size_right: None`. For `LlamaModel` that is TRUE. For a windowed family it is A GUARD ENCODING A CLAIM THAT IS FALSE** — and the silent window-drop the original row feared happens **only if the offer site lies**, never because the arm is inexpressive. **⚠️ AND THE COST OF THE BLUNT WORKAROUND IS LARGER THAN THE RISK IT AVOIDED: skipping the offer entirely loses the flash arm on EVERY layer of Qwen2 / Qwen3 / Qwen3Moe / Gemma3 — INCLUDING THE LAYERS THAT ARE DENSE AND NEVER NEEDED A WINDOW.** Gemma3 at `sliding_window_pattern: 6` is 1-in-6 global (dense); any mixed config's layers at or past `max_window_layers` are dense; and a `use_sliding_window: false` config is dense at every layer. **FACT: those layers are dense and the gate would admit them (subject to bf16/CUDA/head_dim). INFERENCE, EXPLICITLY NOT MEASURED: the speedup — the lane refused to quote a CUDA number it did not have, and that refusal is why the eventual baracuda ask will be credible.** **AGREED WORK (Fuel-side, contained, NO baracuda ask): thread the per-layer window into the spec instead of asserting `None`, carrying it on `DecodeLayerInputs`. Then BY CONSTRUCTION — windowed layer -> window set -> the tested gate declines -> decomposed path -> correct; dense layer -> `None` IS TRUE -> arm offered -> correct and fast. The guard stops lying and starts describing.** Seam addition and its only consumer land in ONE commit — *a seam whose consumer arrives in the same change* is the sequencing that avoids the inert-axis problem this same lane caught on LFM2. **⚠️ VERIFICATION SHAPE, AND THE ARCHITECT OVERRODE THE LANE'S FIRST PROPOSAL: do NOT widen `FlashArmCapability::all_available()` out of `#[cfg(test)]` to enable a `fuel-core`-side test. ADMISSIBILITY IS ALREADY CORRECT AND ALREADY TESTED; THE DEFECT IS THAT THE OFFER SITE ASSERTS A FALSE WINDOW — so assert on THAT: the constructed `DecodeFlashSpec` carries `Some(w)` for a windowed layer and `None` for a dense one, where `None` is then TRUE RATHER THAN ASSUMED. Needs no capability helper, no dtype, no CUDA, and tests the actual claim one level closer than the alternative — which would have exported a test affordance into the public API permanently to check a property adjacent to the defect.** **HONEST RESIDUAL, REQUIRED IN-FILE: that dense layers actually RECEIVE the arm cannot be seen from f32/CPU — the gate declines on capability and dtype BEFORE reaching the window check. A green CPU suite must not be citable as evidence for the admit half; that needs bf16 + CUDA via the detached-forge protocol. If the CUDA leg costs more than the change is worth, LAND THE CORRECTNESS IMPROVEMENT AND REPORT THE ADMIT HALF AS UNVERIFIED — the guard ceasing to lie is worth having on its own.** **CROSS-PROJECT, HELD DELIBERATELY: making `flash_decoding` implement local attention would let WINDOWED layers take the arm too, and the capability exists in the family already (the prefill kernel does it). NOT RAISED YET — an ask carrying a measured dense-layer win plus a count of still-excluded windowed layers is a different object from *"we think it'd help"*.** **⚠️ THE FILING ERROR'S SHAPE, WORTH KEEPING: inexpressiveness was INFERRED FROM A HARDCODED `None` AT THE ONE CALL SITE BEING READ, WITHOUT CHECKING THE TYPE IT WAS FILLING IN.** Same family as reading a file's feature gate and concluding a guarantee's coverage. **And note the direction: this row OVERSTATED a correctness risk, which is not the safe error — a wrong OPEN row costs someone a day and looks responsible while doing it.** **⚠️⚠️ CORRECTNESS HALF LANDED `e785188e` — AND CHECKING GEMMA3 FOUND A **SECOND** FALSE FIELD AT THE SAME SITE, WHICH THE LANE WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE INTRODUCED ITSELF. THE OFFER SITE HARDCODED **THREE** FIELDS, NOT TWO: `softcap: None` IS TRUE FOR LLAMA AND **FALSE FOR GEMMA3**, which applies `attn_logit_softcapping`.** **⚠️ AND THE SOFTCAP LIE IS STRICTLY WORSE THAN THE WINDOW ONE: a dropped window loses an optimisation and mis-attends; an un-applied softcap produces **NUMERICALLY WRONG OUTPUT** — uncapped attention — on the family the lane had JUST FINISHED PORTING. Wiring Gemma3's offer without re-reading the struct would have introduced it in the same commit family as the fix for its twin.** **THE GENERALISABLE RULE, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS ENTRY EXISTS: WHEN ONE FIELD OF A STRUCT LITERAL IS FOUND ASSERTING A FALSE CLAIM, THE OTHER FIELDS OF THE SAME LITERAL ARE THE HIGHEST-PRIOR PLACE TO LOOK NEXT — they were written by the same person, in the same sitting, under the same assumption about which model was being served. Three hardcoded fields, TWO lies, and only one was the one anybody set out to find.** **WHAT LANDED, and one part is stronger than the fix required: `MaskPlan::window_for_layer` IS THE SINGLE SOURCE SHARED WITH THE MASK BYTES** — the arm's window and the mask `Const` come from the same plan entry, so they **cannot** drift. A family deriving the window separately for the arm would be free to disagree with the mask it actually applies, and *"arm says dense, mask says windowed"* IS the silent defect. **Made unrepresentable rather than merely tested against — structural over disciplinary.** `DecodeLayerInputs.attn_window` carries it; `flash_window_bounds` translates Fuel's `j` in `[i-w+1, i]` to FA-v2's `(left = w-1, right = 0)`, matching `translate_window`. **Llama is unchanged — still `None`, but DERIVED FROM ITS PLAN rather than asserted.** **TESTS ASSERT THE CLAIM, NOT THE KERNEL — correct, because the defect was a false assertion: the SAME region offered windowed gets no arm and offered dense gets one (BOTH halves, since a decline that also declined the dense case would prove nothing), plus the translation, plus that a degenerate width 0 does NOT collapse to `(None, None)` — the one answer that silently re-introduces the defect.** `all_available()` was NOT widened. **The admit half is documented in-file as not citable from a CPU suite.** **THE OFFER IS DELIBERATELY NOT YET WIRED INTO THE FIVE FAMILIES, and the reason is a VERIFIED prerequisite rather than an assumed one: those layers call `merge_heads()` (permute+reshape), so `attn_v`'s sole consumer is the PERMUTE, not the merged tensor — the offer needs that chain split, exactly as `LlamaModel` does manually. Mechanical, and CPU-INERT. NOT MEASURED: any speedup.** **⚠️ ARCHITECT NOTE, CORRECTED BY THE LANE — AND MY FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS NOT MERELY WRONG, IT WAS ACTIONABLE-AND-WRONG, WHICH IS WORSE. THE LANE USED `git show origin/main:docs/gaps.md`. The rewrite `801d4fa2` landed 10:03 and their commit was 10:28 — it was ALREADY AN ANCESTOR OF THEIR BASE. A READ IS ONLY AS FRESH AS THE MOMENT YOU TAKE IT** — and a claim repeated from memory carries the authority of the original measurement while having none of its currency. **PATTERN IN THE ARCHITECT'S OWN ERRORS, THIRD INSTANCE THE SAME DAY: I REACH FOR THE MECHANISM I HAVE MOST RECENTLY BEEN BURNED BY RATHER THAN THE ONE IN FRONT OF ME** — stale-tree here, because a stale tree had cost three incidents the previous night. **A CORRECTION DRAWN FROM RECENT PAIN IS THE ONE LEAST LIKELY TO BE MEASURED BEFORE IT IS WRITTEN DOWN.** **⚠️⚠️ BARACUDA ANSWERED THE PROPOSE-FIRST INQUIRY 2026-08-14, MEASURED AGAINST THE KERNEL SIGNATURE, AND IT CHANGES THIS ROW A THIRD TIME — THE ARM *CAN* SERVE WINDOWED LAYERS TODAY, WITH NO KERNEL CHANGE.** **THE ABSENCE OF WINDOWING IS DELIBERATE AND CONTRACT-DOCUMENTED**, not a gap: `flash_decoding.rs:40` and `baracuda_flash_decoding.cuh:62` both say *"sliding window / ALiBi / soft-cap — caller pre-masks the cache"*, and **the kernel ignores `is_causal` entirely** (`.cuh:57`) — **decode does NO masking AT ALL, by design**, because decode minimises the per-step hot path while prefill amortises the plumbing over many queries. **So the prefill/decode asymmetry Fuel filed as a capability gap is one axis of a consistent decision.** In-kernel windowing would be a **VARIANT, not structural** (the K-loop is a contiguous `[k_start, k_end)`), i.e. **someday-not-never, currently unplanned**, reopened by a real-model measurement. **⚠️ AND THE ARCHITECT'S TECHNICAL CHALLENGE WAS THE VALUABLE PART OF THE EXCHANGE — WITHOUT IT A LANE WOULD HAVE BUILT THE WRONG THING. Baracuda's first reply suggested windowed layers could take the arm today by *pre-masking the cache*. The obvious reading — zero the out-of-window keys — IS NUMERICALLY WRONG AND BARACUDA CONFIRMED IT: `q · 0 = 0`, `exp(0) = 1`, so a zeroed key gets a FINITE softmax weight and still inflates the denominator, rescaling every surviving weight. PLAUSIBLE-BUT-WRONG OUTPUT — the exact failure class this row exists to prevent. There is NO mask or bias input to the kernel (confirmed from the signature; the `-INFINITY` uses inside the `.cuh` are internal chunk-tail padding, not caller-facing), so there is no channel through which -inf could be supplied at all.** **THE REAL CONTRACT IS PHYSICAL SLICING, NOT VALUE-MASKING: `flash_decoding.rs:34` — *"caller is responsible for slicing the cache to the prefix it wants"*. K/V are the PHYSICAL layout and the kernel attends exactly those `k_len` keys. So *"pre-mask"* means *"PRESENT EXACTLY THE KEYS TO ATTEND"*.** **AND FOR SLIDING-WINDOW DECODE THAT IS ACHIEVABLE BY CONSTRUCTION, BECAUSE A KEY THAT LEAVES THE WINDOW NEVER RETURNS: maintain the per-layer cache so the LIVE RANGE *IS* THE WINDOW — no masking, no kernel change, arm usable.** **This is a FUEL-SIDE CACHE-MANAGEMENT problem, not a kernel one.** **TWO CONDITIONS PIN THE SCOPE, AND THEY MAKE IT BOUNDED ENGINEERING RATHER THAN FREE: (i) CONTIGUITY — the kernel takes a single contiguous `[B,H_kv,K_len,D]` run; a window is a SUFFIX, which a base-pointer offset plus `k_len=W` supports, BUT A RING-BUFFER CACHE WHOSE WINDOW WRAPS IS TWO SEGMENTS AND CANNOT BE PRESENTED AS ONE SLICE. The job is specifically *keep the window contiguous* — compacting cache, window-sized-no-wrap, or present-unwrapped. (ii) `k_len` IS ONE VALUE PER LAUNCH — uniform window-per-layer works directly; PER-SEQUENCE-RAGGED windows within a batch are the `batch_paged_decode` family's shape, a DIFFERENT ARM.** **NOT ALLOCATED: this interacts with the block pool, prefix sharing and paged decode, and there is still NO measured benefit — the dense-layer win was correctly refused as unmeasurable on synthetic models. Scoped and costed, awaiting a real-model benchmark or CireSnave's call, NOT started.** | **CORRECTNESS HALF CLOSED `e785188e`; WIRING `c270a957`** (CPU-inert, structural witness); **admit-half CUDA confirmation UNVERIFIED — two instrumentation failures, see GAP-200 family**; ⚠️ **baracuda answered: windowed layers ARE servable today via CONTIGUOUS CACHE SLICING (Fuel-side), scoped with two conditions, NOT allocated and needing a real-model number first** |

A 2^-26 difference implies THE TWO PATHS ARE NOT THE SAME SEQUENCE on aarch64.** **NEXT STEP IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A RULING, AND IT NEEDS NO aarch64 HARDWARE: read whether the prefix-reuse path and the fresh path call the SAME function with the same inputs.** **SAME fn -> a 2^-26 diff should be impossible on any architecture, so something arch-specific is selecting a different kernel underneath, and that is a real defect. | GAP-204 | Fuel COPIES every safetensors tensor element-by-element on load — mmap may be buying page-sharing it then throws away | C | **RAISED 2026-08-14 while checking whether an alignment hazard MLMF found in their own design was live in ours. It is NOT — and the reason is this copy.** `fuel-core/src/lazy.rs:~6750`: `Vec::with_capacity(elem_count)` then a per-element `data.push(f32::from_le_bytes([...]))` over `bytes.chunks_exact(4)`, per dtype. **Byte-wise, alignment-independent, endian-explicit — correct and NOT undefined behaviour**, which is why MLMF's hazard (safetensors guarantees NO alignment; an F32 tensor after an odd-length U8 tensor is misaligned) cannot bite us. **MEASURED ASYMMETRY IN OUR OWN CODE: the GGUF/GGML path handles alignment (`DEFAULT_ALIGNMENT = 32`, reads `general.alignment`, `div_ceil(alignment) * alignment`, `align32()`); the safetensors path has ZERO alignment references. That is correct — GGUF pads and safetensors does not — but it means the two paths reach safety by different means, and only one of them is free.** **⚠️ THE QUESTION, AND IT IS NOT YET ANSWERED — DO NOT TREAT THIS ROW AS A FINDING: mmap's value for Fuel is documented as OS-level PAGE SHARING plus lazy residency, which is what makes MULTI-SESSION SERVING memory-viable. If every tensor is decoded into an owned `Vec` at load, two sessions loading the same model DO NOT share those pages — each gets its own copy — and the mmap degrades to a fancy `read`. WHETHER THAT IS ACTUALLY WHAT HAPPENS IS UNMEASURED.** **WHAT MUST BE MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING IS CONCLUDED: (i) is the byte-wise decode THE lazy load path or A path — the module doc cites `lazy.rs:9802-9810`, which at head is `RebindOutcome`, so THE DOC'S OWN POINTER IS STALE and the real site is ~6750; (ii) does anything retain the mmap so pages stay shared, or is it dropped after decode; (iii) what the copy costs in wall-clock and RSS for a realistic model; (iv) whether two sessions on one model actually duplicate resident bytes.** **IF IT IS REAL, THE FIX IS NOT TO REMOVE THE BYTE-WISE PATH — it is what makes us alignment-safe. It is to make it CONDITIONAL: borrow when the slice is aligned for the target type, fall back to the decode when it is not. MLMF's rev-2 §4.6 specifies exactly that primitive (`try_as_slice::<T>()`, `Err` on misalignment, NO infallible typed accessor, realigning copy as a separately-named call), so the seam is being designed upstream and Fuel would consume it rather than invent one.** **⚠️ ROW CORRECTED SAME TURN — MLMF CAUGHT THAT THE PROPOSED FIX WAS INCOMPLETE, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART. The byte-wise decode does TWO jobs and only one of them is alignment: `from_le_bytes` is also ENDIAN-EXPLICIT, whereas `try_as_slice::<f32>()` is a NATIVE-endian reinterpret.** Identical on every target either project runs today, and **not the same operation** — so *"borrow when aligned"* would silently inherit a native-endianness assumption Fuel does not currently have. **The honest form of the upgrade is: BORROW WHEN THE SLICE IS ALIGNED *AND* THE FILE'S BYTE ORDER MATCHES THE HOST.** MLMF is carrying the endianness distinction explicitly in the `mlmf-gguf` plan rather than folding it into alignment. **AND CHECKING THEIR CAVEAT MEASURED A SEPARATE, UNDECLARED FUEL LIMITATION: `fuel-formats/src/gguf.rs` IS HARDCODED LITTLE-ENDIAN — 43 endianness references, EVERY ONE `LittleEndian`, plus 37 LE reads, and ZERO big-endian handling. GGUF v3 permits big-endian files.** Safetensors is little-endian by specification, so `from_le_bytes` on THAT path is correct and not at issue; **GGUF is where the assumption is undeclared.** **⚠️ NOT YET CONFIRMED, AND IT DECIDES WHETHER THIS IS A LIMITATION OR A DEFECT: the magic is read via `read_u32::<LittleEndian>()` and matched, so a big-endian file's magic SHOULD fail to match and be REJECTED rather than silently misread — reject-not-misread is the safe failure and would make this a limitation to DECLARE. If instead any BE file parses and yields byte-swapped values, it is a correctness defect. NOBODY HAS RUN A BIG-ENDIAN GGUF THROUGH IT; the inference from reading the magic path is not a measurement.** **⚠️ MEASURED 2026-08-15 — THE ROW IS REAL, AND THE ARCHITECT'S POINTER WAS STALE TWICE OVER.** The module doc cites `lazy.rs:9802-9810`, which at head is `RebindOutcome`; **the `~6750` the architect then found by search is `from_safetensors_bytes`, which is TEST-ONLY** (called only from in-module tests). **THE REAL PATH IS `load_tensor_as_f32` (`lazy.rs:10309`)**, reached by every safetensors loader via `*Weights::load_from_mmapped`. **So the conclusion held but was attributed to the wrong site — a real function measured correctly and cited for a role it does not have.** **Q2 — THE MMAP IS DROPPED. `st` is a LOCAL in every loader and NO model struct retains a `MmapedSafetensors`.** All weights are eagerly decoded into owned `Arc<[f32]>` up front, which is **anonymous heap, never file-backed — so OS page-sharing of the decoded weights is IMPOSSIBLE regardless of alignment.** That settles the row's original question: **mmap's benefit does not survive the load.** **⚠️⚠️ AND THE FINDING BEYOND THE FOUR QUESTIONS, WHICH DOMINATES THE SERVING-MEMORY STORY: `load_tensor_as_f32` UPCASTS BF16/F16/F64 TO F32** — verified at head (`F64 => … as f32`, `BF16 => half::bf16::from_bits(raw).to_f32()`, `F16 => …to_f32()`). **Fuel holds ALL weights as f32 in RAM even when the file is BF16 — the common LLM format — so a model costs 2x its disk footprint, permanently, with no lazy residency.** Measured: BF16->f32 at ~352 MiB/s with **2x RAM inflation** (256 MiB disk -> 512 MiB RAM). Extrapolated (stated as extrapolation — no multi-GB weights locally): a 7B BF16 model, 14 GB on disk, becomes **~28 GB of f32 RAM** after ~40 s of decode. F32 decode measures ~1.19 GiB/s single-threaded. **⚠️ SO THE FIX THIS ROW ORIGINALLY PROPOSED IS MUCH NARROWER THAN IT LOOKED: MLMF's conditional-borrow (`try_as_slice::<T>`) helps ONLY aligned host-endian **F32** tensors, AND ONLY IF THE MODEL RETAINS THE MMAP — which it does not. It does nothing for the BF16 2x inflation or for eager full residency.** The serving-memory story is dominated by **(a) f32-in-RAM for BF16 weights and (b) eager residency**, not by the F32 copy or by alignment. **NET FOR THE MULTI-SESSION SERVING GOAL: it IS memory-viable — but via APPLICATION-LEVEL `Arc`-sharing of ONE decoded model across N sessions, NOT via mmap. The mmap mechanism is unavailable by construction.** A definitive N-session RSS number is not runnable until the serving path is wired; what is certain is the mechanism. | **MEASURED — row CONFIRMED but re-scoped.** mmap benefit does not survive the load (mmap dropped, weights eagerly decoded to owned `Arc<[f32]>`); **the dominant cost is the BF16->f32 upcast (2x disk footprint in RAM) plus eager residency, NOT the copy**; conditional-borrow addresses neither. **Highest-leverage lever is the dtype/residency question** — needs a ruling, not a fix |
| GAP-205 | `fuel-formats/src/gguf.rs` PANICS on a malformed `.gguf` (`general.alignment = 0`), plus three unbounded allocations from declared lengths | A | **FOUND 2026-08-14 by MLMF's port reconnaissance, VERIFIED AT HEAD BY THE ARCHITECT rather than accepted.** **⚠️ THE PANIC IS THE URGENT ONE AND IT IS A NEVER-PANIC VIOLATION ON AN UNTRUSTED-INPUT PATH.** `gguf.rs:445-451` accepts `general.alignment` as U8/U16/U32 or non-negative I8/I16/I32 **with no zero check**, then `:454` does `position.div_ceil(alignment) * alignment`. **`div_ceil(0)` divides by zero — process abort, reproduced by MLMF at exit 101, reachable from ANY untrusted `.gguf`.** Fuel loads user-supplied model files; CLAUDE.md's rule is *never panic on production paths*, and a parser is the most exposed production path there is. **AND NON-POWERS-OF-TWO ARE ACCEPTED, WHICH IS A CORRECTNESS BUG RATHER THAN A CRASH: ggml pads with a GGML_PAD BITMASK, so position 11 with alignment 3 gives 12 via `div_ceil` and 13 via the bitmask. Accepting a non-power-of-two means computing a DIFFERENT TENSOR DATA OFFSET THAN EVERY OTHER READER** — silently wrong bytes, not a refusal. **THREE UNBOUNDED ALLOCATIONS FROM DECLARED LENGTHS (MLMF's measurement, not yet re-verified here): string length `vec![0u8; len]` (`:75`); array element count `Vec::with_capacity(len)` (`:327`); and `n_dimensions` `vec![0; n]` (`:420/:425`) — that last is 34 GB from a 4-BYTE FIELD. Array recursion at `:329` is depth-unlimited.** **⚠️ AND A CORRECTION MLMF MADE TO THEMSELVES THAT MUST SURVIVE INTO THE FIX: `tensor_count`/`metadata_kv_count` are NOT the hazard — both loops use `HashMap::new()` rather than `with_capacity` and terminate at EOF. A FIX THAT CAPS COUNTS AND NOT LENGTHS FIXES NOTHING.** Rust allocation failure calls `handle_alloc_error`, which **aborts and cannot be caught**. **ALSO IN THIS FILE, LOWER URGENCY: (a) `read_string` (`:78-82`) strips a RUN of trailing NULs then applies `String::from_utf8_lossy` — MLMF measured 5 declared bytes becoming 9 with two U+FFFD substitutions. It applies to metadata keys, string values, token strings, merges AND TENSOR NAMES, so a tensor name that round-trips no longer matches the file. (b) `HashMap` at `:380-381` destroys declared order and silently LAST-WINS on a duplicate key or tensor name. (c) The write path is not a faithful inverse: `write_string`/array always emit u64 widths so nothing written reads back as GgufV1; an empty array's element type is fabricated as U32 (`:354`); mixed arrays are refused outright (`:357-361`) — which is exactly the one non-UTF-8 token in a 128k vocabulary.** **⚠️ `gguf.rs` HAS ZERO TESTS — 462 lines, no `#[test]`, no `#[cfg(test)]`; the crate's only GGUF coverage is one 21-line happy-path zero-tensor zero-metadata v3 header.** So every item above is unguarded, and any fix needs its own born-red. **⚠️ TWO REPORT-ONLY ITEMS RULED 2026-08-15: DOCUMENT, DO NOT FIX — and the reason is SEQUENCING, not triviality. Both are real; both are API-SEMANTICS changes to a parser on a RETIREMENT PATH (`mlmf-gguf` replaces this file; `fuel-formats` becomes a re-export shim retired on Fuel's schedule).** **(1) `read_string` (`:78-82`) strips a RUN of trailing NULs then applies `from_utf8_lossy`.** It covers metadata keys, values, tokens, merges **AND TENSOR NAMES** — so a name with a trailing NUL or a non-UTF-8 byte **no longer byte-matches the file, and a lookup by the original name fails.** **(2) `HashMap` (`:380-381`) silently LAST-WINS on a duplicate metadata key or tensor name, discarding declared order.** **MLMF HAS ALREADY SOLVED BOTH IN THE REPLACEMENT, INDEPENDENTLY AND THE SAME WAY WE WOULD HAVE: `MetaValue::Bytes(Vec<u8>)` for (1) — *"a GGUF string is a length-prefixed BYTE ARRAY; `from_utf8_lossy` would substitute U+FFFD and silently change what a model tokenizes to"* — and Vec-in-declared-order plus an index with a duplicate tensor name FATAL for (2).** **AND THE HARM IS UNMEASURED: real ggml files do contain trailing NULs, but nobody has measured whether any real file has a TENSOR NAME that is non-UTF-8 or NUL-terminated, which is the case where the harm is concrete. AN API BREAK NEEDS THAT MEASUREMENT; A DOC NOTE DOES NOT.** **⚠️ REQUIRED SO IT DOES NOT GO INVISIBLE — *"the replacement will fix it"* IS HOW A KNOWN DEFECT OUTLIVES THREE REPLACEMENTS: both are documented AT THE SITE with the behaviour, the concrete harm, and the deliberate deferral named. If the MLMF port stalls, that note is what makes this findable rather than folklore.** **PANIC FIXED `abf478e5`** — one `!alignment.is_power_of_two()` guard catches BOTH failure modes (0 is not a power of two), typed error naming the value. **Born-red witnessed: without the fix `alignment_zero` PANICKED at `:454`, `alignment_non_power_of_two` returned `Ok` with a WRONG OFFSET; two positive controls (align 64, absent->default 32) passed, proving discrimination. THE FIRST TESTS THIS 462-LINE FILE HAS EVER HAD.** **HARDENING LANDED `44193322`** — all bounds are on DECLARED LENGTHS (not counts) and are CHECKS not catches (alloc failure aborts uncatchably): `read_string` -> `take(len).read_to_end`; array -> `Vec::new()` growing to real elements; `n_dimensions` checked against bytes-remaining; array recursion capped at 64 via a PRIVATE depth-tracked reader leaving `Value::read`'s pub signature unchanged. **And `Content::read` now PRESERVES the reader's start position** (seek for stream-len, then restore) — unasked-for, and without it a bounds check needing total length would have silently started reading from offset 0. **BORN-RED NOTE WORTH KEEPING: the `u64::MAX` probe panics on CAPACITY OVERFLOW rather than OOM-aborting, because `u64::MAX` overflows `isize` — a NON-DESTRUCTIVE witness where a merely-large value would have taken the machine down.** | **PANIC FIXED `abf478e5`; HARDENING DONE `44193322`** — first tests ever in this file; **2 report-only semantics items DOCUMENTED-NOT-FIXED by ruling, deferred to `mlmf-gguf`**; write-path fidelity out of scope |

ASSERT AT THE SEAM WHERE THE PROPERTY IS DEFINED, NOT AT THE NARROWEST HELPER THAT HAPPENS TO SERVE MOST CASES — asserting on a helper's coverage drags every legitimate bypass into the helper.** **(iv) SHIPPING: for every checked-in ledger record, assert there EXISTS at least one `op` with `build_primitive_probe(op, record.dtypes, seed) == Some`.** Population from checked-in data (not self-referential); assertion at `build_primitive_probe` (so `Bool` passes honestly via the literal, no invented convention, no laundered exception); born-red on exactly the U32/I64/U8/I32/F8E4M3/I8/I16 records; and **UNCONDITIONAL — a bare `cargo test -p fuel-dispatch`, no feature, no device.** The per-record join (`iter_entries` recovers the op the record lacks, closing the *at-least-one* slack) is approved as a follow-up but is **necessarily `--features vulkan`: the weaker-because-conditional instrument despite being the stronger assertion.** Given how much of Fuel's coverage is feature-invisible, the unconditional floor is worth more. **AND NOTE WHAT ALMOST HAPPENED: version (ii) would have added a fabricated `Bool` encoding TO SATISFY A GATE. Adding data to satisfy an instrument is the failure, not the fix.** | OPEN — Fuel 2, ahead of the CPU seeding path |

Same defect class, new syntax; knowing the rule did not fire. **Durable form: never place a pipe between an operation and an `&&` that depends on its success** — capture to a file, test `$?`, then branch. Same shape as the `--all-targets` lesson (`gate-must-match-change-kind`): the fix is to **derive the check from the operation**, not to remember harder. That binds a direct `-- cargo ...` invocation. It does **NOT** bind `-- cmd /c <bat>` when the bat sets the variable itself: the bat's `set` runs DOWNSTREAM of `:294`, wins silently, and the wrapper logs nothing **because from its side nothing happened** — there was no pre-existing value to disagree with at the moment it looked. **The launch shape that defeats it is the one this repo mandates**: a `.bat`, because the quoting trap forces vcvarsall into a script and the ~10-minute harness kill forces detachment. So a lane can follow BOTH rules correctly and end up uncapped while believing the wrapper handled it. Same class as three other findings the same day: **a control that is real, correct, and silently not in the path**. **Fix (cheap, proposed):** the bat template carries vcvarsall and nothing about forge concurrency — let the wrapper own K. The alternative (re-assert after the child starts) is not possible. **Residual the fix does NOT cover, named so nobody reads it as total:** the cap binds only participants who route through the wrapper at all; Baracuda's uncapped ~16 nvcc is exactly the case it cannot reach, and counted admission cannot observe a non-participant. **CROSS-PROJECT:** `$SlotCount`/`$ForgeThreads` are a protocol shared with Baracuda, so a template change is a cross-project ask, not local tuning. the first-registered alternative"*. **That comment is about choosing among SIBLINGS UNDER ONE EXACT KEY, and I read it as dtype-blindness.** Measured: `lookup_with_caps` builds `let key = (op.into(), SmallVec::from_slice(dtypes), backend)` and does **`self.bindings.get(&key)` — an EXACT hash lookup with dtypes IN the key.** It is not a scan and **it cannot return a kernel of a different operand arity.** Sibling order is program order (`.entry(key).or_default().push(entry)`), so it is **deterministic**. **AND PRODUCTION DISPATCHES THROUGH IT** — `compiled.rs:97`, `pipelined.rs:3069`. ARITY HAZARD AND THE NONDETERMINISM ARE CONFINED TO A `pub`-BUT-TEST-ONLY DIAGNOSTIC API, NOT THE SERVING PATH.** **TWO DEFECTS WERE FOLDED INTO ONE ROW AND THE SEVERE-SOUNDING HALF BORROWED THE OTHER'S LOCATION** — that is the defect in the row, and it is worth more than either finding: **(1)** `first_runtime_fused` silently picks among ambiguous matches and can hand back a wrong-arity kernel — **no production consumer, but it is `pub`, it is the obvious-looking way to resolve an id, and it already caused an eleven-day cross-project misattribution against Baracuda (GAP-001).** A public API that answers ambiguously and silently is worth closing on those grounds alone. **(2)** the route-picker bypass is real and IS on the dispatch path — `lookup_with_caps` picks the first dense-safe sibling instead of consulting §04's layer-3 telemetry. **Deterministic, so a design gap against a ratified commitment rather than a correctness bug.** Split them. ⚠️⚠️ **AND IT IS WORSE **(3)** `jit_ingest.rs:131 struct DiffOutcome { within, max_ulp, detail }` — the differential-summary carrier, **its `max_ulp` field read by nothing.** **`DiffOutcome` IS LITERALLY THE SHAPE AN `agrees_with_<backend>_to_ulp` ROW WOULD SERIALIZE: the type already exists, unnamed and unrecorded.** ⚠️ **AND KISS HAS A RELATED BOUNDARY — filed as KISS #293. ⚠️ CORRECTED SAME DAY: I first recorded, from a relay, that KISS's spec has "NO statement of what the bound is measured against." **THE KISS ARCHITECT RETRACTED THEIR OWN CLAIM AND THE TRUE VERSION IS SHARPER, NOT WEAKER.** The anchor **does** exist — `KISS-OPS-6.8-0001` states it explicitly for transcendental **ATOMS**: *"measured against an audited wide-precision reference — never a byte-exact comparison across languages or targets."* **THE BOUNDARY IS THAT IT DOES NOT PROPAGATE UP A TIER:** `KISS-OPS-6.0-0003`, which governs every op whose decomposition transitively contains a transcendental atom, says only *"evaluate it with the op's declared-ULP comparator"* — **naming the COMPARATOR and not the REFERENT** — and `conform.md`'s comparator declaration has the same shape. **So a live two-implementation differential SATISFIES §6.0-0003 and the comparator declaration while measuring something categorically different from what §6.8-0001 demands one tier down.** **THAT IS THE JUSTIFICATION-SCOPE-MISMATCH CLASS, not an absence: an anchor that is real, correct, and narrower than the clause relying on it** — and *"the anchoring stops here"* is a better finding than *"there is no anchor"* because it names where. **PROCESS NOTE FOR THIS REGISTRY: that wrong sentence was a claim about ANOTHER PROJECT'S artifact, taken from a relay and recorded as measured. Mark such claims as relayed, or verify them in the other project's tree, before a Fuel row asserts them.** Filed upstream with Fuel credited; **note `ulp.rs`'s justification already cites KISS 2026-07-18, so this is "implemented and never specified", not "Fuel found something KISS missed".** **Same defect as a mapping that is not injective onto an identity: two distinguishable things sharing one name, silent all the way down.** `agrees_with_<backend>_to_ulp` is not a new concept to introduce — it is **the name for a distinction already documented and already implemented, that has nowhere to be recorded.** ⚠️ **AND THE SIZING CONSEQUENCE: the ×2 widening belongs to the `agrees_with` claim, NOT to `max_ulp`, so IT DOES NOT HELP WITH THE 424.** It makes the kiss-ref differential path honest and earns a different, weaker, correctly-named claim. **Both are worth having; they are not substitutes, and a plan that lists them together will read as one increment.** **(iii) MatMul / conv / attention: accumulation order changes the answer, so "the mathematically correct result" is NOT WELL DEFINED without pinning an order — the CLAIM needs redefining before it can be earned. The backward wrapper binds `k_len: _` (*"Backward is the static (full-K) training path"*), so **there is no live-prefix parameterisation to reach** and the mirrored assertion would be TRUE AND MEANINGLESS — a control that cannot move, the failure this harness has now caught three times. | GAP-228(f) | `docs/kernel-contracts/cpu/attention.fkc.md` + `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/verify/probe_recipes.rs` | B | **DONE — PagedAttn's 8, and with it UNAUDITED reaches ZERO.** **PRE-DECLARED DELTA, held exactly:** residue **15 -> 7**, sweep **644 -> 652**, ledger **1551 -> 1559**, UNAUDITED **8 -> 0**, backed **615 -> 623**, downgrades **0**. ⚠️ **THE INDEX-OPERAND QUESTION WAS THE DELIVERABLE, AND MEASURING IT MOVED THE HAZARD RATHER THAN CONFIRMING IT.** The worry was *out-of-range indices either trap or silently read the wrong blocks*. Measured at head: **out-of-range ALWAYS traps** — the kernel bounds-checks `physical_block >= num_blocks` and `ctx_len > max_blocks_per_seq * block_size`, both to a typed `Err`. **And the naive fill FAILS SAFE:** `fill_deterministic` through the `DType::U32` arm yields ~2e9, out of range for any probe-sized cache. **The obvious wrong thing is the loud one.** **THE REAL HAZARD IS AN IN-RANGE DEGENERATE TABLE — valid, silent, perfectly bit-stable.** A constant table collapses every logical block onto one physical page; **the IDENTITY is worse, because a kernel that ignored `block_table` entirely and used `logical_block` directly would return BYTE-IDENTICAL output.** That is the all-zeros-integer-probe defect wearing a valid value. The table is therefore hand-constructed **distinct and non-identity** (logical 0,1,2 -> physical 2,0,1; physical 3 never addressed, so a mis-indexed read lands on different data). ⚠️ **AND THE GATE TESTS THE INDIRECTION BEHAVIOURALLY, NOT STRUCTURALLY: it swaps the IDENTITY table in and requires the output to CHANGE.** Asserting the fixture's shape cannot tell a dereferenced table from an ignored one; this can. Born-red on the identity table — **both the structural and the behavioural arm fired.** Third mechanism pattern recorded: this probe is **PARTIALLY seeded** (q/k/v seeded, index operands literal and asserted seed-INVARIANT, because a "different" table would have to be independently proven valid). ⚠️ **A TRIPWIRE FIRED ON COMPLETION, WHICH IS WHAT IT WAS FOR.** `gap_226_split_of_the_entries_that_declare_nothing` carried `assert!(nothing > 0, "...that would be the program finishing, not a quiet pass, and THIS test is then the stale thing")` — a guard against the test going vacuous while the program was in flight, when a zero could only have meant a broken instrument. **It went off. CONVERTING it was the required response, not deleting it:** the non-vacuity job is now done by `assert_eq!(nothing, 0)`, which is strictly stronger (it fails on a broken instrument AND on a regression), and what remains is a **population floor**, since `nothing == 0` is trivially true of an empty table. **The pin is now a RATCHET, not a progress marker.** **SCOPE OF THE ZERO, STATED NARROWLY:** every entry in the CONTRACT-DERIVED table is backed by contract + earned record **without `fill_unset_cpu_precision`**, because `register_into` never applies it. **It does NOT by itself license retiring the fill from `register_cpu_kernels`**, which builds a different (production) table — that comparison is its own measurement and has NOT been made. Separately, **7 registrations still lack a probe recipe** (Nf4Matmul 3, NonZeroIndices 2, Copy 1, QMatMul 1); they are already audited, so they are a different population from the zero and are not covered by it. Related: GAP-207, GAP-226, GAP-228(a)–(e). | **DONE** — CPU UNAUDITED 320 -> 0; 623/623 backed without the fill |

The answer is that the fill is NOT redundant, and its scope is now exactly bounded.** GAP-228 drove the CONTRACT-DERIVED table to zero UNAUDITED, and `register_into` never applies the fill — but the two tables differ BY CONSTRUCTION, so redundancy is a measurement and not an inference from that zero. **Measured 2026-08-26: contract-derived **623** entries, production (`register_cpu_kernels`) **659**, production-only orphans **36**, contract-only **0**, **shared entries that DISAGREE: 0**, and **orphans the fill UPGRADES: 36 — all of them**.** By op: ArgMaxDim 4, ArgMinDim 4, Copy 10, Nf4Matmul 3, NonZeroIndices 2, QMatMul 1, Tril 6, Triu 6. **CONCLUSION: the fill supplies NOTHING for the 623 entries a contract covers (zero disagreements) and fabricates a `PRIMITIVE_DETERMINISTIC_CPU` claim for 36 registrations with NO CONTRACT AT ALL. Retiring it is a CONTRACT-AUTHORING question about eight ops, not a precision question** — and those 36 become UNAUDITED the moment it goes, which is the honest state until the contracts exist. **That is the next gap, and it is small and named.** ⚠️ **THE ATTRIBUTION WAS MEASURED, NOT INFERRED, AND THAT DISTINCTION IS THE POINT.** *"An orphan with a non-UNAUDITED precision can only have been filled"* is a plausible inference and is not evidence — some other path could set it. **Control: comment out the `fill_unset_cpu_precision` call and re-run — `orphans the FILL upgrades` drops **36 -> 0** while `production-only` stays 36 and `DISAGREE` stays 0.** The control edits production code so it cannot live in the suite; the method is recorded in the test's doc comment rather than asserted, because an unreproducible control is a claim. **The test REPORTS the delta and pins none of it** — only non-vacuity floors on both table sizes. Pinning 36 would turn a measurement into a claim the evidence does not license, which is precisely what the fill itself was doing.
